# STAGE_P_PRIME_NOTES.md — Stage P-prime Design Freeze + Findings Log

**Stage:** P-prime — Seam-1 Audit Close-Out.
**Status:** Entry freeze locked 2026-08-21 (PP-D1—PP-D9, plus the four entry-adjudicated rows PP-D20 / PP-D21 / PP-D38 / PP-D39). **PP2 (Workstream 0) executed 2026-08-21** — both keys rotated, PP2.3 skipped on chain, `4b589bc` pruned, backup deleted; residual CLOSED. Branch `stage-p-prime` (cut from `stage-p-bis` @ `9ec513d` = the audited commit) per PP-D2. PP-D10—PP-D19 and PP-D22—PP-D37 are indexed in `STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md` and lock here at their sub-steps; this file gains each entry when it locks. Execution truth until superseded by explicit amend. No code lands until this freeze locks (§12 ambiguity-gate).
**Reproduction pins:** auditician `22aa9851caf68f13c9439bd145ef7594f217df5c`; target aumm-deploy `9ec513d99a68fb454a8a54271b34b884f40f2088` (`stage-p-bis`); snapshot 2026-08-19; run completed 2026-08-21; the clone at `~/audits/aumm-q-governance-2026-08/` (82 confirmed / 11 invalid / 0 plausible; 2 Critical, 17 High, 32 Medium, 14 Low, 17 Info after the independent review — `PROGRESS.md`'s pre-review 78 / 21 High / 13 Info is superseded).
**Canonical spec:** `STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md` (the collapsed defect list, the twenty fix-sequencing constraints, the Decisions index); `report/audit-report.tex` (Recommendations 1—11 in priority order; "Defects that only bite once you fix something else"); constitution §xxix (`10_constitution.md` L203).
**Related:** `STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md` PB-D2 / PB-D11 / PB-D12 (the dissolution class this stage re-adjudicates under PP-D22), PB-D10 (F-15, REOPENED by G39 §5), PB-D61 / RB-017 (the admissibility rule the report quotes against the Stage-K handoff), PB-D63 / PB-D64 (F-22, whose accepted residual G36 correction 4 reclassifies as a latch), PB-D68 (F-23, whose strict-rise fix G10 shows was never backported to the helper), PB-D69 (the admission flag family C.6 turns on), PB-D70 (the redeploy cascade PP-D12 inherits); `CLAUDE.md` §11 (the operator-secret residual PP-D8 closed at PP2, 2026-08-21); `docs/white_hat/AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md` (F-01 / F-05 / F-16 shown incomplete, F-15 reopened).

**Framing.** Stage P-prime exists to consume the seam-1 audit output before Stage Q engages a firm: 82 confirmed files reduce to 51 distinct root causes in eight families plus two composites that own no root, every root cause exits Fixed, Accepted-risk-with-evidence, or False-positive-with-refutation, and closure is proven by a second seam-1 run against the patched commit rather than asserted from the ledger. The stage is inserted after P-bis because the audit ran against P-bis's tip while P-bis is still open (PB3.9, PB5); the name is the user's and is not re-litigated. Four sequenced workstreams (PP-D3): **(0)** operator security, **(A)** adjudication, **(B)** the patch cycle, **(C)** non-code findings, then the re-run gate. `stage-p-prime-complete` replaces `stage-p-bis-complete` as the audit candidate Stage Q receives.

---

## PP-D1 — Stage scope: consume seam 1 only; every root cause exits with a recorded disposition

The input is bounded to what the seam-1 run produced: the 82 files under `audit-state/issues/confirmed/`, the 11 under `issues/invalid/` (read for their refutations, never re-filed), the 18 globals, and the report. The unit of work is the root cause, not the file — the plan's collapse is by fix: two files share a root only when one code change closes both, and files the validators kept apart for disjoint or antagonistic fixes stay apart (the `admissionAuthority` pair, the two `vetoProposal` faces, the three F-16 stages, the two G10 contracts). Exit rule per root cause: **Fixed** (ledger row at `Fixed` with the commit hash and an inverted PoC), **Accepted-risk-with-evidence** (a quantifying artifact in the PB-D9 sandwich-sim shape, never a prose argument alone — the audit reopened F-15 precisely because PB-D10's acceptance was an argument about one direction), or **False-positive-with-refutation** (the PP3.2 PoC fails to reproduce and the refutation is written into the queue and here). Nothing is silently carried past the tag; every disposition is re-checked by the PP6 re-run. Out of scope by this entry: seams 2—5 (PP-D20), the CircuitBreaker track (PB-D63 descoped it; this stage builds only the typed `PoolPause` / `PoolUnpause` pair under PP-D24), any Vault or submodule edit (§8c — the two upstream changes the report suggests are declined in favour of the Aureum-layer alternatives it also names), and any reopening of PB-D63 (xi)'s scoping of F-22 beyond restating its residual as conditional on no authorizer change ever succeeding.

## PP-D2 — Branch `stage-p-prime` cut from `stage-p-bis` @ `9ec513d`, the audited commit

The branch base is the audited commit and not `stage-p-bis`'s moving tip, so that `git diff 9ec513d..stage-p-prime -- src/` is exactly the remediation the PP6 re-run measures, with nothing from PB3.9 or PB5 mixed in. P-bis stays open and rebases nothing; whether `stage-p-prime` rebases onto P-bis's close or the reverse is decided with PP-D21 (the recommendation in the plan's PP7 is P-prime → P-bis → `main`, because PB3.9 runs on generation 4 if PP-D21 (b) holds). Close mechanics mirror PB-D3: two-commit PENDING close (`PENDING_PPRIME` in the `STAGES_OVERVIEW.md` master row + the Completion Log; `CLAUDE.md` §11 hash-free) → the `*-backfill` commit → tag `stage-p-prime-complete` at the backfill commit. §8c's push-to-main ban applies as written; the 2026-08-18 carve-out covered one verified single-file fast-forward and extends to nothing here.

## PP-D3 — Stage shape: four sequenced workstreams 0 → A → B → C → gate

**(0)** Operator security runs FIRST and needs no adjudication: the deployer private key and the Etherscan API key are exposed in an orphaned but reflog-recoverable commit (`CLAUDE.md` §11) and have not been rotated; the item is independent of every finding and waits behind nothing (PP-D8). **(A)** Adjudication before any build (PP-D4), so no rung re-derives judgment. **(B)** The patch cycle in the dependency order the plan's "Fix sequencing" fixes — never family order, because the twenty constraints cross families. **(C)** Non-code findings run alongside B, with one exception: the dissolution decision (PP-D22) gates B's registry-branch rung, so PP5.1 is scheduled before PP4 rung 17 rather than after B closes. **Gate:** the seam-1 re-run (PP-D7) runs once, on the final commit, after the formal bundle re-attests last in B (PP-D37). The one A→B→C ordering P-bis used is not enough here because C's decisions change B's shape; the four-way order and its single cross-edge are stated so a cold session does not open rung 17 early.

## PP-D4 — Adjudication-first, per root cause: the queue row locks before the rung opens

`docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_QUEUE.md` (PP3.1) holds one row per root cause and per composite — 53 rows — with `RC | audit files | post-review severity | reproduction PoC | verdict | fix intent | ordering constraints (PP-D) | redeploy unit | done-criteria | F-NN`. A Workstream-B §8e.1 may open only when its row carries a verdict, a fix intent and done-criteria, and the prompt's `Must match` is drafted from that row. This is the §12 ambiguity-gate applied to external findings: the audit's own record shows what happens when a fix is reasoned about in isolation (G16 retracted a recommendation that would have introduced an unbounded-gas catch-up; the `admissionAuthority` pair's two natural fixes make each other worse), so judgment is materialized once, in the queue, and the rungs transcribe it. Verdicts lock in family batches (PP3.3), each batch one NOTES edit, so a later rung cites a PP-D clause rather than a chat turn.

## PP-D5 — Whitehat ledger numbering: one F-NN row per distinct root cause, F-24 through F-74

The ledger is one row per finding and append-only; the audit's 82 files are not 82 findings. One row per root cause (51 rows, F-24—F-74) keeps the ledger's granularity at the fix, which is what a `Fixed` hash attests to; each row's Explanation names every audit file it closes and carries every validator correction the plan marks **[corrected]**, so the ledger is readable without the clone. The two composites (`composite-dust-lp-position-buys-permanent-vault-capture`, `composite-composition-challenge-is-a-monotone-electorate-destruction-ratchet`) are recorded inside their component rows (B.1 / B.3 / C.4 and C.2 / G.2 / B.1 respectively), not as rows, because a composite has no fix of its own and a row with no `Fixed` hash would sit Open forever. Stage/Step is `P1.<RC>` (the reproduction PoC's step); Surface per the S0—S12 catalog; Status enters at `Confirmed` on the PoC, never at `Open`, because PP3.2 runs before PP3.4. The coverage roll-up gains a "Seam-1 external audit (auditician 22aa9851 @ 9ec513d)" row.

## PP-D6 — Severity input is the report's post-review figure; Workstream A moves a row only on reproduction or refutation

The working severities are 2 / 17 / 32 / 14 / 17 — the independent review's figures, not `PROGRESS.md`'s pre-review 2 / 21 / 28 / 14 / 13. Where a validator note records a raise that the review later returned (the four High→Medium returns) the plan carries both and binds the later one. A root cause's severity is the maximum over its files. Workstream A may move a row in either direction only with an artifact: a PP3.2 reproduction that shows the impact differs from the filing, or a refutation. The two boundaries the report itself flags as arguable are re-decided there and nowhere else: `composition-challenge-victim-resolved-at-execute-time` (Medium, raisable to High by a reviewer who accepts G21's attendance-bar result) and `tvl-floor-is-checked-only-at-admission-…` (Low only on the missing auto-revocation; an Info + Low split is defensible). The six author-validated findings the report names in §secmethod get their PP3.2 reproduction first within their family, because they had refutation attempts but no independent re-derivation.

## PP-D7 — The exit gate is a second seam-1 run; the delta must be zero-or-explicitly-accepted

A fresh auditician clone at the PP-D39 commit, `audit-target/` at the rung-19 commit (the frozen `src/`), `audit-context/` byte-identical to the first run's plus `CORRECTIONS.md` (PP1.3), `RUN-METADATA.md` written with BOTH hashes before `/audit` launches, the same model and effort as the first run, `issues/plausible/` at zero before the delta is read. Acceptance, stated before the run: (i) every queue row at verdict Fixed has no surviving confirmed file, or one whose validator text states the fix holds and re-files a residual — which becomes a NEW row; (ii) every Accepted-risk row is re-confirmed at its accepted severity or lower; (iii) zero NEW Critical or High — any reopens PP4; (iv) NEW Medium / Low / Info are triaged into the queue and fixed or accepted before PP7; (v) the regenerated capability matrix, diffed against G37, shows every row the plan retires as absent or INERT. Both hashes of both runs and the delta table land in this file's "Exit gate" section. What the gate proves is bounded and stated: the same class of tool has the same blind spots (the report's §secmethod names six author-validated findings and a self-validation that missed two duplicates), so PP6 establishes that the harness no longer finds what it found, not that nothing remains; Stage Q's firm is the independent instrument and receives the first report, the queue and the delta.

## PP-D8 — Workstream 0: rotate both keys, purge the orphan, delete the backup, prove the negative

The 2026-08-18 incident left one residual open: `4b589bc` on `main` swept 163 files including every `.env*` variant carrying `DEPLOYER_PRIVATE_KEY` (`.env.example` L43) and `ETHERSCAN_API_KEY` (L32); it was never pushed and is reachable from no remote ref, but it stays reflog-recoverable until garbage collection, and neither key has been rotated (`CLAUDE.md` §11). Scope, all user-run per §8b / §8c: (i) a new deployer EOA, funded from the old one (5.316662104309836821 SepETH at the last reading), written to `.env`, `.env.sepolia`, `.env.mainnet` after `git check-ignore -v` on all three; (ii) a regenerated Etherscan key with the old one revoked; (iii) on Sepolia generation 3, the rotatable seats the old EOA holds — the four emission `setGovernanceContract`s, `setDonateAuthorizer`, `setAdmissionAuthority` — move to the new EOA ONLY IF PP-D21 keeps generation 3 alive past PP6, each preceded by the RB-017 `eth_call` from the intended sender; the immutable seats (`pauseManager` on all 27 pools, the hook's `governanceModule`, `_incendiaryAdmin`, `CCBMultiplier.registrySetter`) stay on the old EOA, accepted on a testnet and recorded in deployment-record section 27 with the reason; (iv) `git reflog expire --expire=now --all && git gc --prune=now --aggressive` on `main`, then `git log --all -p -S` for each secret printing nothing on every branch, then deletion of `~/aumm-env-backup-20260818-145359`, the four commands and their empty outputs recorded here. The exposed key is a testnet key holding SepETH and testnet seats; the reason to rotate anyway is that the same operator posture carries to Stage R, and a residual that `CLAUDE.md` has called open since 2026-08-18 does not get to age into the mainnet runbook.

**PP-D8 amendment (2026-08-21, same day as the lock).** Clause (iii)'s condition — "ONLY IF PP-D21 keeps generation 3 alive past PP6" — evaluates **FALSE**, because PP-D21 (b) retires generation 3 at a generation-4 refresh after the exit gate. **PP2.3 therefore moves zero seats and sends no transaction.** The rotatable seats stay on the old EOA: the four emission `setGovernanceContract`s, `setDonateAuthorizer` and `setAdmissionAuthority`. The four seats that cannot move at all — `pauseManager` on 27 pools, the hook's `governanceModule`, `_incendiaryAdmin` and `CCBMultiplier.registrySetter` — are not a gap in this workstream but root causes C.1 and C.7 of the collapsed defect list, and generation 4 is where they are fixed rather than rotated. Spending real authority-write transactions on a constellation already scheduled for replacement buys nothing and adds risk, since each rotation is a live authority write that the RB-017 rule requires a pre-send `eth_call` to justify; the honest record is the skip and its reason, so deployment-record section 27 states that zero seats moved and why, rather than describing a rotation that did not happen. **This narrows PP2.3 to documentation and narrows nothing else:** PP2.1 and PP2.2 are unaffected, because the exposure is a KEY that leaked, not a seat that is misplaced, and a leaked key stays leaked whichever constellation it points at. One correction rides with this amendment: PP-D8 as written names `ETSCAN_API_KEY`, which is not a variable that exists — the key is `ETHERSCAN_API_KEY` (`.env.example:32`), and the name is corrected in place here and in the plan's PP-D8 row so a later session greps a string that resolves.

## PP-D9 — The G.3 fix carries a mandatory companion: an execute-time `slotOf(p.targetPool) == 0` re-check on the `GaugeChallenge` branch

The refuted hypothesis "a Miliarium pool can be gauge-challenged out" (`PROGRESS.md` "refuted hypotheses") is refuted only by an undocumented cross-contract coupling: `proposeGaugeChallenge` checks `slotOf(targetPool_) == 0` at propose time only (`src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol` L229), and that premise cannot be falsified today because the only reachable `replaceSlot` caller, the composition branch (L432), immediately calls `registerGaugeFromComposition` (L433), which rejects an `Active` pool (`src/gauge/GaugeRegistry.sol` L170). A pool that was `Active` at a gauge challenge's propose time can therefore never become a Miliarium member before that challenge executes. The natural fix to `composition-challenge-bricked-by-permissionless-gauge-activation` — let the composition branch TOLERATE an already-`Active` candidate, so a 100-svZCHF `activateGauge` cannot poison it — makes an `Active` pool seatable, falsifies the premise, and lets a banked simple-majority `GaugeChallenge` terminally revoke a Miliarium pool, bypassing the 2/3 bar the constitution requires for any structural change to the 28. Lock: the tolerate change and the `GaugeChallenge` re-check at L426-427 land in ONE rung (PP4 rung 9, with G.2's incumbent freeze over the same six lines per PP-D30), with a comment at both sites naming the coupling, and a regression that banks a gauge challenge against an `Active` unslotted pool, seats that pool by composition, and asserts the banked challenge reverts at execute. This is the first of the twenty constraints and the template for the rest: a fix is not done until the thing that was relying on the defect is named.

## PP-D20 — Seams 2—5: two run inside P-prime after PP6 against the patched commit; the rest go to Stage Q (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

**(i) Stated gap first.** No audit artifact records a seam roster. `audit-context/AUDIT-INSTRUCTIONS.md` names seam 1 and, in passing, "seam 5's scope" for `script/`; the report's §"Suggested next seams" names the emission mathematics and CCB scoring formulas, the fee-routing hook's swap logic, and the constellation's economic design as areas examined only where authority passed through them. The roster below is therefore this stage's proposal, not a recovered fact: seam 2 = emission mathematics + CCB engine (`src/emission/`, `src/ccb/`), seam 3 = the fee-routing hook and fee controller value paths (`src/fee_router/`, `src/vault/AureumProtocolFeeController.sol`), seam 4 = oracle, rate providers and pool composition (`src/emission/TVLOracle.sol`, `src/rate_provider/`, `src/factory/`), seam 5 = `script/` deploy wiring and the runbooks. **(ii) Placement.** Seams 2 and 3 run inside P-prime, serially, AFTER PP6 and against the patched commit, each with its own re-run gate of the PP-D7 shape — because they audit the code this stage changes most (the electorate redesign, the distributor, the hook unit), and a seam run against the audited commit would find defects already fixed and miss defects the fixes introduce. Seams 4 and 5 go to Stage Q as the firm's first deliverable, with this stage's queue and delta as their input. **(iii) Rejected:** running any seam in parallel with Workstream B on the audited commit — seam 1 produced twenty ordering constraints against a frozen tree; a seam's findings against a moving tree would be adjudicated twice and the second adjudication would be against code that no longer exists. **(iv) Consequence for the plan:** PP6 becomes PP6 (seam 1), PP6-bis (seam 2), PP6-ter (seam 3), each recorded with both hashes; the stage closes after the third; the plan's roadmap gains those rows at PP3.1 when the queue is created.

## PP-D21 — Sepolia generation 4: yes, after PP6 and after PP-D22 is decided (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

Generation 3 cannot be patched in place — the Vault seals `_hooksContracts` at registration, `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` is immutable on the controller, `setEmissionRecorder` / `setAuMTContractForPool` / `setMiliariumRegistry` / `setVotingWeight` / `setGovernanceContract` on `VaultClassRegistry` are one-shot, and `AureumGovernance` holds its six dependencies `immutable` (`src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol` L39-86), so the transitive redeploy unit (PP-D12) is the whole Stage F—L stack plus the 27 pools; only the Vault, the factory, the 87 stubs and `AuMM` survive, and `AuMM` survives only if `GENESIS_BLOCK` is kept — which it is, since no P-prime rung changes the token (the F.3 constant lives in `AureumTime` and moves era boundaries without touching the sealed genesis). **Lock:** a generation-4 refresh runs AFTER PP6 (so it deploys the code the re-run measured) and AFTER PP-D22 is decided (so the dissolution script's read-backs, the `PoolPause` pair and the authorizer probe are wired the way Stage R will wire them), re-running the PB3.13 / PB3.14 procedure with the stubs reused. The reason is the one P10 established: the wiring classes this stage changes — handoff read-backs, typed de-escalation, execute-time probes, recorder self-heal — are exactly what a fork harness exercises against a test-contract governor and a live broadcast exercises against an EOA, and PB-D31, PB-D46 and PB-D48 each record a defect that survived the fork harness and died at the live dry run. PB3.9 (der Bodensee's first distribute) runs on generation 4, which is why the merge order in PP7 is P-prime → P-bis → `main`. **Rejected:** (a) no testnet redeploy — leaves Stage R as the first deployment of every changed wiring path; (c) generation 4 only for the frontend — the frontend is not the risk.

## PP-D38 — The audit harness configuration is versioned in this repo now, at `docs/audit/seam-1/`, and promoted to `aumm-skill` later (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

The reusable configuration is small and load-bearing: `audit-context/AUDIT-INSTRUCTIONS.md`, `THREAT-MODEL-SEED.md` and `RUN-METADATA.md` from the clone, plus the `CORRECTIONS.md` this stage writes (the carried corrections and refuted hypotheses a re-run must not re-derive). The PP6 re-run needs all four reproducible from a commit hash, and the hash it needs is this repo's. `aumm-skill` is the wrong home today on two grounds: its `references/` tree is generated from aumm-site and protected by a CI check that rejects hand edits, and its `SKILL.md` is the spec-grounding layer for answering questions about the protocol, not a tooling surface — a per-seam threat-model seed is a different kind of artifact from a canon reference. **Lock:** PP1.3 creates `docs/audit/seam-1/` with byte copies (USER VERIFY diffs each against the clone, never against a retyped expected-file, per the §8e.1 transcription rule); seams 2 and 3 add `docs/audit/seam-2/` and `seam-3/` at their runs; promotion to `aumm-skill` — under a new top-level `audit/` that the protect-references check does not cover, or to a dedicated repo — is decided when a second seam has proven the template reusable, and is a copy.

## PP-D39 — The exit gate runs on the same auditician commit, `22aa9851`; a second pass at upstream HEAD only if the first is clean (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

A delta attributes to exactly one change only when exactly one thing changed. Re-running on a newer harness would fold harness improvements and harness regressions into the target's delta, and neither could be separated afterwards. **Lock:** PP6.1 clones auditician at `22aa9851caf68f13c9439bd145ef7594f217df5c`; if the PP6.3 delta is zero-or-explicitly-accepted, a second run at upstream HEAD is permitted and is recorded as its own row with its own hash pair, its findings triaged under the same PP-D7 rule — it is a bonus pass, not the gate. The same rule applies to seams 2 and 3 under PP-D20: each pins the harness commit it runs on before it launches and records it in that seam's `RUN-METADATA.md`.

## PP-D40 — The queue's `redeploy unit` column is a closed seven-token vocabulary naming the ENTRY contract rather than the transitive closure, and `done-criteria` names a test artifact (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

**(i) Why this locks before PP3.1a rather than being settled per row.** The queue's ten columns split three ways: five are transcribed from the §"Collapsed defect list" tables, three (`reproduction PoC`, `verdict`, `F-NN`) are deliberately empty at creation, and two are authored from scratch. `redeploy unit` is the column PP4.0 aggregates into the generation-4 manifest, so an unenumerated vocabulary yields rows that are individually plausible and collectively inconsistent — the §12 typed-domain gate in its exact shape, and cheaper to fix in a lock than across 53 saved rows.

**(ii) SEVEN tokens, not the six proposed at adjudication — a correction with its cause.** The six-token set was drawn from the §"Fix sequencing" rung table, which names its units only in passing (order 10 "D.6 also redeploys `TVLOracle`", 13 "distributor/oracle/eligibility unit", 14 "`CCBMultiplier` redeploy", 15 "hook unit", 16 "governance/eligibility/script"). That table never mentions Family F, and Family F is the gap: F.2 edits `src/token/AuMMMinterRouter.sol` and F.3 edits `src/token/AuMM.sol:23` together with `src/lib/AureumTime.sol:28`, all of which PP-D12 places in the SURVIVING set. Both collapse to the same unit for different reasons — `AuMM.setMinter` is one-shot and spent, so a new router orphans the token, and `AureumTime` is a pure library whose functions are all `internal` and therefore inlined into ELEVEN consumers including `AuMM` and `AureumProtocolFeeController`. Either edit takes `AuMM` with it, abandons `GENESIS_BLOCK` and restarts the emission clock: the PB-D70 cascade. That is the most expensive statement the column can make and it does not fold into a token that means something cheaper.

**(iii) The map, by the file a fix edits.** `hook` — `fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol`. `ccb` — `ccb/CCBMultiplier.sol`, `CCBScore.sol`, `CCBShare.sol`, `EMASampler.sol`. `emission-stack` — `emission/EmissionDistributor.sol`, `emission/EfficiencyOracle.sol`, `emission/BodenseeBootstrapChannel.sol`, `gauge/GaugeRegistry.sol`, `gauge/GaugeEligibility.sol`, `gauge/SwapAndDepositToBodensee.sol`, `incendiary/IncendiaryRegistry.sol`. `tvl-oracle` — `emission/TVLOracle.sol` alone, broken out because rung 10 redeploys it on D.6 independently of the distributor. `governance` — `governance/AureumGovernance.sol`, `VotingWeight.sol`, `AureumGovernanceAuthorizer.sol`, `gauge/VaultClassRegistry.sol`, `registry/MiliariumRegistry.sol`. `token-schedule` — `token/AuMM.sol`, `token/AuMMMinterRouter.sol`, `lib/AureumTime.sol`; per (ii) this token MEANS a chain restart and a row carrying it says so. `source-only` — no deployed bytecode changes: `docs/`, `test/`, `formal/`, `script/`, and the `aumm-site` spec repo.

**(iv) The token names the ENTRY contract, never the closure.** PP-D12 states the transitive closure of a `ccb` change as the whole Stage F–L stack plus the 27 pools, with only the Vault, the factory, the stubs and `AuMM` surviving. Expanding that per row would make every non-`source-only` row read the same thing and carry no information, so the column records where the source edit lands and PP4.0 — which already owns "the dependency map derived from every `immutable` and every one-shot setter" — computes what that forces. The queue is the input to that map, not a second copy of it.

**(v) Exactly one token per row.** A fix spanning buckets takes the token of its primary source change and names the others in `ordering constraints` per PP-D12; where two are genuinely co-equal the row takes the one earliest in the cascade `ccb → emission-stack → hook`, because PP-D12 makes the earlier force the later. A root cause that fits no token is a blocker to raise, not a token to invent.

**(vi) `done-criteria` names an artifact, never prose alone.** The form is `path::case`, e.g. `test/whitehat/P1_A3.t.sol::test_donate_strictRise`, so PP4.52 reads a green run rather than a judgment. Rows that cannot reproduce — the Family H Infos and the Workstream-C rows — name the artifact that attests instead: a NOTES clause, an `aumm-site` commit, or a ledger row. `reproduction PoC` is filled at PP3.2, `verdict` at PP3.3, `F-NN` when the ledger row lands in PP4; all three stay empty at creation per the PP3.1 spec.

**(vii) AMENDMENT, 2026-08-21 at PP3.1f-pre — clause (iii)'s map omitted `src/vault/` and `src/factory/`, and Family E raised it.** E.2, E.3 and E.4 all edit `src/vault/AureumProtocolFeeController.sol`, which no token covered, so clause (v)'s "a root cause that fits no token is a blocker to raise" fired as designed. The resolution is a measured weld rather than a preference: `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` is `immutable` at `AureumProtocolFeeController.sol:222` and set in the constructor at `:252`, so the controller cannot be redeployed independently of the hook and PP-D12 constraint 11 already prices the pair as one redeploy; rung 15 calls it "the hook unit". Clause (iii) is therefore READ AS: `hook` covers `fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` AND `vault/AureumProtocolFeeController.sol`; `governance` additionally covers `vault/AureumAuthorizer.sol`, the Stage-B authorizer C.4 and C.8 touch. `vault/AureumVaultFactory.sol` and `factory/AureumWeightedPoolFactory.sol` stay OUT of every token: they are in PP-D12's surviving set, the only remedy any row proposes for them is `renounceOwnership()`, which is an operator transaction and not a source edit, so they take `source-only`; a root cause that genuinely edits their SOURCE breaks the surviving set and is a blocker under clause (v) rather than a token assignment. `src/rate_provider/` is named by no root cause at all. No eighth token is created and the vocabulary stays closed at seven.

## PP-D41 — Capability, not conduct: no row exits Accepted-risk on operator trust (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

A queue row does not exit Accepted-risk on the ground that the key holder is trusted, is the project's own operator, or has no motive. The disposition bar is capability: the capability is absent, or bounded by code that still holds if the holder is hostile. The banned rationale is the test rather than a value-scale filter, so a non-value capability such as E.9's protocol-wide liquidity kill-switch is caught exactly as a value-transfer one is. Economic findings whose remedy is a genuine cost-benefit argument are unaffected and may still exit Accepted-risk with quantifying evidence (F-13 / PB-D9). Inertness is itself a capability argument — a key that can do nothing harmful is bounded by construction — so rows resting on it stay eligible (C.8). Where a fix bounds blast radius rather than removing the capability, the bound is defence in depth and the removal is the primary remedy. This constrains the standing-key rows (C.1, C.4, C.5, C.7 and their kind): enumerating unbounded authorities in §xxix is not an Accepted-risk. It does NOT close PP-D22 (PP5.1) or PP-D23 (PP5.4); those stay per-slot and per G41 row, with this lock as the input that option (b) for an unbounded key cannot satisfy — a constructor-stamped expiry that holds against a hostile holder can, and correcting §xxix after the key is gone or bounded is housekeeping rather than the remedy. **F.1 obligation:** F.1's queue `done-criteria` still names the clamp (`test_boostClampedToLpTrancheFraction`); PP3.3 inverts it to a seating-control case (`code.length` / interface probe / Vault `pool` validation / two-step delayed rebind), with the clamp case retained as a second test and defence in depth. This lock does not edit the queue.

## PP-D42 — done-criteria `path::case`: the CASE NAME (the PP-D40 (vi) invert) is binding at creation, the PATH follows D35 once the PoC lands (user-adjudicated 2026-08-21)

PP-D40 (vi) fixes done-criteria as `path::case` and the queue preamble makes creation names binding on PP3.2. Both are READ AS: the binding half is the CASE NAME — the reproduction-vs-fix invert (A.2's `Bricks` → `DoesNotBrick`, E.6's `IsBurned` → `DoesNotForfeit`), which encodes what the fix must prove and is fixed at adjudication. The PATH is mechanical: the directory the landed reproduction PoC lives in, which D35 fixes by whether the finding is fork-only (`test/fork/<fixture>.t.sol`) or unit (`test/whitehat/P1_<RC>.t.sol`) and which is knowable only once PP3.2 has built the PoC. Where PP3.1 guessed `test/whitehat/` for a finding PP3.2 then reproduces fork-only, PP3.2 corrects the done-criteria path to the fork fixture in the same rung and keeps the case name byte-identical. This is not the ad-hoc cell edit the preamble bars: the preamble bars re-authoring the intended artifact from memory, whereas a path reconciled to a green fork run under D35 discharges (vi) rather than overriding it. It is distinct from the PP-D41 F.1 obligation, which changes the REMEDY a case proves (clamp → seating-control) and is PP3.3's to adjudicate; PP-D42 changes only the directory, is settled by D35 plus a green run, and carries no verdict content. At this lock's creation, across the nine PoCs then filled, exactly one row was directory-split: the five plain rows (A.1, A.3, A.4, C.4, E.1) are fork-to-fork same-case, A.2 / E.6 / F.1 are same-directory inverts, and only E.4 named `test/whitehat/` for a finding the real Vault and `authenticate` chain force fork-only. **E.4 application:** `test/whitehat/P1_E4.t.sol::test_routeYieldFeeDebitsLedger` → `test/fork/AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol::test_routeYieldFeeDebitsLedger`, corrected in the companion queue rung; the case name `test_routeYieldFeeDebitsLedger` — the fix-side invert of the landed `test_P1_E4_routeYieldFeeToHookSpendsWithoutDebitingLedger` — is unchanged. Any later fork-only row whose PP3.1 path assumed `test/whitehat/` is corrected the same way when its PoC lands. **Census as of PP3.2ai:** sixteen PoCs filled, twelve carrying fork done-criteria and four unit; six rows have taken the path correction (E.4, E.2, E.3, C.6, G.3, G.2) across seven applications, C.6 twice because its veto leg landed in a different fixture from its grant and rotation legs; no filled row remains directory-split. The queue is the authority on the running figure, and this paragraph is not re-amended per application.

## PP-D31 — The keeper model is (c) BOTH: three surfaces fold on-chain, two stay a funded keeper named as a trust assumption (user-adjudicated 2026-08-22)

Verified on disk before adjudication, not taken from the plan summary. FIVE production-zero surfaces, not six: `updateEMA` (`src/ccb/EMASampler.sol`), `updateMultiplier` (`src/ccb/CCBMultiplier.sol`), `recordScore` (`src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol`), `advanceTournament` (`src/gauge/GaugeRegistry.sol`) and `poke` (`src/governance/VotingWeight.sol`) each have ZERO call sites across `src/` and `script/`, while carrying 63, 47, 82, 2 and 73 call sites in `test/` respectively. They are not dead code; they are functions only a fixture has ever driven, which is why every green suite in this repo is a hand-operated keeper. **`recordEmissions` is NOT in the keeper set** and the plan's count of six is corrected here: `EmissionDistributor._settlePool` calls it at `:396` on every settlement and it is gated `onlyEmissionsRecorder`, so it is already protocol-driven. **(a) is not a new pattern.** `EmissionDistributor.claim` at `:608` already opens `_accrueGlobal()` then `_settlePool(pool)`, so folding accumulator advancement into an economically motivated user call is a mechanism this codebase already runs. **FOLD SET (a)** — `recordScore` into the first economically motivated settle/claim path, extending that existing lazy settle; `poke` into the recorder path per PP-D18 / B.2, never a free third-party staleness write; `updateEMA` into the first `recordScore`/`claim` of the day for that pool. **KEEPER SET (b)** — `advanceTournament` and `updateMultiplier`, which have no profit motive at all: nobody is paid to advance the tournament, and CCB reads the EMA but never writes it. Both are named as a trust assumption in the constitution and the runbook. **CONSEQUENCE FOR D.5, which is why it lands first or in the same rung.** Under (c) a keeper outage is NOT a global freeze: active pools keep advancing EMA, score and weight through the folded paths, and only idle pools plus the tournament and the multiplier go stale. D.5's freeze-not-zero therefore specifies an IDLE-AND-KEEPER-ONLY freeze with the active paths self-healing, and that target must exist before D.1's `MIN_SAMPLES` floor or D.3's tighter freshness window increase sampler dependence — with no caller, either gate landing first would make every pool immature or stale by default.
