## PP10 — a fork fixture's `setUp` mocks decide which defects it can express; read them before attaching a reproduction to it

**Caught 2026-08-24 at PP3.2by (`test/fork/StageIIntegration.t.sol`).** B.3's flash-inflation reproduction was attached to `StageIIntegrationFixture` because that fixture visibly had everything the PoC needed: `Vault.unlock` deposit and withdrawal callbacks, a self-seat as the hook's trusted router at L104, and a `getSender()` implementation at L120. Both new cases failed on their first run, one reporting a BPT balance of `1e30` for an address that had never received BPT.

**Root cause — an inherited blanket mock.** `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol` L226, reached through the fixture chain, calls `vm.mockCall` on every pilot pool for `balanceOf(address)` returning `1e30`, matched on the signature alone and therefore answering for every caller and every argument. Two mechanisms the PoC turned on are consequently no-ops in that fixture and in every fixture beneath it: the F-17 read-cap at `src/governance/VotingWeight.sol` L180—L181 never binds, because the mocked balance always dominates the recorded stake, and `EmissionDistributor._syncDown` always takes its `recorded <= referenceBalance` early return, so no `syncPosition` heal is expressible at all. The second case was not failing; it was unrunnable.

**This is not a fixture defect.** StageG documents the mock at L218—L224 and gives its reason: those fixtures stage an artificial credited-but-holdless position, which the F-17 receipt cap correctly scores as a phantom, so the receipt view is mocked to a dominating value to keep `_syncDown` and `_positionPower` out of the way, and the comment states outright that the receipt invariant is proven in the unit suites and in the real-BPT faithful test. Every word of it held under test: the untrusted exit's burn worked, `totalSupply` fell by exactly the burned amount, and three assertions passed before the mocked view failed the fourth.

**How it presents, and why that is the trap.** A fixture that cannot express a defect fails as a WRONG ASSERTION rather than as a missing capability. A balance of `1e30` against an expected zero reads as a defective reproduction, not as an unsuitable host, and the assertions that pass beforehand make the run look like a nearly-correct test rather than a misplaced one. Helper availability is not fixture suitability — and the helpers are the visible property, while the mocks are not.

**Fix — relocate, do not patch.** Patching in place would have meant mocking the very balance the first case asserts, which is circular, and the second case would have stayed unrunnable regardless. `test/fork/RouterIntegration.t.sol`, on the StageP chain, carries no blanket `balanceOf` mock and seats the canonical Router on the hook allowlist, which is also closer to the plan's own description of the attack than a fixture acting as its own router.

**Generalization.** Before attaching a reproduction to an existing fork fixture, grep its whole inheritance chain for `vm.mockCall` and `vm.etch`, and ask of each hit which mechanism under test it no-ops. A mock installed for one fixture's convenience is inherited silently by every fixture beneath it, and the mechanism it disables is often exactly the one a later reproduction exists to exercise.

**Cross-references:**

- **PB19** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md` L1193) — the parent class, whose own rule already names "what layer a mock intercepts" among the things to check before borrowing from working code. This entry exists because PB19's trigger is copying a property from a sibling, while this trigger is choosing a host fixture, and a reader with the second trigger does not think to consult the first.
- **F15** (`STAGE_F_NOTES.md` L600) — the same reachability failure one rung earlier: a recipe that existed and carried the governing rule, but could not be found from the file a cold session actually reads.
- **`test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol` L218—L226** — the mock and its stated rationale.
- **PP3.2by** — the sub-step where the misattachment surfaced; reverted rather than patched, and re-authored against the correct fixture.

## PP11 — a zero-hit sentinel is unsatisfiable against any term the drafter's own `Must match` requires to appear in prose

**Caught repeatedly across 2026-08-24, never once costing a fix cycle and never once earning its place.** The §8e.1 USER VERIFY block routinely pairs a zero-expectation with a positive control per PB20. The recurring miss is narrower: the drafter writes a `Must match` bullet REQUIRING some term to appear in a comment or NatSpec, then writes a USER VERIFY line expecting `grep -c` on that same term to read zero. The save is correct, the grep is correct, and the prediction is impossible. It fired on `getSender` at PP3.2by-retry, on `EmissionDistributor` at PP3.2co, on `VotingWeight` at PP3.2ct, and on `AuMTAlreadyBound` at PP3.2dc — four times in one session, each time resolved by reading the file and confirming the hit was the very sentence the prompt had demanded.

**Why PB20 does not already cover it.** PB20 says a zero-hit sentinel proves nothing until the pattern's REACH on the target is measured, and prescribes pairing every zero-expectation with a known-positive control. Both halves were being followed. The reach failure here is not in the target file's pre-existing content but in the payload the drafter is about to commission — the prompt and the sentinel are written in the same breath, and the sentinel is checked against the file as it exists rather than as it is about to exist. Adding a positive control does not help: the zero still reads as one or two and still looks like a defect.

**The fix, demonstrated mid-session.** Sentinel the CODE form, not the bare term. `grep -c 'vm.mockCall(\|vm.etch('` with the opening parenthesis reads zero on a file whose comment discusses `vm.mockCall` in prose, where the bare pattern reads one; the same trick distinguishes `function getSender` from a NatSpec mention of `getSender()`. Where no code form exists — a reserved done-criteria case name, say — the correct move is to keep the zero-hit sentinel and NOT require the term anywhere in the payload, which is what the reserved-name sentinels do correctly throughout the stage. Either discipline works; writing both a requirement and a zero-expectation for the same string does not.

**Generalization.** Before writing a zero-expectation into a USER VERIFY block, grep the DRAFTED PROMPT for the same pattern. If the prompt requires the term, either narrow the sentinel to a form only code can produce, or drop the requirement. This is the drafting-time twin of PB18: PB18 says measure the payload rather than predicting its counts; PP11 says the payload includes the prose you are about to commission.

**Cross-references:**

- **PB20** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — the parent reach rule; its remedy, a paired positive control, does not resolve this variant.
- **PB18** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — the measure-do-not-predict sibling, whose subject is counts rather than reach.
- **PP3.2by-retry, PP3.2co, PP3.2ct, PP3.2dc** — the four occurrences, all harmless, all resolved by reading the saved file.

## PP12 — a `-r` grep handed explicit file arguments does not recurse, and its short result does not look truncated

**Caught at PP3.2do, 2026-08-25, one rung before the wrong number would have entered the queue as fact.** The H.1 pre-flight needed the size of the spec-side fee divergence, and the sweep was a `grep -rn` for the two fee literals over `*.md`, run from the `aumm-site` clone root and piped through `head -12`. It returned 8 matches across 5 files, and that figure was about to become the queue cell's edit-surface estimate. The true figure is 17 occurrences across 11 files. The `-r` was inert: the shell had already expanded `*.md` into an explicit list of root-level files, and grep does not descend into directories it was never handed. The nine hits inside `dist/aumm-skill/`, a GENERATED mirror of the same spec documents, were invisible — and that mirror is what an AI consumer loads, so a fix sized from the 8-figure would have corrected the sources and left the artifact people actually read still saying 0.03%.

**Why the result did not look wrong.** The usual tell for a truncated sweep is output ending exactly at the limit. Here `head -12` returned 8 lines, which reads as a complete result with room to spare; the narrowing happened upstream of `head`, in the file set, where nothing announces itself. A sweep over too small a universe returns a well-formed, plausible, internally consistent answer.

**Why neither PB20 nor PB18 already covers it.** PB20 governs the PATTERN's reach on a target — a zero that could never have matched. Here the pattern was correct and the count non-zero; what failed was the TARGET SET's extent. PB18 says a count is a prediction until a tool produces it, but a tool did produce this one and ran correctly, over a smaller universe than intended. Neither parent catches a measurement whose denominator is wrong.

**The second trap in the same command.** The corrected form must quote its pattern: `--include=*.md` unquoted is glob-expanded by zsh before grep ever sees it and dies on `no matches found`, which is at least a loud failure. `grep -r --include='*.md' .` is the form that works, taking a DIRECTORY argument and never a glob.

**Generalization.** A measurement's universe is part of the measurement, so report the denominator beside the numerator. Pair every tree sweep with its file count, `grep -rl` piped to `wc -l` alongside the occurrence count, because an unexpectedly small file count is the only symptom a silently narrowed universe produces. Never hand `-r` a shell glob.

**Cross-references:**

- **PB20** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — the parent reach rule, whose subject is a pattern's reach on a target rather than the target set's own extent.
- **PB18** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — measure rather than predict; here the tool ran and was still wrong, because the universe was.
- **PP3.2do** — the rung where it surfaced and was corrected before the figure was recorded; that queue cell and the PP3.2dq §11 obligation both carry 17 across 11 files.

## PP13 — measuring the payload does not discharge measuring the file it lands in

**Caught across PP4.1, 2026-08-26 to 2026-08-27, in roughly a dozen verify blocks, none of which failed a save.** PB18 was followed throughout: every payload went through `wc -l` and `grep -o` before handoff, and every payload count was right. What was wrong, repeatedly, was the number DERIVED from that measurement about the file the payload lands in. PP4.1c12 is the clean instance — BLOCK A was measured as content, then the file total was predicted as `150 + 15` for a block that is 23 lines, giving 167 against a true 175. The save was correct; the expectation was not.

**The same shape in four other costumes.** An anchor cited at its PRE-INSERT position when inserting necessarily shifts it (PP4.1b put `---` at L274; it had moved to L275). An occurrence count asserted from the drafter's reading of the NEW text alone when the term already lived elsewhere in the file (`FORTY-ONE` predicted 1, measured 2, the earlier amendment already carrying it). A count asserted for a form the code does not actually contain (`address vault_` predicted 2, measured 1, because the guard reads `vault_ == address(0)` and holds no such substring). And a roster recalled rather than counted (`revert ZeroAddress();` predicted 9, measured 11, three other guarded setters forgotten).

**Why PB18 does not already cover it, and why that let the habit survive.** PB18 governs the PAYLOAD — the text about to be inserted, measured before handoff. Every miss above was a claim about the FILE: its totals after the insert, its existing occurrences, its anchors' positions once shifted. Two different universes, and measuring the first feels like diligence for the second. The command WAS run, the number it produced WAS correct, and it answered a different question than the verify block asked.

**The rule.** A `Must match` or USER VERIFY number about the SAVED FILE is a prediction until measured against that file. For a pure insertion the cheapest instrument is `git diff --stat`, whose insertion count is independent of the drafter's arithmetic; for an existing-occurrence claim it is one `grep -c` on disk BEFORE the expectation is written; for an anchor's position it is the observation that everything below an insert has moved. Where a number genuinely cannot be measured in advance, label it DERIVED in the chat text so a mismatch reads as a bad prediction rather than a bad save.

**Cross-references:**

- **PB18** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — the payload half of the same discipline; this is its file-state complement, not a restatement of it.
- **PP4.1c12** — the clean instance: payload measured, file total derived, 167 against 175.
- **PP4.1b / PP4.1c4b / PP4.1c11-pre** — the shifted anchor, the substring form the code does not contain, and the term already living elsewhere.

## PP14 — a fixed `tail -N` selects by position, and truncated output looks exactly like a report

**Caught twice in one session, 2026-08-30, at the PP4.7 rung gate and again at the PP4.8e3 build gate.** The rung-7 fork roster ran 25 file-scoped invocations through a loop capturing `tail -1` per file. Forge prints its `Tip: run forge test --rerun` line AFTER the summary when a run has failures, so for the one file that failed, the captured line was the tip while the `[FAIL]` rows and the tally scrolled past unread — the failure text was destroyed and no cause could ever be assigned, which is why `WK18ThinVenuePumpSim`'s transient is recorded as UNEXPLAINED rather than diagnosed. Hours later `forge build 2>&1 | tail -20` captured only trailing lint warnings from an unrelated fixture and cut off the verdict line, leaving the build's success unreadable at the exact rung whose success criterion WAS compilation.

**Why it survives a careful reader.** Truncated output does not announce itself. A tip line reads as a summary; a tail of warnings reads as a clean run that has warnings; an empty filter result reads as nothing to report. In all three the command ran, the tool answered correctly, and the harness threw the answer away — so the failure is invisible precisely where a reader is looking for reassurance, and both instances here were caught by accident rather than by the check.

**The rule.** Select on the OUTCOME, never on position. Filter for the line forms that carry the verdict — `^Ran .* test suite`, `^\[FAIL`, `Compiler run successful`, `Compilation failed` — and where the tool offers an exit status, read THAT instead of any text, because `EXIT=$?` cannot miss a line form the drafter failed to anticipate. A fixed `tail` is admissible only where a tool's last line is guaranteed to be its verdict, which forge guarantees in neither of its two most-used commands.

**Cross-references:**

- **PB20** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — the pattern-reach sibling: a zero-hit sentinel proves nothing until the pattern's reach on the target file is measured.
- **PP12** — the file-set sibling: a `-r` grep handed explicit file arguments does not recurse, and its short result does not look truncated.
- **PP4.7i** — the WK18 transient, recorded unexplained because this defect destroyed its evidence; **PP4.8e3b** — the build verdict lost to `tail -20` and recovered by `EXIT=$?`.

## PP15 — a same-named sibling setter satisfies the grep that was supposed to prove the seat

**Caught at PP4.12ag, 2026-09-07, twenty-eight commits into the rung and against a fully green Part-A.** B.2's remedy is a recorder-to-reader push that needs `EmissionDistributor.setVotingWeight` called once at Stage K. Every source edit had landed and every gate was green, yet the setter had ZERO call sites in `script/` and `test/`: the production spine left the sink at zero, both push sites returned early behind their unbound guard, and the remedy was DEAD on chain. A grep for `setVotingWeight` returned five hits, so the seat read as covered. Four were `VaultClassRegistry.setVotingWeight`, a same-named setter on a different contract seated as `_wire`'s FIRST call, and the fifth was the new declaration itself.

**Why it survives a careful reader.** A missing call site usually fails loudly, at a revert or a zero read. This one cannot: the push sites are guarded on the unbound slot precisely so the Stage-H-to-K window works, so an unseated sink is indistinguishable from a correctly deferred one and every test that does not exercise the push passes either way. The name grep then supplies false comfort in the one place a reader looks for it, because its answer is non-empty and plausible, a wiring script that really does mention the name, in a call that seats a different contract. PP-D46 is the same defect with a thinner mask: there a script proposed without ever accepting, and the missing entry had no namesake to hide behind.

**The rule.** A new one-shot setter is UNSEATED until a grep proves otherwise, and that grep must be one a namesake cannot satisfy: its EVENT, or its contract-qualified call. `VotingWeightBound` had zero hits outside `src/` and would have answered in one command. A bare-name grep is evidence of nothing whenever another contract declares the same name, and same-named setters are COMMON in this tree by design, since the shapes are deliberately mirrored on each other. Pair the grep with a witness that reads the slot back through the production path.

**Cross-references:**

- **PP-D46** — the same gap without the namesake: `DeployStageL` proposed and never accepted, and that rung's closing gate did not cover the fixture which would have caught it.
- **PB20** (`STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md`) — the reach sibling. A grep's answer is worthless until its reach on the target is measured; here the reach was too WIDE rather than too narrow.
- **PP4.12ag** — the amendment that recorded it, PP-D55 (xiv); **PP4.12ai** — wire (5), the line that had never been written; **PP4.12al** / **PP4.12an** — the two witnesses that now read the slot back.

## PP3.3 — Family A verdicts (A.2 and A.3 locked; A.1 and A.4 deferred)

**Locked.** Both rows exit **Fix**, and neither severity moves.

- **A.2 — Fix (High, unchanged).** The capability is available to any address at a cost of two wei, permanently, with no rescue path in the contract. PP-D41's bar is a capability that is absent or bounded by code against a hostile holder, and this is neither; it is not an economic cost-benefit case either, the attacker's cost being approximately zero. Re-verified at HEAD before locking: the absolute residual `if (residual != 0) revert HelperBalanceNonZero(residual)` appears TWICE and identically, at `src/gauge/SwapAndDepositToBodensee.sol:351-354` and `:381-384`, once per rail, which is what makes the full brick cost two wei rather than one.
- **A.3 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** The trigger needs no attacker at all: a third-party rate provider reporting below par bricks every governance entry, because `_createProposal` donates before it records. Nothing bounds it in code. This one is not a design question — the correct form ALREADY EXISTS in this repository at `src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:641-643`, `if (postReserve <= preReserve) revert ReserveDidNotRise(...)`, carrying a PB-D68 (xvii) comment stating why exactness fails on a DONATION: that branch round-trips raw through scaled18 rather than deep-copying `maxAmountsIn`, so the consumed amount is not caller-predictable. The sibling at `:424-427` still asserts exact equality. The fix is a backport of a proven form.

**Severity: neither moved, deliberately.** The queue preamble lets PP3.3 move a severity, but only against a recorded reproduction, and both rows reproduce exactly as filed. A.3's consequence matches A.2's while its trigger requires an oracle state where A.2's requires two wei from anyone — which is precisely what the report's High/Medium split already encodes. Moving either would re-litigate a post-review figure without new evidence.

**Deferred with cause.** Neither row is unripe; each waits on a decision that IS its disposition, and PP-D4 makes a verdict a lock rather than a placeholder, so "Fix pending PP-D22" is not a PP3.3 product.

- **A.1** — waits on **PP-D17** (seed accounting, decided ONCE for governance and emission). Its other constraints, PP-D10 and PP-D24, are locked.
- **A.4** — waits on **PP-D22** (the dissolution handoff). The interaction is direct rather than incidental: rotation 5, `setDonateAuthorizer`, loses its rationale entirely once A.4's fix makes governance's donate entry immutable, so the shape of A.4 and the decision on whether to run the handoff are one question.

**Cross-family: this batch does not own its own fix rung.** PP-D11 binds A.2, A.3 AND **E.1** into ONE PP4 rung (rung 2) in one shape — strict rise or delta, never an absolute `balanceOf(this)` — on the G10 ground that the project found the right form once at PB-D68 (xvii) and did not propagate it. E.1 is unblocked and locks in the E batch. Verdict batching is per family; this fix rung is not, and PP4 must not lose the grouping because the A batch closed first.

**A.2's sweep is load-bearing, and the fix-intent cell understates it.** That cell offers "delta assertion or donate `balanceOf(this)`" as alternatives. The second half alone is NOT a complete remedy and the queue cell is deliberately left unedited here rather than rewritten mid-adjudication. Verified at HEAD: a rescuer cannot clear a stray residual by donating it, because the Vault rejects a sub-minimum amount at `_ensureValidTradeAmount` (`lib/.../Vault.sol:1509`, reverting `TradeAmountTooSmall()` at `:1524`, reached from the add-liquidity path at `:689`) before the helper's residual check is ever evaluated. A sweep or permissionless flush is therefore part of the remedy, not an optional companion to it. `IncendiaryRegistry.buyBoost` is a fourth victim of the same assertion.

## PP3.3 — Family B verdicts (six locked; B.3 and B.4 deferred)

**Locked.** Five exit **Fix** and one exits **Accepted-risk**; no severity moves. Each was re-verified at HEAD before locking rather than inherited from its reproduction rung.

- **B.1 — Fix (High, unchanged).** The quorum denominator is `_totalQualifiedWeight` (`src/governance/VotingWeight.sol:114-115`), the sum of POKED checkpoints, so it counts only holders who opted in. At launch the trace is provably empty and the turnout-relative two-thirds bar degenerates unopposed.
- **B.2 — Fix (High, unchanged).** `recordWithdrawal` (`src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol:579-598`) contains ZERO references to `VotingWeight` — it writes the clock and `userLP` and nothing else — so a closed position keeps voting until some third party pokes it.
- **B.5 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `_syncDown` zeroes `effectiveQualBlock[pool][user]` unconditionally at `:525` and `syncPosition` is permissionless and names its victim at `:532-533`, making the §viii reset a stranger's option to exercise one block before a snapshot.
- **B.6 — Fix (High, unchanged).** Both liquidity callbacks trust `IRouterSender(router).getSender()` and `recordWithdrawal` zeroes the clock on any `amount > 0` (`src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:590-591`), so a trusted router zeroes any holder's matured clock for a dust burn.
- **B.7 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** See the F-15 carry below.
- **B.8 — Accepted-risk (Low, unchanged).** The first Accepted-risk of the stage, and it clears PP-D41 on capability rather than on trust: the corrective is PERMISSIONLESS, any third party re-poking any holder restores the era-1 exponent, so the drift is bounded by code and not by conduct. The gain is also quantified and modest, `ema^(1/12)`, which is 3.16x at 1e6 CHF and 4.64x only at 1e8. **That quantification is MEASURED, not derived** — `test/whitehat/P1_B8.t.sol:94` asserts the ratio against a `1_000_000^(1/12)` constant and `:124` asserts the self-heal. Keep the contrast in view when D.7's Info half and C.8's factory-owner reach their batches: both rest on source argument alone, where this row has a test artifact. The row's own fix intent sets its floor at "at minimum document".

**Deferred with cause.**

- **B.3** — waits on **PP-D17** (seed accounting). Its done-criteria additionally names the `IERC20(pool).totalSupply()` branch PP-D17 does not recommend, so the name cannot settle before the decision does.
- **B.4 (composite, Critical)** — waits transitively. It closes via B.1 + B.3 + C.4, and B.3 (PP-D17) and C.4 (PP-D25) are both deferred. B.1 locking Fix does not release it.

**The composite rule, recorded once here and applying to G.4 as well.** A composite row defers until EVERY constituent is locked. Its disposition is not independently determinable — if a constituent's own decision resolves away from Fixed, the composite's closure path breaks — and PP-D4 makes a verdict a lock rather than a placeholder. This also corrects the PP3.3 pre-flight readiness map, which was built by grepping the ordering-constraints column for open PP-D ids and therefore missed both composites, whose dependency is expressed in prose ("closes via ...") rather than as a PP-D reference. **The corrected census is 40 unblocked and 13 deferred, not 42 and 11.**

**B.7 reopens F-15's Accepted-risk disposition (PB-D10), and the reopening is narrow.** `vetoProposal` banks absolute `vetoSupport` and divides by a LIVE `votingWeight.totalSupply()` at `src/gauge/VaultClassRegistry.sol:284-286` with no zero guard. PB-D10's evidence suite `test/whitehat/F15_vetoDenominatorInflation.t.sol` carries six tests, NONE of which burn, withdraw or revoke, and its own header states that it bounds defender INFLATION. Those six assertions remain true; what fails is PB-D10 reading them as bounding the whole risk, because B.7's ratchet moves the denominator DOWN. Three carries, none of them this sub-step's work: **(1)** F-15's whitehat ledger row needs re-statusing, which is PP3.4's; **(2)** PB-D10 in `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md` needs a reopening amendment, which is a cross-stage beat; **(3)** the zero-denominator face is NOT an edge but the launch condition — `totalSupply()` returns the poked sum, structurally zero for the first 60 days of every chain, which is F-21's condition at a second call site, and `AureumGovernance.sol:367` carries the PB-D62 guard `if (snapshotSupply == 0) return false` while `VaultClassRegistry` has none, so during that window every veto panics and every class finalizes unopposed.

**B.1's done-criteria rewrite is deferred to PP4 rung 12.** `test_quorumDenominatorCountsUnpokedHolders` names a recorder-side denominator that PP-D14 says breaks INV-1 by construction, but PP-D14 also requires the redesign to STATE the invariant that replaces INV-1 and assigns that work to PP4 rung 12 in its own owner column. Inventing a replacement case name now would bake a fix shape ahead of the invariant justifying it, which is the shape PP-D41 flags on F.1's clamp. The verdict is Fix; the name waits.

**A recurring meta-defect, named but not filed.** Three rows adjudicated so far are the same failure: a correct form found once and never propagated. A.3 (the hook's strict rise at `AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:641-643`, its sibling helper still asserting exact equality), B.7 (F-21's zero guard on the governance path, absent from `VaultClassRegistry`), and H.5 (`version()` reporting an Aureum name on an upstream body). PP-D11 already names the class via G10. No RB row is opened here — PP3.5 owns the register — but the pattern is recorded now so that beat does not have to rediscover it.

## PP3.3 — Family C verdicts (five locked; C.1, C.2 and C.4 deferred)

**Locked.** All five exit **Fix** and no severity moves. Each rests on an anchor re-verified at HEAD rather than inherited from its reproduction rung.

- **C.5 — Fix (High, unchanged).** `VaultAdmin.enableRecoveryMode` skips `_authenticateCaller()` whenever the pool or the Vault is paused — Balancer's own comment says so — the recovery bit never expires, and `unpausePool` does not clear it. A pool's `pauseManager` therefore opts that pool out of the 50/50 protocol-fee split for roughly four years at about 120k gas, against a repair path costing 17 days and 1,000 svZCHF.
- **C.6 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** The IDENTICAL rail conjunct is evaluated at two different times: `src/gauge/GaugeEligibility.sol:434-435` at activation, where `true` buys a permanent emission entitlement that a later `false` does not remove, and `:454-455` at execute, where `false` annuls a passed two-thirds vote and burns the proposer's bond. The row's own corrections bound it and are kept: the flag confers ZERO governance weight and the key cannot mint to itself.
- **C.7 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `registrySetter` is deployer-pinned at `src/ccb/CCBMultiplier.sol:143` and never sealed: `setMiliariumRegistry` appears twice in `script/` and BOTH are the `TVLOracle` leg, so no script fires the `CCBMultiplier` one-shot. The hook's `_incendiaryAdmin` is likewise unfired and, unlike the CCB slot, has no getter and no clamp.
- **C.8 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** Exactly TWO occurrences of `code.length` exist in all of `src/`: a NatSpec at `src/gauge/SwapAndDepositToBodensee.sol:294` documenting its own absence, and one real guard at `src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol:279`. That single guard sits on the only REVERSIBLE path in the set, while all fifteen one-shot and irreversible seatings guard `!= 0` alone, none probing an interface and none carrying a deploy-time read-back.
- **C.9 — Fix (Low, unchanged).** `if (depositAmount == 0) return 0;` at `src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:611` returns before the `ReserveDidNotRise` proof-of-delivery at `:641-643`, while `StrandedFeesRecovered` still reports the full `amountIn`. The row's "zero test coverage" clause was RETRACTED by the report itself on a truncated grep, and that retraction stands.

**PP-D41 on the two standing-key rows it names here.** Neither C.5 nor C.7 may rest on trust, and neither does. C.5's capability is unbounded by code: any pool's `pauseManager`, no expiry inside the Vault's four-year window, no governance path to reverse it. C.7's CCB face IS bounded — the `[0.75, 1.25]` clamp is an inductive invariant, so a repoint buys availability rather than magnitude — but PP-D41 is explicit that a blast-radius bound is defence in depth while removal is the primary remedy, and the hook's `_incendiaryAdmin` carries no bound at all. Both exit Fix.

**Deferred with cause.**

- **C.1** — waits on **PP-D22 AND PP-D23**, the only doubly-gated row in the queue. Its done-criteria is a PP-D23 clause plus an `aumm-site` §xxix commit.
- **C.2** — waits on **PP-D22**; its done-criteria IS a PP-D22 clause, so the decision and the artifact are the same object.
- **C.4** — waits on **PP-D25** (the authorizer probe versus deliberate ossification).

**Row granularity versus face granularity, settled here for the rest of the stage.** The plan expects three Accepted-risk dispositions — B.8, D.7's Info half and C.8's `vault-factory-owner` — but two of those three are sub-row FACES and the queue carries one verdict cell per row. C.8 is the first to hit it: its root cause is the inconsistent-hardening class across fifteen seatings and plainly needs fixing, while `vault-factory-standing-owner-is-inert` is one of its six audit files, Info, and genuinely inert. PP-D41 keeps inertness eligible, but `renounceOwnership()` is already in C.8's fix intent at near-zero cost, so the face is remediated WITH the row rather than split out of it. **The consequence, stated so PP3.4 does not re-derive it: at queue-row granularity the plan's three expected Accepted-risks collapse to ONE, B.8, already locked at PP3.3b; D.7's Info half and C.8's factory-owner are intra-row notes.** PP3.4 may still split an `F-NN` ledger row per face where a face warrants it, the F-13 precedent.

**C.5's unestablished residual is CARRIED, not chased, and its owner is named.** `CLAUDE.md` §11 records this as an open thread for PP3.3, so it is discharged here by assignment rather than by silence. The measurement stands: control 24,963,783,544,357 against recovery 2,483,268,481, about 10,053 to 1, with the swap still succeeding. The SOURCE of that 2,483,268,481 residual is NOT established — the hook held zero svZCHF and zero sUSDS immediately before that swap, so it is not swept dust — and per PB22 no cause is asserted that was not verified. It does not bear on the verdict, which is Fix wherever the residual originates. The settle path is to instrument the hook's balance across the swap inside the existing PoC, and ownership passes to **PP4's C.5 rung**, which re-measures that path anyway. Turning a verdict batch into a fork investigation is the wrong trade.

## PP3.3 — Family G verdicts (five locked; G.4 deferred)

**Locked.** All five exit **Fix** and no severity moves. Each rests on an anchor re-verified at HEAD.

- **G.1 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `state()` returns `Succeeded` on `eta == 0` at `src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol:340` with NO time bound; expiry exists only downstream of `queue`, so a proposal never queued sits outside every window forever. `queue` at `:388` and `execute` at `:401` are both permissionless, and `TimelockNotMet` reverts at `:407` BEFORE `p.executed = true` at `:408`, so a premature attempt never consumes the ticket.
- **G.2 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** A composition challenge is a mandate against a SLOT INDEX, not against an occupant: propose passes `targetPool_` as `address(0)` at `:244`, discarding the incumbent, and execute re-resolves `SLOT_REGISTRY.poolAtSlot(p.slot)` at `:430` and revokes whoever is standing there. Two honest challenges on one slot therefore let the second revoke the winner of the first.
- **G.3 — Fix (High, unchanged).** `activateGauge` is permissionless, `proposeCompositionChallenge` reads the candidate's gauge status ZERO times, and `registerGaugeFromComposition` reverts `AlreadyGauged` on an `Active` pool while no writer anywhere returns a status to `None`. A credible candidate is therefore pre-poisonable permanently, years before anyone proposes it.
- **G.5a — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `VaultClassRegistry.finalizeProposal` (`:295-303`) tests only `finalized` and the veto window before writing `admittedClasses[...] = true`: no deadline and no re-check of admission. The row's correction is kept — bonds are never refundable, so a stockpile costs N x 1,000 svZCHF sunk, while the live half needs no stockpile at all.
- **G.5b — Fix (Info, unchanged).** Neither `finalizeProposal` nor `vetoProposal` bounds `proposalId` against `nextProposalId`, and a zero struct's `createdBlock == 0` makes the window test pass at any block above `VETO_WINDOW_BLOCKS`, admitting `address(0)` as a vault class. G14's heuristic explains why this is the single instance: the zero record AUTHORISES an action here and merely permits an opportunity at the other nine sites.

**Two severities are held ACTIVELY, and a later reader must not correct them upward.** Both carry provenance that reads like an error if the provenance is lost. **G.1's Medium** is an anti-double-count: it was raised Medium to High at validation on the `VaultAuthorizerChange` consequence and RETURNED to Medium at review, because that High leg is already counted in C.4, so restoring it would count one consequence twice across two rows. **G.2's Medium** is a considered hold carrying an explicit open invitation — the report says a reviewer who accepts G21's attendance-bar result may raise it to High — and the queue preamble permits a move only against new evidence, which this batch did not produce. Both stay where the report's post-review figure put them.

**Deferred with cause.**

- **G.4 (composite, Medium)** — closes via C.2 + G.2 + B.1 + a global challenge rate limit, and C.2 is PP-D22-gated, so the composite rule recorded at PP3.3b applies. One further point belongs here rather than with the fix: **the global challenge rate limit is a fix element with NO queue row of its own**, so it appears in no verdict batch and the census cannot track it. That is the same shape as PP-D9's companion re-check, and PP4 must carry both outside the row census or lose them.

**Cross-family fix bindings, verdict-independent.** Three of the five rows locked here have fix sequencing tied to rows that are still deferred; the verdicts do not wait on that, the sequencing does. **PP-D30** binds G.2 and G.3 into ONE rung over the same six lines at `AureumGovernance.sol:426-433`. **PP-D9** adds a mandatory companion to that rung, the `GaugeChallenge` execute-time `slotOf == 0` re-check, which carries no queue row. **PP-D19** orders G.5a before C.2 makes `revokeVaultClass` reachable, and C.2 is deferred. **PP-D29** orders G.1 after or with A.1, and A.1 is deferred.

## PP3.3 — Family D verdicts (seven locked; D.7 deferred)

**Locked.** All seven exit **Fix** and no severity moves. Each rests on an anchor re-verified at HEAD.

- **D.1 — Fix (High, unchanged).** Maturity is pure elapsed-block arithmetic with no sample-count floor across all three consumers, and the cold-start seed is a raw spot assignment, so a pumped seed left alone for the window and refreshed once reaches every consumer at almost the attacker's chosen value. F-05's analysis named this residual; its FIX is what was incomplete.
- **D.2 — Fix (High, unchanged).** See the acceptance refutation below.
- **D.3 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** Decay is applied once per CALL while the freshness gate is measured in TIME and tolerates fourteen times the daily cadence, so a drained pool's EMA is held high for weeks by sampling exactly as rarely as the gate permits.
- **D.4 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `CCBMultiplier.updateMultiplier` reads three raw `tvlEMA` values with none of the gates the other three consumers apply, over a gauge set anyone can grow for 100 svZCHF, and a PER-POOL cadence guards a GLOBAL baseline. Bounded to plus or minus 25% by the clamp, which is what holds it at Medium.
- **D.5 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** A fourteen-day outage of the unfunded `updateEMA` keeper drives `_positionPower` to zero for everyone, after which permissionless `poke(anyHolder)` is a one-way ratchet writing zeros into the quorum denominator until F-21 defeats every proposal; the kill is permanent per proposal even though live state recovers.
- **D.6 — Fix (High, unchanged).** `TVLOracle._venueRatio` calls `getPoolTokens`, `getPoolData` and `getNormalizedWeights` BARE at `:328-330`, so one reverting third-party `getRate()` anywhere in the roster reverts `tvl()` protocol-wide, `updateEMA` can never again advance its stamp, and fourteen days later the electorate is permanently drainable to zero.
- **D.8 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** The loop accumulates `miliariumAgg` over the LIVE `miliariumRegistry.miliariumPoolsCount()` at `:226-227` and `:231` divides by the literal `MILIARIUM_POOL_COUNT = 28` declared at `:62`. The divisor is not the length walked. The baseline runs 7.143% low against a 0.1% dead zone, seventy-one times it, so a pool at the true constellation average is stepped DOWN every epoch where the anti-cyclical rule should step it up. `poolCount` is already in scope at `:227` for the fix.

**D.2 refutes an accepted-risk rationale written into the source itself, and the refutation is the same shape as B.7's reopening of F-15.** `src/ccb/EMASampler.sol:15-18` states the acceptance in its own NatSpec: the single-block-spike attack surface is accepted and defended in depth by F-10 efficiency-tournament tier caps, a six-week smoothing window and the Year-1 buffer, explicitly not at the EMASampler layer. That reasoning assumes a spike sitting INSIDE an otherwise honest series. But `:130-134` awards one exclusive sampling slot per pool per day to whoever calls first, and `:151` stamps `lastEMAUpdateBlock` to that caller's own block, so a bot calling first each day authors one hundred percent of the sequence. Smoothing cannot dilute an attacker who owns every sample. **Carried for PP4 and deliberately not acted on here: that acceptance rests on three legs, and a second leg is E.7a's subject, which reproduced every efficiency numerator at zero with ranking collapsed to address order. The acceptance must not be restored on the strength of its remaining legs without re-checking them, and E.7a's own verdict is still pending.**

**D.5's Medium is load-bearing and must be held actively, exactly as G.1's and G.2's are.** The plan states the reason: recoverable by anyone for gas, which is the distinction from D.6. Verified — `VotingWeight.poke(address holder)` at `:136` is `external` with no access modifier, so any party can undo the ratchet, while D.6's oracle revert can be cleared by NO caller at all. That asymmetry is the entire difference between this Medium and that High, and a later reader who levels them has erased the reason both figures are where they are.

**Two structural results this batch produces.**

- **The `ccb` redeploy unit is now fully adjudicated.** It carries exactly five rows — C.7, locked at PP3.3c, plus D.1, D.3, D.4 and D.8 locked here — and all five exit Fix. It is the first redeploy unit in the queue with no unlocked member, which matters because PP-D12 prices redeploys per unit rather than per row. D.2 is `hook`, D.5 is `governance` and D.6 is `tvl-oracle`, so those three do not join it.
- **PP-D31's fold set interlocks three of these rows.** Its FOLD SET (a) moves `updateEMA` into the first `recordScore` or `claim` of the day and `poke` into the recorder path per B.2. That is simultaneously the structural answer to D.5's unfunded-keeper root and a material change to D.2's monopolisation surface, because sampling that rides an economically motivated path stops being a dedicated bot's exclusive daily slot. PP-D31's own ordering then puts D.1 and D.3 after or with D.5.

**Deferred with cause.**

- **D.7** — waits on **PP-D23**. Its Info half is one of the two intra-row Accepted-risk notes settled at PP3.3c, so the row-granularity rule already applies to it when PP-D23 releases the row.

## PP3.3 — Family E verdicts (ten locked; E.9 deferred)

**Locked.** All ten exit **Fix** and no severity moves. Each rests on an anchor re-verified at HEAD.

- **E.1 — Fix (High, unchanged).** `distribute()` mints exactly `pendingAccrual`, settles it, then requires `balanceOf(this) == 0` with NO entry snapshot, so one wei by transfer or any LP's `claim(pool, channel)` reverts every future call. The revert is the identical `HelperBalanceNonZero` form A.2 carries, which is why PP-D11 binds the three sites into one rung and one shape.
- **E.2 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** The fee controller's entire `authenticate` surface resolves to `AureumGovernance` after the Stage-K handoff and is reachable by no caller, while the permissionless `collectAggregateFees` keeps pushing swap-leg protocol fees into that dead ledger.
- **E.3 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `_globalProtocolYieldFeePercentage` is never initialised, its only write site being the `authenticate`-gated setter, so every pool is stamped 0% at registration and the constitution's 10% ERC-4626 skim is 0% in code.
- **E.4 — Fix (Low, CONTINGENT — see below).** `routeYieldFeeToHook` approves and lets the hook pull `amount` from the controller's commingled balance without debiting `_protocolFeeAmounts[pool][token]` and without bounding `amount` by the pool's credit.
- **E.5 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `recordScore`'s `isGaugeApproved` gate blocks the CORRECTION as well as the abuse: a revoked pool's score can be removed from `totalScore` by no caller, `claim` has no gauge gate, and every surviving pool is diluted permanently. The constitution's "loses: AuMM emissions" is false in code.
- **E.6 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `_accrueGlobal`'s `totalScore == 0` branch advances `lastAccrualBlock` without integrating, so a window in which every score self-cleared forfeits that window's entire LP tranche, and the permissionless `claim` lets any caller book the forfeiture at a moment of their choosing.
- **E.7a — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `setFeeRecorder` appears in `script/` ZERO times, so every `numeratorSma` is zero, `computeEpochSnapshot`'s sort falls through to its address tie-break, and the single highest-addressed pool is clamped to about 0.1% against an honest 3.6% share, permanently after the handoff.
- **E.7b — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `advanceTournament` enumerates the whole active set in one transaction with a per-pool external call, a cold `SSTORE` and an O(n-squared) sort, and the revert is self-perpetuating because whatever the last affordable snapshot wrote freezes forever, caps included. Measured at PP3.2: 1,152,749 gas at 30 pools and 3,447,068 at 90, a marginal 38,238 per pool, extrapolating to 34,031,820 at the row's 890 pools against a 30M block — and a LOWER bound, since ascending seeding hands insertion sort its best case.
- **E.7c — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** The clamp solves for a score against `totalScore` as it stands in the caller's own block and freezes it, and `recordScore` is permissionless, so the caller picks the denominator and the realised share drifts above the cap.
- **E.8 — Fix (Medium, unchanged).** `poolBoostCursor` advances only inside the non-zero-registry guard, so a rebind after the H-D29 valve pays the whole unbound window's backlog on top of a full tranche. **The verdict is Fix on the REPRODUCED distributor-side face; the registry-side half — `buyBoost` holding no distributor reference, so buyers keep paying for a stream nothing will deliver — is NOT reproduced**, per the §11 carry-forward, so PP4 implements that half against an unproven reproduction. E.8 also closes with F.1's clamp, which bounds the backlog.

**E.4's Low is CONTINGENT and expires on its own fix, which is a different thing from the severities held actively elsewhere.** D.5's Medium, G.1's Medium and G.2's Medium are each stable figures held for a standing reason. E.4's Low is not: the row is Low ONLY because E.2 makes the caller unreachable, and the plan names it a fix-blocker in those words — restoring reachability makes the missing debit live in the same transaction. So "Low" here must never be read as "safe to defer"; it is a severity that becomes wrong the moment the row above it is repaired, and PP-D13 exists to prevent exactly that window.

**PP-D13's chain crosses two families AND two redeploy units, and neither fact is visible from any single row.** The order is `E.4 debit` then `E.2 reachable withdrawals` then `A.3 strict rise` then `E.3 non-zero yield fee`, with no step before its predecessor. Two consequences for PP4. First, **A.3 was already locked at PP3.3a and sits in TWO orderings at once** — PP-D11, one rung and one shape with A.2 and E.1, and PP-D13 as step three here — and both must be satisfied. Second, **the chain alternates redeploy units**: E.4, E.2 and E.3 are `hook` while A.3 is `emission-stack`, so this cannot be discharged by a single hook redeploy; an emission-stack change has to land BETWEEN E.2 and E.3.

**E.3's softening depends on a capability C.4 may remove.** The report corrects "can never be set" as one word too strong, because an authorizer change reaches the setter — but that path is `ProposalType.VaultAuthorizerChange`, which is C.4's subject and is DEFERRED on PP-D25, the authorizer probe versus deliberate ossification. If PP-D25 resolves toward ossification, the correction lapses and "can never be set" becomes literally true again. The durable half of the filing survives either way and is what PP-D13's ordering encodes: switching the skim on without a reachable exit makes the stranding real.

**E.6's naive fix is UNSAFE and PP-D33 gates it.** Returning without writing the cursor pins it, and `_phaseAwareBody:332` calls `IIncendiaryRegistry.integratedSkim`, which loops once per 14-day epoch spanned with no cursor and no `try/catch`, so a pinned cursor makes the eventual catch-up unboundedly expensive for whichever user triggers it. The cursor change therefore lands only after `integratedSkim` is O(1) via a prefix sum.

**Structural: the `hook` redeploy unit is now fully adjudicated.** E.2, E.3 and E.4 join B.6, C.9 and D.2, so all six `hook` rows exit Fix — the second unit after `ccb` with no member left unlocked. `emission-stack` moves to 12 of 16 locked, its remaining four being A.4, D.7 and E.9 deferred plus F.1 pending the next batch.

**Deferred with cause.**

- **E.9** — waits on **PP-D22**. Its capability, repointing the emissions recorder, is a protocol-wide claim and liquidity kill switch, and PP-D41 already forecloses any disposition of it that rests on the holder being trusted.

## PP3.3 — Family F verdicts (F.1 locked; F.2 and F.3 deferred)

**Locked.**

- **F.1 — Fix (Critical, unchanged).** The queue's only Critical root cause. `EmissionDistributor.setIncendiaryRegistry` at `:227-231` is a three-line body — read the old address, write the new one, emit — with no zero guard, no `code.length`, no one-shot and no timelock, the zero address being permitted BY DESIGN as the H-D29 deprecation valve. Its sibling `setAuMTContractForPool` at `:215-219` validates `newAuMTContract` against zero and against a prior binding while leaving `pool` ENTIRELY unvalidated, so any address can be seated as a pool. The boost leg then adds whatever the bound contract returns straight into `poolAccRewardPerLP` at `:406-411`, outside every gate the allocation leg carries. PP-D41 bars any disposition resting on the holder being trusted, and PP-D35 orders this fix before any dissolution decision is exercised.

**PP3.3's one mandated done-criteria rewrite is discharged here.** PP-D41 carries a standing obligation in its own text: F.1's done-criteria named only the clamp, `test_boostClampedToLpTrancheFraction`, and PP3.3 must invert it to a seating-control case with the clamp retained as a second test. The reason is PP-D41's general rule rather than a preference about this row — where a fix bounds blast radius instead of removing the capability, the bound is defence in depth and the removal is the primary remedy, so a done-criteria naming only the bound would let PP4 close a Critical row without ever building the control that removes it. The cell now reads `test_setIncendiaryRegistryRequiresCodeAndTwoStep` first and `::test_boostClampedToLpTrancheFraction` second. **This is the ONLY done-criteria cell PP3.3 edits.** B.1's rewrite was deferred to PP4 rung 12 at PP3.3b, because PP-D14 requires the replacement invariant to be stated there before a name can be chosen honestly.

**F.1's seating control is largely C.8's class fix applied to these two setters, and PP4 must not build them twice.** C.8's fix intent already reads `code.length` or an interface probe on EVERY seating, plus `pool` validated against the Vault — which is precisely what F.1's primary remedy needs at `setIncendiaryRegistry` and `setAuMTContractForPool`. C.8 is the same `emission-stack` redeploy unit and was locked Fix at PP3.3c. The two rows are one body of work at the seating layer, with F.1's clamp as the additional defence-in-depth piece that C.8 does not carry.

**Deferred with cause, and the census is unchanged at 40 unblocked and 13 deferred.**

- **F.2** — waits on **PP-D36**. Its verdict would be Fix under either PP-D36 outcome, since the router exposes `mintFor` as its only function with no pause, rotation, rate limit or per-consumer budget regardless of what the schedule does; only the ceiling's derivation depends on the corrected schedule, and the row's own constraint says F.3 first. Locking it now would buy one census row, would not unblock PP4 because the ordering still holds, and would reverse the census correction ratified at PP3.3b for no gain.
- **F.3** — waits on **PP-D36**, and here the decision is the row's whole content. Under the current PP-D36 text both options are code changes, `BLOCKS_PER_ERA = 10_500_000` or a raised `MAX_SUPPLY`, so Fix is the likely verdict either way; an Accepted-risk outcome would require amending PP-D36 to admit documenting the overshoot rather than closing it.

**One analogy is recorded as imperfect so a later reader does not lean on it.** The C.1 and D.7 deferrals rest on PP-D23 deciding whether a fix is a CODE change or a SPEC change, which is why those rows carry an empty `redeploy unit` cell pending the decision. PP-D36 is not that shape: both of its options are code changes and F.2 and F.3 already carry a committed `token-schedule` unit. Their deferral is sound on ordering and on census stability, not on the code-versus-spec ground that governs C.1 and D.7.

## PP3.3 — Family H verdicts (four locked; H.1 deferred) and PP3.3 CLOSE

Asserting `version()` would NOT catch this substitution: `DeployAureumWeightedPoolFactory.s.sol:39` constructs the upstream `WeightedPoolFactory` with a `FACTORY_VERSION` string naming `AureumWeightedPoolFactory`, and `WeightedPoolFactory` passes that string straight to `Version(factoryVersion)` at its constructor, so the deployed upstream body REPORTS the Aureum name. The deeper defect is structural rather than the assertion itself: the four `test/formal/` files carry 55 `prove_` functions and ZERO `test` functions, so forge discovers none of them and no Part-A count can ever see this failure — only `hevm test --match prove_` can, and it last ran at `9c109b1` on 2026-07-17, three weeks before the widening landed at `5bef424`. Fixing only the assertion would leave that blind spot intact, which is what the fix intent's `.t.sol`-mirror-or-CI clause exists to close. A SECOND staleness must be settled at the same re-anchor: `formal/RESULTS.md` says the suite stands at 52/52 in its headline Status block while its own Result block reports 55/55 re-attested at that same commit.

Two ledger obligations from PP3.3b: **F-15's row needs re-statusing** because B.7 reopened PB-D10's disposition, and a **PB-D10 reopening amendment** in `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_NOTES.md` is a separate cross-stage beat. One register obligation for PP3.5: the recurring **found-once-never-propagated** class, now visible in four rows rather than three — A.3's strict-rise form, B.7's F-21 zero guard, D.2's monopolised-sampling refutation of an in-source acceptance, and H.5's version string — which PP-D11 already names via G10 but which has no RB row.

## PP4.0 — Fix conformance and protected invariants (PP-D12, part 1)

**Every fix in this workstream conforms to the canon, and a fix that makes a finding disappear by weakening a protocol guarantee is not a fix.** The canon is `aummfi-bit/aumm-site`, read from the local clone per `CLAUDE.md` §4; where code and canon disagree the canon is the authority, unless PP-D23 has adjudicated that particular divergence the other way, which is the H family's business and no other rung's. FOUR anti-shapes are named here because each is the natural relief a fix author reaches for when the correct fix is expensive, and each trades a finding for a lesser protocol: adding a setter to an `immutable` slot so that a redeploy can be avoided; converting a structurally fixed destination or authority into a governance-mutable one; lowering a threshold, floor or window so that a boundary case stops failing; and widening an authority so that a caller which legitimately cannot reach something now can. A remediation stage is exactly where this pressure peaks — 51 root causes, several of them expensive — and the audit's whole value evaporates if what emerges is a weaker protocol that merely passes a re-run. Where the correct fix genuinely costs a redeploy, THE REDEPLOY IS THE FIX; the closure table in part 2 prices every one of them so the cost is known in advance rather than discovered mid-rung.

**PROTECTED INVARIANT — the protocol fee destination is der Bodensee, structurally, and no rung in this workstream touches it.** Verified at HEAD rather than assumed. `DER_BODENSEE` is `immutable` at `AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:65` and written once at `:259`; every other occurrence in that file is a read or a guard. `DER_BODENSEE_POOL` is `immutable` at `AureumProtocolFeeController.sol:207`, written once at `:251`. NO setter exists for either anywhere in `src/` — the only `set*` functions on that path are the two pool-creator fee percentages, which the protocol pins at zero forever. The hook and the controller are additionally MUTUALLY PINNED, `FEE_CONTROLLER` `immutable` on the hook and `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` `immutable` on the controller, so neither can be substituted for the other: that pair is an irreducible cycle with no setter to break it, resolved at deploy time by address prediction, which is what `script/DeployFeeRoutingHook.s.sol` reads as `predictedHook` and what PB-D27's phase-A projections computed. The one Vault-layer redirect, `Vault.setProtocolFeeController`, is unreachable by design per PB-D63 (xi) and `AureumVaultFactory.sol` already documents exactly that in its own NatSpec, so F-22's accepted residual is load-bearing here rather than merely unclosed. The dependency graph states the same thing quantitatively: redeploying der Bodensee drags 18 of the 24 contracts in `src/`. THE CYCLE IS THE GUARANTEE, NOT A DEPLOYMENT WART, and it must not be read as an inefficiency the map invites relief from — six queue rows carry `hook` as their redeploy unit, and a fix author pricing that redeploy will reach for a setter on one of these slots, which would convert the fee destination from structural to governance-mutable in a single line. No rung does that. PB-D68 (xi) already paid this price knowingly, refusing to swap a corrected hook under live pools and refreshing an entire Sepolia generation instead.

## PP4.0 — Redeploy closure (PP-D12, part 2)

**The closure below was DERIVED mechanically from `src/` at HEAD, not transcribed, and the method matters because a hand-read map would have been wrong.** Every `immutable` declaration in `src/` was parsed and its target resolved by type, by variable name, or by an explicit alias; entries that resolved to nothing were PRINTED rather than dropped, which is the only reason three real edges were caught — `AureumVaultFactory.INITIAL_FEE_CONTROLLER` is typed `IProtocolFeeController`, which strips to a name no contract carries, `AureumGovernance.SLOT_REGISTRY` is typed `IMiliariumSlotRegistry` for the same reason, and der Bodensee appears under two names a first alias set missed. The final graph is 24 contracts and 46 edges. An edge means the holder pins the target's ADDRESS at construction, so redeploying the target forces redeploying the holder, transitively. Read the table as a price list: the `drags` column is what a rung in that unit must rebuild, and `governance` costing one contract while `token-schedule` costs thirteen is why fix ORDER is not the same question as fix count.

| redeploy unit | root | queue rows | drags | the closure |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `governance` | `AureumGovernance` | 14 | 1 | `AureumGovernanceAuthorizer` |
| `emission-stack` | `EmissionDistributor` | 16 | 4 | `AuMMMinterRouter`, `AureumGovernance`, `AureumGovernanceAuthorizer`, `VotingWeight` |
| `ccb` | `CCBMultiplier` | 5 | 5 | the `emission-stack` set plus `EmissionDistributor` |
| `tvl-oracle` | `TVLOracle` | 1 | 11 | the `ccb` set plus `CCBMultiplier`, `EMASampler`, `EfficiencyOracle`, `GaugeEligibility`, `GaugeRegistry`, `IncendiaryRegistry` |
| `hook` | `AureumFeeRoutingHook` | 6 | 11 | `AuMMMinterRouter`, `AureumFeeRoutingHook` ITSELF, `AureumGovernance`, `AureumGovernanceAuthorizer`, `AureumProtocolFeeController`, `AureumVaultFactory`, `EmissionDistributor`, `GaugeEligibility`, `GaugeRegistry`, `IncendiaryRegistry`, `VotingWeight` |
| `token-schedule` | `AuMM` | 2 | 13 | the `hook` set minus its self-edge, plus `BodenseeBootstrapChannel`, `EfficiencyOracle` and `AuMMMinterRouter` |
| `source-only` | none | 4 | 0 | no contract is deployed; the change is to scripts, tests or docs |
| PROTECTED | der Bodensee pool | n/a | 18 | every contract in `src/` except `AuMM`, `AureumAuthorizer`, `AureumWeightedPoolFactory`, `CompositeRateProvider`, `ERC4626RateProvider` and `MiliariumRegistry` |

## PP4.0 — One-shot setter taxonomy and burn state (PP-D12, part 3)

**The fifteen one-shot setters G12 enumerates are NOT one class, and the difference decides whether a fix may touch them.** Classified at HEAD by asking, of each setter, whether its TARGET holds the HOLDER as an `immutable`. SIX are CYCLE-BREAKERS, where the answer is yes and the pair could not be deployed at all unless one side were seated after construction: `AuMM.setMinter` against `AuMMMinterRouter.AUMM`; `EmissionDistributor.setMintRouter` and `BodenseeBootstrapChannel.setMintRouter` against that same router's `EMISSION_DISTRIBUTOR` and `BOOTSTRAP_CHANNEL`, the router being a three-spoke hub whose three setters exist solely to close it; `GaugeEligibility.setGaugeRegistry` against `GaugeRegistry.gaugeEligibility`; and both `SwapAndDepositToBodensee` setters against `VaultClassRegistry.helper` and `GaugeRegistry.swapAndDeposit`. SEVEN are ORDERING-BREAKERS, where the target holds no reciprocal immutable and the setter exists only because the target is constructed later: the hook's `setIncendiaryModule` and `setEmissionRecorder`, both `CCBMultiplier` setters, `TVLOracle.setMiliariumRegistry` — `MiliariumRegistry` carries ZERO immutables, which is what settles that one and its `CCBMultiplier` sibling — and both `VaultClassRegistry` setters. TWO are NEITHER: `setGovernanceModule` seats a Safe rather than a contract, and `setAuMTContractForPool` is a per-pool binding outside the deploy graph entirely. The consequence for PP4 is asymmetric and must not be flattened: a cycle-breaker CANNOT be removed, since removing it makes its pair undeployable, while an ordering-breaker could in principle be replaced by reordering the deploy, so a fix proposing that is arguable rather than barred. Note also that the two `SwapAndDepositToBodensee` setters carry the codebase's ONLY conditional burn, each releasing `moduleAdmin` only once the other has run, so half-sealed is a reachable state and nothing on chain distinguishes it from sealed — which is C.8's Info instance, not a separate finding.

**The closure table counts `immutable` edges ONLY, and a SPENT one-shot setter is an immutable edge on the deployment that spent it.** This is the one place the table must be read twice. Burn state is per-DEPLOYMENT, not per-source: at Stage R nothing is burned yet and the whole set deploys in dependency order, so the immutable closure is the entire price. On a live generation it is not. `AuMM.setMinter` is already spent on Sepolia generation 3, so redeploying `AuMMMinterRouter` there cannot re-point `AuMM.minter` at the new router and therefore forces `AuMM` itself, and `AuMM` drags thirteen. A unit the table prices at four becomes the most expensive in the protocol, and the same inversion applies to every cycle-breaker whose burn has fired. This is not a defect in the map; it is why PP-D21 retires generation 3 rather than patching it in place, and why the generation-4 refresh is scheduled after the PP6 exit gate rather than interleaved with PP4. A rung that touches a live generation prices its change against the burn state recorded in `docs/STAGE_P_BIS_SEPOLIA_DEPLOYMENT_RECORD.md`; a rung that only changes source for Stage R prices it against the table alone.

## PP4.0 — The per-rung test gate (PP-D12, part 4)

**The per-rung test gate, written once here so that no fix rung restates it.** THREE invocations, split by PATH per D35, because a fork test swept into the unit run fails there and a unit run that reaches the fork tree hangs on the rate limit. PART-A UNIT is `forge test` with `--no-match-path` over the fork tree; its baseline as of PP4.0 is 1060 passed, 0 failed and 1 SKIPPED across 90 suites, and RB-024 requires QUOTING BOTH NUMBERS rather than rounding to green. That skip is `test/unit/DeploymentArtifactAgreement.t.sol:44`, deferred by user adjudication to the generation-4 artifact rebuild, so it is expected to stay skipped through all of PP4 and a rung reporting a clean sweep with no skip has measured something other than this suite. FORK is FILE-SCOPED, one of the 33 files under `test/fork/` per invocation, with `--match-path` naming that file plus `--fork-url mainnet --threads 1 -vv`; never unfiltered, which hangs per D35, and never whole-tree across the fork directory, which fails on D36's sequential face even at one thread. SIZES is `test/unit/BytecodeSize.t.sol` at 4 tests, run by any rung whose contract grows, and PB-D37 forces its named-artifact form because neither compiler profile passes an unscoped size check.

**Before any fork invocation the operator environment is checked BY HAND, and as of PP4.0d RB-023 is REOPENED, so that check must not be assumed to have happened.** What exists: `CLAUDE.md` §11 carries the literal sentinel and states that it runs in the SAME shell that will invoke forge, and `test/fork/ForkEnvGuard.sol` enforces the same thing structurally at `setUp` — but in only SEVEN of the 33 fork files, so 26 of them fail at their first dereference rather than at their guard. What does NOT exist is the runbook half: its marker check still greps the file on disk rather than the resolved value, which passes green on a dirty shell. Treat the sentinel as a required manual step of every fork invocation until RB-023 closes for real. The two-pass `.env` discipline per PB-D59 touches exactly ONE file, `test/fork/PB52SepoliaOracleEvidence.t.sol`, the tree's only Sepolia-fork test: copy the canonical sepolia file onto the working `.env` for it and RETURN the working copy to mainnet afterwards, always canonical to working copy and never the reverse, or every mainnet fixture silently resolves its literals to codeless Sepolia addresses, which is PB-D35 recurring. FIVE obligations per rung, beyond the three invocations: the PP3.2 PoC inverts to a regression in the SAME rung; the ledger row moves to `Fixed` with the commit hash, the queue's `F-NN` cell already naming which row since PP3.5f; the rung's NOTES entry records what the fix DECLINED to do and why; where the rung's redeploy unit drags more than itself, the closure in part 2 is cited in the prompt's `Must match` so the blast radius is stated before the edit rather than discovered after it; and per PP-D43 the rung NAMES the canonical clause its fix conforms to, by file and section, in both the `Must match` and the NOTES entry, STOPPING for adjudication if it cannot name one.

## PP-D43 — Canon conformance is binding (the protected set and the escape path)

**PP-D43 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-26): canon conformance is BINDING, and the protected set is §xxix of `10_constitution.md` adopted WHOLESALE rather than a rival list of our own devising.** That section declares itself the single canonical source for all immutable protocol parameters and states its standard without qualification: immutable from block 0, cannot be changed by any means. It covers the supply cap of 21,000,000, the emission halving schedule and the block emission rates, the canonical time constants including `BLOCKS_PER_ERA` at 10,512,000 whose alias is exactly four times `BLOCKS_PER_YEAR`, the derived time boundaries, and the anti-gaming safeguards it classes beside them. FIVE further invariants are protected outside §xxix and are named here so that no rung has to hunt for them: the fee destination, per PP4.0 part 1; no treasury, at `04_tokenomics.md:163`; no wei of AuMM, svZCHF or sUSDS leaving der Bodensee, at `04_tokenomics.md:213`; AuMM barred from emission-eligible pools, at `10_constitution.md:168`; and the Vault's byte-identity with audited Balancer V3, per `CLAUDE.md` §1. NO rung in this workstream changes any of them. A fix that requires one of them to move is not a fix, it is a constitutional amendment, and it STOPS.

**FIFTH per-rung obligation, extending the part-4 gate, and the single escape path.** Every rung NAMES the canonical clause its fix conforms to, by file and section, whether that is `10_constitution.md` §xxix, a clause of `04_tokenomics.md`, or a formula id from `11_formulas.md`, and it names it twice: in the prompt's `Must match` and again in the rung's NOTES entry. A rung that CANNOT name one STOPS and goes to adjudication rather than proceeding on the fix author's judgement, because the inability to cite is the signal itself and not an inconvenience to be worked around. Canon changes by exactly ONE route, a PP5.4 spec edit, user-adjudicated, landing upstream in `aummfi-bit/aumm-site` before any code depends on it: never inside a fix rung, never as a code accommodation, and never by a rung concluding that the canonical value is the thing that is wrong. Where code and canon disagree the CODE yields, unless PP-D23 has adjudicated that particular divergence the other way, which is the H family's business and no other rung's.

## PP-D44 — F.1 (PP4.1, rung 1): the boost clamp, both setter controls, and the F.1/C.8 seam

**PP-D44 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-26): F.1 is the queue's only Critical and rung 1 of the fix-sequencing order per PP-D35, and it lands as three changes to `src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol` — a consumer-side clamp on the boost leg, and a hardening of BOTH seating setters.** The defect is two-sided. `_settlePool`'s boost leg at `:405-413` reads `boostIntegral` from whatever contract occupies the `incendiaryRegistry` slot and adds the result straight into `poolAccRewardPerLP` at `:410`, outside every gate the allocation leg above it carries; and `setIncendiaryRegistry` at `:227-231` is a three-line body that seats any address into that slot with no `code.length`, no one-shot and no timelock. **Canon citation per PP-D43:** `10_constitution.md` §xxviii at L97, which places Incendiary claims as a skim from the LP emission tranche before the CCB splits the remainder and binds them to the shared 15%-per-epoch cap, and §xxix at L135, the `BOOST_CAP_BPS` row reading 1,500 bps = 15% of an epoch's emission integral. The clamp INVENTS no bound: it makes the distributor enforce an immutable it presently delegates to the very contract the clamp exists to bound.

**Scope: F.1 owns the seating layer on BOTH setters, and that is F.1's own language rather than a courtesy pull-forward from C.8.** The queue's F.1 fix-intent cell already reads `pool` validated against the Vault alongside the clamp, the `code.length` and the two-step, so `setAuMTContractForPool` sits inside F.1's stated remedy rather than being borrowed into it; PP3.3g records the two rows as ONE body of work at the seating layer, and F-67 warns in terms that PP4 must not build them twice. C.8 RETAINS every non-seating face it carries — the `try/catch` on the hook's recorder dispatch so a recorder fault degrades accounting instead of locking LPs, the remaining seatings, the post-condition read-backs after every wiring call, PP-D28's pre-send `eth_call` runbook gate, `renounceOwnership()` on the factory, and the two authorizer `require`s — and its rung is unchanged in every respect but this one. A later reader must not conclude from this lock that C.8 shrank to nothing.

**The clamp.** `boostDue` is bounded by `cap = (BOOST_CAP_BPS * _lpTrancheIntegral(capFrom, block.number)) / 10_000` and clamped DOWN to it, where `boostFrom = poolBoostCursor[pool] + 1` reaches `boostIntegral` UNFLOORED and `capFrom` is that same value floored at `GENESIS_BLOCK + 1`, computed only when a boost is due. The floor is required rather than defensive, and PP4.1c3 CORRECTED the mechanism first named here: `_lpTrancheIntegral` is documented for `from >= GENESIS_BLOCK + 1`, and an unbound pool whose cursor still reads zero would otherwise walk from block 1 into `_bootstrapApSum`'s `from - anchorStart` subtraction, which underflows for any `from < GENESIS_BLOCK`. `AureumTime.eraIndex` is NOT that site — it guards pre-genesis and returns era 0 — so a reader checking it alone would conclude the floor was unnecessary; the conclusion held while the mechanism recorded beside it did not, which is PB22's shape. AMENDED at PP4.1c6, and this is the second falsified mechanism in one rung. The paragraph originally floored a SHARED `from` feeding BOTH calls, and claimed that raising the bound could not change honest delivery. That was verified for the RETURN VALUE — `boostIntegral` resolves both 1 and `GENESIS_BLOCK + 1` to epoch 0 through the same pre-genesis guard in `AureumTime.epochIndex`, and epoch 0 can carry no bucket because `buyBoost` reverts until year 1 ends, around epoch 26 — and then asserted as BEHAVIOUR, which is false. The CALLDATA changed, and anything keyed on `from` changed with it: four `BoostDelivery` tests reverted because their `vm.mockCall` is keyed on `(pool, 1, N)` while the call began arriving as `(pool, GENESIS_BLOCK + 1, N)`, hitting a codeless address that returns no data. The floor now applies to `capFrom` alone, `boostIntegral` never needed it, and the bound is unchanged. PB22 twice over in the same rung: right conclusion, wrong mechanism, over-broad claim. Pre-genesis emission is zero, so the floor changes no honest value. The literal is DUPLICATED on the distributor as its own `internal constant BOOST_CAP_BPS = 1_500` and is deliberately NOT read from `IncendiaryRegistry.sol:50`, because reading the cap from the contract being bounded reintroduces exactly the trust the clamp removes; `:459` carries the same plain-integer BPS arithmetic per L-D4 / L-D20, so the two sides agree by construction rather than by call. Excess is DISCARDED and never banked — `poolBoostCursor[pool]` still advances unconditionally to `block.number` at `:413` — and that is also what bounds E.8's backlog face, since a rebind after an unbound window is then measured against the tranche integral over that same window rather than billed as one lump.

**The two-step, which is a NEW shape and not a mirror.** `setIncendiaryRegistry` splits into `proposeIncendiaryRegistry(newRegistry)` and `acceptIncendiaryRegistry()`, both `onlyGovernance`: propose stores the pending address together with `block.number`, accept requires a strictly later block and commits, emitting the existing `IncendiaryRegistrySet(old, new)` so the event surface is unchanged. `code.length > 0` is required on a NON-ZERO proposal only, which preserves the H-D29 zero deprecation valve; the zero write routes through the same two steps with NO fast path — a single-step zero is itself a lever that strands boost delivery protocol-wide, and PP-D41 bars a disposition resting on the holder declining to pull it. There is NO propose/accept rebind precedent anywhere in `src/`, so this shape is locked here rather than mirrored from something: `VaultClassRegistry`'s propose/veto/finalize is a different animal — a permissionless admission carrying a veto window rather than a two-party rebind — and is NOT the model. The gap is ONE BLOCK and no more, because a longer delay would introduce a constant §xxix has no row for while one block already buys the entire property: the seat-and-drain sequence cannot be atomic, so the PoC's four calls become two transactions in two blocks with the pending registry publicly readable in between.

**Pool validation, and the one structural cost this rung pays.** `setAuMTContractForPool` at `:215-219` validates `newAuMTContract` against zero and against a prior binding while leaving `pool` ENTIRELY unvalidated, which is precisely what lets the F.1 PoC seat the attacker as recorder for a fabricated pool. It gains `IVault(vault).isPoolRegistered(pool)` reverting `PoolNotRegistered(pool)`, plus `code.length > 0` on the recorder per C.8's every-seating rule. The shape mirrors `GaugeEligibility.sol:39` literally — an `address public immutable vault` read as `IVault(vault)` at the call site — and `isPoolRegistered` is real Vault API rather than an invented membership probe: it is declared at `IVaultExtension.sol:127` and reaches `IVault` through the inheritance list at `IVault.sol:13`, VERIFIED at HEAD rather than assumed, per G10. The cost is MEASURED and not estimated. The constructor goes from eight arguments to nine with a `ZeroAddress` guard on the new one, and the ripple is NINETEEN call sites across ELEVEN files — `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol` alone constructs it nine times, and `test/formal/EmissionProofs.sol` must be updated to COMPILE even though forge runs none of its `prove_` functions. Every site that also calls `setAuMTContractForPool` needs a Vault answering `isPoolRegistered` for its pool, which is a harness addition rather than merely an extra argument, and that is the real weight of this rung. It is a MAP AMENDMENT and not a new unit: it adds an EmissionDistributor-to-Vault edge to the PP4.0 part-2 closure, the Vault sits in PP-D12's SURVIVING set, so the `emission-stack` price of four is unchanged and no cascade widens. Recorded here so the closure is not read as stale at PP4.52.

**The done-criteria gap this rung closes, and why it closes by ADDITION rather than by rewrite.** F.1's binding done-criteria names exactly two cases, `test/whitehat/P1_F1.t.sol::test_setIncendiaryRegistryRequiresCodeAndTwoStep` and `::test_boostClampedToLpTrancheFraction`, the first primary and the second retained as defence in depth — that ordering being PP3.3g's inversion discharging PP-D41's F.1 obligation. NEITHER names the pool-validation face, which this rung builds, and PP-D40 (vi) fixes done-criteria as `path::case` precisely so PP4.52 reads a green run rather than a judgment: a face built with no artifact pointing at it is a face PP4.52 cannot see. F.1's done-criteria therefore gains a THIRD case, `::test_setAuMTContractForPoolRequiresRegisteredPool`, APPENDED to the two existing names rather than replacing either, which makes this the second done-criteria edit of the stage after PP3.3g's inversion and the only one that rewrites nothing. The alternative was considered and REJECTED: leaving the face unnamed and letting C.8's own PoC invert finish the claim would make `Fixed` for C.8's `pool` face rest on a change that landed in a different rung with no artifact naming it, which is the stale-attestation shape PP-D37 keeps the formal bundle out of and PP3.2 found live in H.4.

**What this rung DECLINES, each with its reason, per the part-4 gate's third obligation.** The C.8 REBIND face, so `setAuMTContractForPool` stays one-shot per pool: rebinding is a topology decision rather than a validation one, I-D9 having removed it deliberately with its NatSpec at `:211` recording that an asymmetric rebind between the hook's `auMTByPool` and the distributor's `auMTContractByPool` would break per-pool topology — validating `pool` and making the binding reversible are separable, and only the first is F.1's. A LONGER TIMELOCK on the rebind, for the non-canonical-constant reason above. A FAST ZERO-VALVE on the deprecation path, for the lever reason above. READING `BOOST_CAP_BPS` from the registry instead of duplicating the literal, which would reintroduce trust in the contract being bounded. ROTATING `EmissionDistributor.governance` to `AureumGovernance`, which F-67 rejects in terms because it strands three setters while the consumer clamp holds under EITHER dissolution outcome — and that is exactly why PP-D35 orders this rung BEFORE PP5.1 decides the dissolution question rather than after, so the choice is made on its merits and not as a Critical's remedy. And every remaining C.8 face enumerated above, which is C.8's rung and not this one.
