**AMENDMENT at the PP4.1c pre-flight, 2026-08-26 — the cost paragraph above priced the CONSTRUCTOR ripple only, and the setter split carries a second one.** The nineteen call sites across eleven files are the `new EmissionDistributor(` sites and stay correct as far as they reach, but `setIncendiaryRegistry` becoming two-step moves every CALLER of it as well: TWENTY-SIX call sites across SIX files, measured at HEAD — fourteen in `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol`, five in `test/formal/EmissionProofs.sol`, four in `test/whitehat/P1_E8.t.sol`, and one each in `script/DeployStageL.s.sol`, `test/fork/StageLIntegration.t.sol` and `test/whitehat/P1_F1.t.sol`, the `DeployStageL` site being the PRODUCTION path rather than a fixture. Neither set overlaps the other, since a constructor call site is not a setter call site, so PP4.1c prices at FORTY-FIVE call sites across FOURTEEN distinct files rather than the nineteen recorded above. The two ripples land in one rung by adjudication at this pre-flight rather than by oversight; the alternatives, dropping the two-step or deferring it behind the constructor ripple, were both put and both declined. TWO formal proofs additionally go SEMANTICALLY false rather than merely failing to compile, and both are rewritten in the SAME rung so that neither sits false through PP-D37's no-rerun window: `prove_distributor_setIncendiaryRegistry_gateAndClear` at `test/formal/EmissionProofs.sol:423` and `prove_distributor_governanceRotation` at `:400` each call the setter once and assert the slot committed, which a two-step makes untrue. Leaving them would reproduce H.4 exactly — a proof false by inspection surviving because forge runs none of the bundle's `prove_` functions and no Part-A count can ever see it — which is among the shapes this stage exists to close.

**SECOND AMENDMENT at PP4.1c4, 2026-08-26 — the first amendment was itself measured too narrowly, by a factor of about 2.3, and the reason is instructive.** Both figures above came from grepping `new EmissionDistributor(` and the callers of `setIncendiaryRegistry`, and NEITHER pattern can see what actually breaks. `EmissionDistributorHarness` at `test/unit/harness/EmissionDistributorHarness.sol` SUBCLASSES the distributor and forwards eight arguments through a base-constructor invocation, which solc reports as error 2973 rather than 6160; that line is invisible to a `new` grep, and repairing it changes the harness's OWN signature, dragging its SIXTEEN construction sites with it. The constructor ripple is therefore THIRTY-FIVE call sites across TWENTY-SIX files plus the harness declaration, not nineteen across eleven. Separately and unpriced anywhere above, gating `setAuMTContractForPool` on `isPoolRegistered` breaks every caller that passes a placeholder vault: FORTY-ONE call sites across TWENTY-TWO files, and NO unit-level Vault mock exists in `test/` to answer that call, exactly one fork file mentioning the function at all, so one must be written before any of them can pass. PP4.1c prices at roughly ONE HUNDRED AND FOUR edit points across about thirty files. The user re-adjudicated at PP4.1c4 against the corrected figure and elected to PROCEED AS LOCKED, across six sub-rungs c5 through c10, two of which are deliberate red windows each closed by the rung immediately following. The lesson is PB18's one level up: a count is a prediction until the thing counted is the thing that breaks, and `new X(` counts CONSTRUCTIONS while what breaks is CONSTRUCTOR ARITY, which inheritance propagates and no call-site grep can follow.

**THIRD AMENDMENT at PP4.1c11, 2026-08-27 — the two guards on `setAuMTContractForPool` are SPLIT, and only the pool check lands inside F.1.** The pool-validation paragraph above names both `IVault(vault).isPoolRegistered(pool)` and `code.length > 0` on the recorder. They have different owners and wildly different costs, measured at HEAD rather than assumed. The POOL check is F.1's own remedy and the face its PoC exploits, and it ripples to NOTHING: `MockRegisteredVault` answers true for every pool by default, which is precisely why it was built that way at PP4.1c5a, so all FORTY-ONE `setAuMTContractForPool` call sites keep passing untouched. The RECORDER check is C.8's class fix, its row reading `code.length` or an interface probe on EVERY seating, and it would break roughly TWENTY-FIVE to THIRTY of those forty-one: `AUMT_REC` is `address(0xA0DC)`, `AUMT_REVOKED` is `address(0xAB01)`, and every short local is a `makeAddr`, none of which carries code, so each site would need a deployed stub or a `vm.etch`. It therefore rides with C.8, where that fixture work is paid ONCE across all fifteen seatings instead of twice. **The second reason is sharper than the cost.** F.1's inverted regression binds `ATTACKER`, a plain address, as the recorder in step 1, and depends on that call SUCCEEDING so the sequence reaches the clamp it exists to pin. A recorder `code.length` guard would make step 1 revert, the exploit would stop at seating, and the clamp regression would silently lose its subject while still reading green — a test that passes for a reason it does not name is the shape PP4.1c8 split into two phases to avoid.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.1c25, 2026-08-27 — F.1 is BUILT and F-67 reads Fixed at `daa293e`.** All three remedies are green: the clamp (`7d79cd2`, floor narrowed at `380940f`), the `isPoolRegistered` gate (`f4d2442`), and the two-step (`f9b43c5`). **Canon per PP-D43:** `10_constitution.md` §xxviii L97 and §xxix L135 — the two-step introduces NO constant, the gap being the bare strict block inequality, precisely because §xxix carries no delay row to cite. Gates: Part-A 1063 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped of 1064 across 90 suites, the skip being RB-024's deferral; `StageLIntegration` 2/2 and `DeployStageL` 3/3 file-scoped on a mainnet fork with the RB-023 sentinel reading 0 in the same shell. The ripple measured TWENTY-SEVEN real call sites across seven files, not the twenty-six the first amendment predicted — `test/whitehat/P1_F1.t.sol` carried two, not one, and `test/formal/EmissionProofs.sol` carried five where a `.setIncendiaryRegistry(` grep saw three, the two invisible ones being `abi.encodeCall` function-POINTER references, which take a comma rather than a paren. That is the first amendment's own lesson recurring inside the rung that was written to apply it.

**What this rung DECLINED, beyond the lock's four.** A local binding helper in `test/whitehat/P1_E8.t.sol`: four sites in three shapes, two needing bespoke absolute block targets, so each stays explicit. Modelling the two-step as Act transitions: both halves read the environment and propose queries `code.length`, so they route to a residual per `formal/act/ema_sampler.act` L41—L60's convention for `updateEMA`, and Act is source-only upstream and NOT installed, so any invented construct would be unfalsifiable here. Renaming F.1's done-criteria case despite its name carrying the retired symbol: PP-D40 (vi) binds case names at creation. And re-running the formal bundle, barred by PP-D37 — which is why `formal/RESULTS.md` gains `PENDING-REATTESTATION` to the PB-D17(iv) vocabulary and marks P-ED3 and P-ED4 with it rather than carrying `PROVED` forward from `9c109b1`, that being H.4's exact shape.

**THREE consequences the lock did not anticipate, each a fixture or artifact cost rather than a design change.** FIRST, the registry `code.length` gate collides with the unit suite exactly as the third amendment predicted for the RECORDER check — every registry bound in `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol` is a bare `address(0xBEEF)` with no code — but this gate is F.1's OWN remedy and cannot ride to C.8, so `_bindIncendiaryRegistry` etches a byte at non-zero targets and the whole file routes through it. SECOND, `forge script` executes an entry at a single `block.number`, so propose and accept CANNOT share one invocation: `script/DeployStageL.s.sol` splits into `run()` which proposes and a separate `runAcceptRegistry()` / `acceptRegistry(address)` pair, making the Stage-L bind a two-invocation operator procedure. This is the fourth instance of the simulation-versus-broadcast class after PB-D31, PB-D46 and PB-D48. THIRD, the constructor ripple at c5—c10 MISSED `formal/act/emission_distributor.act`, whose constructor still described the eight-parameter form after `0a95429` made it nine; a ripple measured over Solidity construction sites cannot see a spec that describes the same constructor in another language.

**And one measurement failure of my own, which is PP12 rather than PB18.** Every sweep written during this rung scoped to `src/ script/ test/`, so `formal/` sat OUTSIDE the searched universe and the c20i sweep reported the symbol gone when two live references remained — the `.act` transition and its `RESULTS.md` coverage row. It was caught only because the c23a sweep happened to widen the directory list. The pattern was right and the tool ran; the file set was wrong, which is exactly PP12's shape and not PB18's. A sweep that certifies a symbol's removal names EVERY tracked directory, or reports which ones it searched.

## PP-D45 — PP4.2 (rung 2): the residual delta, A.3's strict rise, and the channel carve-out

**PP-D45 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-27): PP4.2 is PP-D11's one-shape rung — A.2, A.3 and E.1 — and it lands as FOUR edits across TWO contracts, not three, because A.2 carries two identical sites.** The defects are two shapes, not one, and PP-D11's phrase "one shape" governs only the first. THE RESIDUAL SHAPE, at `src/gauge/SwapAndDepositToBodensee.sol` in both `swapAndDeposit` and `donate`, and at `src/emission/BodenseeBootstrapChannel.sol` in `distribute()`: an ABSOLUTE `balanceOf(address(this)) != 0` check reverting `HelperBalanceNonZero`, which any pre-existing wei bricks permanently. Each becomes an ENTRY-SNAPSHOT DELTA — read the balance before the `_vault.unlock` and revert only if the post balance EXCEEDS it — so a residual that was already there is tolerated while a residual the call itself creates still reverts. **E.1's snapshot is taken BEFORE `mintRouter.mintFor`**, not after, because the mint transiently adds `amount` to the very balance under test; a post-mint snapshot would measure nothing. THE RISE SHAPE, at `SwapAndDepositToBodensee._swapAndDepositCallback` only: exact reserve equality `postReserve != preReserve + amount` becomes `postReserve <= preReserve` reverting a NEW `ReserveDidNotRise(uint256 preReserve, uint256 postReserve)`, byte-mirroring `src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:641-643`, which PB-D68 (xvii) already got right and never backported — the found-once-never-propagated class PP-D11 names via G10. **Canon per PP-D43:** `04_tokenomics.md:213`, no wei of AuMM, svZCHF or sUSDS leaving der Bodensee — every one of these four edits keeps the conservation assertion that guarantees it and only widens the tolerance to what the Vault actually does, so no rung here weakens the guarantee it protects.

**THE CARVE-OUT, and it is the single thing this rung is most likely to get wrong.** `src/emission/BodenseeBootstrapChannel.sol:308` carries the IDENTICAL exact-delta assertion A.3 condemns, `postReserve != preReserve + amount` reverting `ReserveDeltaMismatch`, and it is CORRECT THERE AND IS NOT TOUCHED. The channel donates AuMM: a STANDARD token at unit rate with no rate provider, so the Vault's DONATION branch performs no scaled18 round-trip on it and `PoolDataLib` debits it no yield fee. A.3 is scoped by the plan to 18-decimal WITH_RATE rails for exactly that reason, which is why PB-D68 (xvii) was a hook-only correction in the first place. **This is PB19 running in reverse.** PB19 records the cost of copying the AuMM-valid exact form ONTO rails; the error available here is copying the rails fix BACK onto AuMM, and PP-D11's "one shape" phrasing invites it. The residual fix touches the channel; the rise fix does not. A rung that changes `:308` has broken a correct assertion and weakened a conservation guarantee to close a defect that is not there.

**TWO done-criteria cells are CORRECTED, and this is the adjudicated half of the lock.** A.2's cell was created correctly as the invert, `test/fork/SwapAndDepositToBodensee.t.sol::test_P1_A2_strayWeiDoesNotBrickEitherRail`. A.3's and E.1's were created naming their own reproductions verbatim — `::test_P1_A3_belowParRateBricksDonate` and `test/fork/StageHIntegration.t.sol::test_P1_E1_strayWeiBricksDistribute` — so both name a case asserting the defect is LIVE and neither can serve as a fix gate: PP4.52 would read a green run of a test whose name claims the brick still bites. PP-D42 fixes the cell as the reproduction-versus-fix INVERT and cites A.2's own `Bricks` to `DoesNotBrick` as the exemplar, so these two are MALFORMED AT CREATION rather than binding as written. They become `::test_P1_A3_belowParRateDoesNotBrickDonate` and `::test_P1_E1_strayWeiDoesNotBrickDistribute`, PATHS UNCHANGED since both reproductions already landed on their D35-correct fork fixtures. This is the SECOND done-criteria edit of the stage after F.1's inversion at PP3.3g, and it follows that precedent deliberately: the correction is adjudicated in its own beat BEFORE the code rung, so no fix shape is baked in while its gate is still wrong.

**What this rung DECLINES.** The permissionless `flush(token)` hard-wired to der Bodensee that A.2's fix-intent offers as an alternative: the delta assertion alone closes the brick, and a new permissionless external entry on the donation helper is a surface expansion this rung has no reproduction for. Dropping `:276-277` entirely, which E.1's fix-intent offers: `BptMintedOnDonation` and the reserve check do prove the tranche landed, but the residual check additionally proves the helper consumed what it pulled, and a delta keeps that proof while removing the brick. And retiring `ReserveDeltaMismatch` from `SwapAndDepositToBodensee`: it goes unused there once A.3 lands, but the channel still declares and uses its own, and deleting an error from one contract while its twin survives in the other is churn this rung does not need.

**FIRST AMENDMENT at PP4.2c, 2026-08-27, before any code landed — the residual delta needs DIFFERENT arithmetic in the two contracts, and the form locked above is VACUOUS for A.2.** The lock says to read the balance before the `_vault.unlock` and revert if the post balance exceeds it. That is correct for E.1 and wrong for A.2, because the two contracts obtain `amount` by opposite routes, VERIFIED at HEAD rather than assumed. `SwapAndDepositToBodensee` has NO `transferFrom` anywhere: callers PUSH the tokens first and then invoke, `GaugeRegistry.sol:145-146` doing `safeTransfer(swapAndDeposit, ANTI_SPAM_FEE)` immediately before `swapAndDeposit(...)`, and `AureumGovernance.sol:213-214` doing `safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(BODENSEE_CHANNEL), amount)` immediately before `donate(...)`. So the entry balance is already `residual + amount`, the post balance is `residual`, and `residual > residual + amount` is NEVER true: the assertion would compile, would survive the reproduction's inversion, and would assert NOTHING. `BodenseeBootstrapChannel` is the opposite: it MINTS to itself via `mintRouter.mintFor(address(this), amount)` inside `distribute()`, so a snapshot taken before that mint is genuinely the pre-existing residual and the locked comparison holds exactly as written.

**The corrected A.2 form, and the error's argument.** Snapshot `entryBalance` at the top of each entry, after the `payToken` validation — `payToken` is a validated parameter and constant within the frame, so the snapshot and the tail check read the same token. At the tail compute `expectedResidual = entryBalance - amount` and revert `HelperBalanceNonZero` when the post balance EXCEEDS that. The subtraction cannot underflow on any reachable path: the callback's `payToken.safeTransfer(address(_vault), amount)` requires the helper to hold at least `amount`, nothing adds to the balance between the snapshot and that transfer, and a callback revert makes the tail unreachable — and under solc 0.8 an impossible underflow would revert loudly rather than wrap. **The error's ARGUMENT changes from the total residual to the EXCESS**, `residual - expectedResidual`, which is the only figure that is non-zero exactly when the error fires; reporting the total would leave an operator unable to tell a tolerated pre-existing residual from an excess the call created, and reporting the excess makes the existing name `HelperBalanceNonZero` read correctly rather than approximately. E.1 keeps the plain `residual` argument, because there the tolerated baseline is whatever predates the mint and the residual IS the excess whenever the two differ.

**Why this is recorded as an amendment rather than silently built.** The lock was written from the defect's shape and not from the callers' behaviour, and one read of two call sites falsified it. PP-D44 took three amendments for the same reason, each after the compiler or the suite contradicted something already recorded as settled; this one lands BEFORE the code rung instead of after it, which is the only difference and the one worth keeping. A fix author reading only the residual-shape paragraph above would have written the vacuous form in both contracts, and no test in the tree would have caught it — the reproduction inverts to a SUCCESS assertion, and a vacuous guard passes a success assertion perfectly.

**SECOND AMENDMENT at PP4.2g, 2026-08-27 — A.3's fix has TWO clauses and the lock carried only one; the fork gate caught it.** `test_P1_A3_belowParRateDoesNotBrickDonate` failed `BalanceNotSettled()` at the first PP4.2 fork run, four of five passing. The queue's A.3 fix-intent reads "`if (postReserve <= preReserve) revert ReserveDidNotRise`; SETTLE THE VAULT'S RETURNED `amountsIn` AFTER THE ADD", and PP-D45 above transcribed only the first clause. The helper still transferred and settled the OFFERED `amount` BEFORE the add, so once a below-par rate makes the DONATION consume less than offered, the helper holds unsettled credit and the unlock cannot close. This is PB-D68 **(xix)**, the hook's SECOND correction, sitting five lines below the `:641-643` the lock cites as its template and present in the pre-flight output that was read before drafting. The strict rise alone does not fix A.3; it converts a `ReserveDeltaMismatch` brick into a `BalanceNotSettled` brick.

**Closing it CONFLICTS with A.2's guard, and the conflict is structural rather than incidental.** With settle-after, the helper transfers only `amountsIn[idx]`, so the unconsumed rounding remainder STAYS on the helper as dust — the hook's own NatSpec says so in terms and relies on its next route sweeping it, a sweep this helper does not have. The residual then reads `stray + dust` while A.2's baseline `entryBalance - amount` reads `stray`, so the guard built at PP4.2d fires on the dust the A.3 fix necessarily creates. Every alternative shape was traced and leaves the same dust: donating `balanceOf(this)` rounds the larger amount and strands the same remainder, and `sendTo`-ing the excess credit back returns it to the helper by definition. **RESOLVED (user-adjudicated): thread the ACTUAL consumed amount back.** A FIFTH transient slot carries `amountsIn[idx]` from the callback to the entry, and the baseline becomes `entryBalance - consumed` rather than `entryBalance - amount`. The conservation proof survives exactly, with no tolerance figure and therefore no PP-D43 exposure; the two REJECTED alternatives were dropping the residual assertion outright, which is the option the plan offers for the sibling E.1 and which retires a conservation assertion rather than repairing it, and bounding the dust with a slack constant, which invents a number no canonical clause supports.

**The comparison stays `>` on the difference rather than `!=`, and that is deliberate.** With the baseline now `entryBalance - consumed`, the below-baseline direction is structurally unreachable: the settle transfer is the callback's only outflow of `payToken` and it moves exactly `consumed`, so a residual BELOW the baseline cannot arise. `>` is therefore exact in effect while keeping `HelperBalanceNonZero`'s argument meaningful as the excess that should not be there, where `!=` would force the argument to a bare total an operator cannot interpret without the expected value beside it. **One test consequence, recorded so the rung does not read as a regression:** `test_P1_A3_belowParRateDoesNotBrickDonate` asserts a zero helper residue, which was correct under settle-before and is wrong under settle-after — the below-par case leaves exactly the rounding dust it exists to demonstrate, so that assertion moves from zero to the dust the strict rise now tolerates.

**THIRD AMENDMENT at PP4.2h, 2026-08-27, before any code landed — the threading mechanism changes from a fifth transient slot to `unlock`'s own return channel, which this codebase already uses.** `AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` has THREE call sites (`:684`, `:727`, `:773`) that capture `bytes memory result = _vault.unlock(...)` and decode it, and `AureumProtocolFeeController.sol:269` carries an explicit comment marking a site where the return is deliberately unused — VERIFIED at HEAD, this codebase already has a live convention rather than an invented one. The exact shape, from `_routeYieldFeeUnlocked` at `AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:704`: the callback declares `external onlyVault returns (uint256 bptMinted)` and returns the value directly, solc handling the ABI encoding; the caller does `bptMinted = abi.decode(result, (uint256))`. `_swapAndDepositCallback` adopts the identical shape: `returns (uint256 consumed)`, assigning `consumed = amountsIn[idx]` in place of the current bare `.safeTransfer`/`.settle` pair's silent discard, and both `swapAndDeposit` and `donate` capture `bytes memory result = _vault.unlock(...)` then `uint256 consumed = abi.decode(result, (uint256))` immediately after.

**Why this is strictly better than the slot, stated so a later reader does not have to re-derive it.** The four EXISTING transient slots in this file are all written by the ENTRY before `unlock` and cleared by the entry after — a single direction. A fifth slot for this purpose would run the OPPOSITE direction, written by the callback during the call and read by the entry after, which is a second write/read protocol in the same file for one single value with no reuse. The return channel needs no new slot declaration, no reversed-direction write-then-clear pairing to get subtly wrong, and cannot go stale, because it is scoped to the single `unlock` call rather than to a slot that persists across the whole transaction. `D22` in `CLAUDE.md`'s engineering-lesson index already names the risk on the DECODE side — inner `returns (uint256)`, not encoded `bytes` — which this rung now has three real, already-correct examples of in the sibling contract to check against rather than getting right from first principles.

**What does NOT change.** The RESOLUTION locked at the second amendment is untouched: `expectedResidual = entryBalance - consumed`, the comparison stays `>` on the difference for the reason already stated there, and the below-baseline direction remains structurally unreachable. Only the PLUMBING that gets `consumed` from the callback to the entry moves.

## PP-D46 — the DeployStageP accept entry, and the PP4.1 fork-gate gap that surfaced it

**PP-D46 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-27): PP4.1 was closed with an INCOMPLETE fork gate, and two fixtures were left red by its own changes.** The gap was found while running PP4.2's wider gate, not PP4.1's. `test/fork/DissolutionGovernanceHandoff.t.sol` fails 2 of 9 with `PoolNotRegistered`, the error F.1 ADDED at `f4d2442`, because both `test_P1_C2_*` cases bind a recorder for a `makeAddr` pool that the real Vault has never registered — precisely what the new gate exists to reject. `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol` fails 1 of 23 at `test_wires_stageHandoffsComplete`, whose fourth assertion reads the L-D25 boost leg and finds zero, because `DeployStageL.run()` only PROPOSES as of `325c1f9` and `DeployStageP` composes it. PP4.1's gate ran Part-A, `StageLIntegration` and `DeployStageL`; neither failing fixture was in it, though both exercise contracts F.1 changed. **The lesson is the gate's scope rule and it generalises past this rung: a fix rung's fork gate covers every fixture that EXERCISES the changed contract, not merely the fixtures the rung EDITED.** The four files PP4.1 touched under `test/` were the four it thought about; the two it broke were the two it never listed. F-67's `Fixed at PP4.1` stands — the remedy is correct and its done-criteria are green — but the closure was attested on a gate that had not been run to completion, and PP4.2 pays the difference.

**The production gap is real and separate from the stale tests.** `DeployStageP.run()` no longer completes the Stage-L wiring: it arms the incendiary registry and stops, so a spine that returns successfully leaves the protocol with `incendiaryRegistry` at zero and the L-D25 boost leg dead. `_assertPostConditions()` does not check that binding, so nothing fails loudly. **RESOLVED: `DeployStageP` gains a second entry** forwarding to `DeployStageL.runAcceptRegistry()`, invoked as a second `forge script` run at a later block, mirroring the split `DeployStageL` already carries. Two rejected alternatives, each for a stated reason. A RUNBOOK-ONLY step was rejected because the orchestrator would still produce an incompletely-wired protocol with only prose between that and mainnet, the shape RB-025 and PP-D28 exist to harden against. And adding the incendiary binding to `_assertPostConditions()` was rejected AS STATED, because that function runs at the END of `run()` — asserting the binding there would make the production spine fail BY DESIGN, since the registry is legitimately unbound at that instant. The assertion moves to the ACCEPT entry instead, which is the only point at which it is true, so option 3's intent survives at the one place it can hold.

**The two stale fixtures, and what their fixes must NOT do.** The `StagePIntegration` fixture drives the accept explicitly rather than having it buried inside `DeployStageL.deploy()`: PP4.1c20a considered and rejected a `vm.roll` inside that testable entry, on the ground that it would make a fork test assert a binding which in production requires a separate operator action, and that reasoning is unchanged. The two `test_P1_C2_*` cases move from `makeAddr` pools to pools the Vault actually knows, preserving the C.2 mechanism they exist to demonstrate — that after dissolution the bind path works only under prank and no proposal type can emit it — WITHOUT weakening it into a test of F.1's gate, which is a different finding with its own row. C.2's verdict is DEFERRED under PP-D22, so its reproductions stay reproductions and neither is inverted here.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.2z, 2026-08-27 — PP4.2 is BUILT and F-25, F-26 and F-56 all read `Fixed at PP4.2`.** Commits: A.2 and A.3 at `1216b10`, `9979185`, `427f75f` with tests at `025f3f5`, `5fd3f15`, `a30011a` and `8b3c7f4`; E.1 at `d97432f` with tests at `627cf88` and `57dc871`; the PP-D46 fallout at `b0ef486`, `5eef63d`, `a50f9b5`, `e4db5d3`, `3f42c53` and `59a4b00`; the ledger at `517cfd9` and `61feb37`. **Canon per PP-D43:** `04_tokenomics.md:213`, no wei of AuMM, svZCHF or sUSDS leaving der Bodensee — every edit KEPT its conservation assertion and only widened the tolerance to what the Vault actually does, and the A.3 done-criteria now pins that widening as an identity rather than an inequality: reserve rise plus stranded dust equals the offered amount, so nothing is lost. Gates: Part-A **1064 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped of 1065** across 90 suites, the skip being RB-024's deferral and the total being 1065 rather than 1064 because PP4.1c21 added a case to `P1_F1.t.sol` after the last full run; and eight fork files file-scoped with the RB-023 sentinel reading 0 in the same shell — `SwapAndDepositToBodensee` 5/5, `StageHIntegration` 13/13, `StageGIntegration` 40/40, `DeployStageG` 1/1, `DeployStageK` 6/6, `StagePIntegration` 23/23, `DissolutionGovernanceHandoff` 9/9, and `StagePRunRehearsal` **10/10, up from 9** with the accept coverage this rung added.

**FOUR results not recoverable from the diffs.** FIRST, the locked delta was VACUOUS and only a source read caught it: `SwapAndDepositToBodensee` has NO `transferFrom`, callers PUSH before invoking, so the entry balance already contains `amount` and the locked `post > entry` comparison could never fire. It would have compiled, survived the reproduction's inversion, and asserted nothing — a vacuous guard passes a success assertion perfectly, so no test in the tree would have caught it. SECOND, A.3 has TWO clauses and the strict rise alone converts one brick into another: settling the OFFERED amount before the add leaves the unlock over-credited once a below-par rate makes the DONATION consume less, and the fork gate surfaced it as `BalanceNotSettled`. THIRD, closing that conflicts with A.2's guard STRUCTURALLY — settle-after strands the rounding remainder as dust, which the `entryBalance - amount` baseline rejects — and every alternative shape traced leaves the same dust, so the resolution had to be threading the ACTUAL consumed amount back, which `unlock`'s own return channel already carries in three sibling call sites. FOURTH, the CARVE-OUT held and its evidence is a passing test: `BodenseeBootstrapChannel.sol:308` keeps the exact-delta assertion A.3 condemns, correctly, because the channel donates AuMM at unit rate with no rate provider, and `test_Distribute_RevertWhen_ReserveDeltaMismatch` still passes untouched.

**What this rung DECLINED, beyond the lock's four, and one process cost worth naming.** Retiring `ReserveDeltaMismatch` from `SwapAndDepositToBodensee`, which is now unused there but whose twin is live in the channel; a permissionless `flush(token)`; and dropping the residual assertion outright, the option the plan offers for E.1, which would have retired a conservation assertion rather than repairing it. **The process cost: THREE stale unit tests and TWO stale fork fixtures were found by gates rather than by sweeps.** PP4.2f found `testReserveDeltaMismatchUnderMockedReserves` by sweeping `test/fork/` for the changed error and never ran the same sweep over `test/unit/`, where two more sat; and PP-D46 records the two fork fixtures PP4.1 never listed. Both are the same omission at different scopes — the sweep that finds a stale expectation must cover every directory that references the changed symbol, and the gate that proves a fix must cover every fixture that exercises the changed contract. Neither is a new rule; both were available and neither was applied until a red run forced it.

## PP-D47 — E.6 (PP4.5, rung 5): the pending window, the era-bounded catch-up, and the forfeiture's end

**PP-D47 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-27): E.6 lands as shape (b) of its fix intent — the bounded-chunk settle — as TWO coupled edits inside `EmissionDistributor._accrueGlobal` and NOTHING else; the registry, its interface's behaviour, and every mock stay untouched.** EDIT ONE, the guard: the empty-interval short-circuit tightens from `block.number == last` to `block.number <= last`, making the cursor monotone by construction — a pre-genesis touch (PB-D19 seeds `lastAccrualBlock` at a FUTURE `GENESIS_BLOCK`) now no-ops without dragging the anchor below genesis, so the below-genesis snap that `test/unit/PreGenesisAccrual.t.sol:99-108` pins today, and the inverted-window hazard PB-D19 (v) guards, both become structurally impossible rather than guarded. EDIT TWO, the branch and the bound: the `totalScore == 0` branch stops writing `lastAccrualBlock` — it returns with the cursor where it was, so a window in which every score self-cleared stays PENDING instead of forfeited — and the integrate path clamps its upper end to `to = min(block.number, last + MAX_ACCRUAL_SPAN_BLOCKS)` with `MAX_ACCRUAL_SPAN_BLOCKS = AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_ERA`, writing the cursor to that clamped `to`. One call's work is thereby bounded by one era — at most two iterations of `_lpTrancheIntegral`'s era loop and roughly 104 epoch buckets in the continuous-phase `integratedSkim` walk — regardless of how long the gap grew, and a backlog drains across successive touches, same-block touches included, because the tightened guard compares against the ADVANCED cursor. When no gap exists the clamp never binds and the function is behaviour-identical to today. **Canon per PP-D43:** `11_formulas.md` F-6, Effect — "The entire post-Incendiary emission is distributed in proportion to scores. No emissions left unallocated." The live branch leaves a whole window unallocated; the fix defers the window to the next accrual with live scores, which is the only F-6-conformant disposition, since shares are undefined while the score sum is zero.

**What changes semantically, stated so the NatSpec amendment is a record rather than a surprise.** H-D26's "fresh against `block.number`" weakens to "fresh against the cursor, which equals `block.number` except mid-drain": during a drain, `_settlePool` and `claim` read a monotone, converging accumulator — value is DEFERRED, never lost — and the drain needs no new entry point, every existing mutating entry advancing one era per call. The crediting rule is explicit: a pending window is credited pro-rata to the scores live at drain time. And the fix intent's two-sided conservation invariant pins the whole thing as an IDENTITY, not an inequality: over any zero-score gap plus its drain, the accumulator's growth times the score equals `_lpTrancheIntegral` over the drained range to the wei — forfeiture would fail it low, double-counting would fail it high.

**Why (b) and not (a), recorded so the Fenwick's absence reads as declined rather than missed.** A plain write-time prefix sum is UNCONSTRUCTIBLE against `_placeBoost:483-495`, which writes only FUTURE epochs and tops the same epoch up across purchases until it begins; an honest (a) is therefore a Fenwick tree over per-block epoch rates — O(log) both sides, about forty lines of new data structure on the emission surface across two contracts — or a lazy freeze-and-roll whose unfrozen tail goes unbounded exactly in the no-activity case E.6 is about. (b) is one guard and one clamp in one contract; it preserves L-D23's divide-first exact-tiling arithmetic byte-identically — the surface that already ONCE chose the direct walk over a cache, when L-D23 superseded L-D9 — and it closes the tail (a) also closes: a decades-long single-call catch-up whose walk exceeds the block gas limit would resurrect the forfeiture as a gas-brick, and under the era bound no such call exists. What (a) would have bought and (b) declines: bounding every OTHER `integratedSkim` consumer at the source, in particular the F.1 clamp's one-time cold-cursor `_lpTrancheIntegral(GENESIS_BLOCK + 1, block.number)` walk on a pool's first boost delivery — pre-existing, one-time per pool, roughly 26 epochs per year of protocol age, and not this rung's defect. (c), the `try/catch`, is declined on H-D29's own recorded ground: silent skim-suppression past the 21M cap is worse than a loud revert.

**One stale document is corrected in passing, because it names a mechanism that does not exist.** `IIncendiaryRegistry.sol:12` still promises "bucket-cached cumulative sums internally so `integratedSkim` itself stays O(1)" — an L-D9 survivor that L-D23's rewrite orphaned, RB-029's comment-names-a-different-mechanism class verbatim. It is rewritten to state the direct epoch walk and the distributor-side era bound that now contains it. Doc-only; the registry's runtime bytecode does not move.

**Blast radius, measured at HEAD rather than predicted.** `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol:1138` `test_Accrual_EmptyTotalScoreGuardAdvancesBlockOnly` pins EXACTLY the removed write and inverts to the pending semantics; the two `PreGenesisAccrual` snap pins at `:99-108` invert to anchor-holds-at-genesis while the `:89-94` premise pin stands; `test/whitehat/P1_E6.t.sol`'s burned case inverts to the done-criteria name `test_zeroTotalScoreWindowDoesNotForfeit` and gains the era-drain case and the two-sided conservation case; the `integratedSkim` mockCall expectations at `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol:1499-1608` are untouched so long as no test's accrual span crosses one era, which is asserted at drafting, not assumed. The formal sweep covers `test/formal/` and `formal/act/` for any distributor-accrual property the cursor semantics falsify — the `lastAccrualBlock` hits at `EmissionProofs.sol:556-582` are the CHANNEL's cursor, a different contract, but the sweep runs regardless, and any rewrite lands as PENDING-REATTESTATION per PP-D37. The fork gate per PP-D46 covers every fixture exercising `EmissionDistributor`: the symbol grep's thirteen — DeployStageH, DeployStageI, DeployStageL, StageHIntegration, StageGIntegration, StageIIntegration, StageKIntegration, StageLIntegration, StageMIntegration, StageNIntegration, StagePIntegration, RouterIntegration, DissolutionGovernanceHandoff — plus `StagePRunRehearsal`, which exercises the deploy spine that constructs it; fourteen file-scoped runs behind the RB-023 sentinel.

**What this rung DECLINES, beyond the Fenwick.** Any registry edit and any new external entry — no permissionless drain function, because every existing mutating entry already drains a chunk and a keeper wanting speed can batch `recordScore` calls; any change to `poolBoostCursor` or the boost-delivery leg, whose defects are E.8's and live in their own rows; and any touch on `BodenseeBootstrapChannel.lastAccrualBlock`, a same-named cursor with its own semantics in a contract this rung does not open.

**FIRST AMENDMENT at PP4.5c, 2026-08-27, before any code landed — the pending window as locked is credited to ONE pool, structurally rather than as a race, and the launch case makes it certain.** The lock says a pending window is credited pro-rata to the scores live at drain time and treats that as self-evidently fair. It is not, and the reason is call ORDER, verified at HEAD rather than assumed: `_accrueGlobal()` runs at step 2 of `recordScore` (`:532`) while the score write lands at step 10 (`:558`), so the first `recordScore` after a zero-score gap still observes `totalScore == 0`, returns, and writes pool A's score; the SECOND call then integrates the entire backlog against a denominator consisting of pool A alone. An honest keeper batching all 26 calls produces exactly that outcome — this is the default path, not an attack, and no ordering of honest calls avoids it. **Against F-1 it is canon-hostile.** During bootstrap `_alphaF3` is 0, so the H-D33 Miliarium reshape gives every scored pool `f5Total / 28`, the canonical equal split of `11_formulas.md` F-1; crediting one pool 100% of a window the spec divides 28 ways is precisely the divergence PP-D43 makes binding. **And the launch window is guaranteed, not hypothetical:** `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS` is 432,000 and `_gatedTvlEMA:515-521` returns 0 until it elapses, so `totalScore == 0` holds for the whole post-genesis maturity window — on generation-3 numbers, seeding at 11405532 against genesis 11597476 leaves about 240,000 blocks, some 33 days of bootstrap-rate tranche, landing entirely on whichever pool is scored first. The locked shape therefore replaces a forfeiture every LP shares with a windfall one pool takes, which is E.6's own second face — a caller booking the outcome at a moment of their choosing — reappearing with the sign flipped.

**RESOLVED (user-adjudicated): cold-start FORFEITS and lapses are HELD, and the span bound drops from one era to one epoch.** The zero-score branch advances `lastAccrualBlock = block.number` while `accRewardPerScoreUnit == 0` and returns WITHOUT advancing it otherwise — two lines, no new state. The sentinel is exact for its purpose: the accumulator is zero if and only if no accrual has ever completed against live scores, which is the launch window and nothing else, and it is monotone thereafter. A window in which no pool was ever eligible is forfeited because no pool earned it — the 60-day maturity gate is F-04's own anti-spot-pump FIX, so the emission it withholds is unearned by construction rather than lost — while a genuine lapse, where scores WERE live and staled, stays pending exactly as E.6 requires. `MAX_ACCRUAL_SPAN_BLOCKS` becomes `AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH`, 100,800, rather than `BLOCKS_PER_ERA`: the bound now serves two purposes rather than one, capping both the per-call gas at roughly two `integratedSkim` buckets and the per-call MISALLOCATION at fourteen days, so E.6's own reported scenario — one 14-day keeper lapse staling all 26 at once — drains in exactly one call while any longer lapse spreads across successive calls as the roster refills.

**One caveat recorded rather than hidden, and one alternative declined.** The `accRewardPerScoreUnit == 0` sentinel also reads zero in the pathological case where every accrual so far rounded to zero under `divDown` — a contribution smaller than the score sum — after which a subsequent lapse would be treated as cold-start and forfeited. That edge fails in the CONSERVATIVE direction, matching today's shipped behaviour rather than over-crediting, and an explicit `bool hasAccrued` is the exact alternative, declined because a storage slot for a case that requires the accumulator to have never moved buys precision no reachable state needs. DECLINED separately: preserving the launch window and adding a mechanism to spread its credit across the roster, which is the most canon-faithful answer and the one this rung cannot afford — it needs either new per-pool state or a deferred-release design, and PP-D47 is scoped to two edits in one function. That option is not refuted, only deferred; if a later reader wants the launch tranche distributed rather than withheld, it is a design rung of its own and not an amendment to this one.

**What this changes in the blast radius stated above.** The `PreGenesisAccrual` inversion is UNAFFECTED — the tightened `<=` guard still makes the below-genesis snap impossible, and the cold-start branch cannot fire before genesis because that guard returns first. `test_Accrual_EmptyTotalScoreGuardAdvancesBlockOnly` at `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol:1138` now inverts to TWO cases rather than one, since the branch it pins has two arms: cold-start still advancing the cursor, and a post-accrual lapse holding it. The done-criteria case `test_zeroTotalScoreWindowDoesNotForfeit` must therefore establish a completed accrual BEFORE the gap it measures, which `test/whitehat/P1_E6.t.sol`'s reproduction already does at its control assertion — the existing PoC drives a healthy accrual first and asserts `accAfterHealthyAccrual > 0`, so the fixture needs no new setup to reach the held-pending arm.

**SECOND AMENDMENT at PP4.5l, 2026-08-28, found by the Part-A gate rather than by any sweep — the epoch clamp splits the protocol's most common window by exactly one block, and the first amendment's "drains in exactly one call" is CORRECTED to one or two.** `MAX_ACCRUAL_SPAN_BLOCKS` is `AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH`, and so is `EMA_STALENESS_BLOCKS` at `EmissionDistributor.sol:60`; the staleness gate is a STRICT `>`, so a window built to just exceed one staleness period measures `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH + 1` blocks, one more than the clamp releases per call. `test/unit/ZeroTvlDisposition.t.sol::test_ZeroTvl_windowC_dustCapturesUntilSelfClear` is exactly that shape and failed by `1e18`, one block at unit rate, against a 100,801-block leg. The collision is structural rather than a coincidence of that fixture: every window in this protocol expressed as one staleness period plus the strictness offset now needs a second call.

**The constant is KEPT at one epoch and the TEST is corrected, which is the opposite of the reflex.** Three alternatives were weighed and rejected. Two epochs would drain every such window in one call but DOUBLES the adjudicated misallocation bound from fourteen days to twenty-eight, undoing the first amendment's whole rationale for the figure. `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH + BLOCKS_PER_DAY` would clear the boundary at fifteen days but is an unprincipled constant chosen to dodge one test. And leaving the fixture red was never an option, since it is wei-exact EVIDENCE for the closed PB-D18 (vi) disposition rather than an ordinary regression test. What the fixture asserts is an economic magnitude — one wei of dust captures the FULL tranche while a stale score lives — and that claim is untouched by how many calls deliver it, so the correction is a second `claim` in leg 1 and nothing else.

**Legs 2 and 3 need NO change, and leg 3 passes BECAUSE of the fix rather than despite it.** Traced against the real constants before any edit: with leg 1 fully drained the cursor sits at `t1`, so leg 2's span is exactly `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH`, which is NOT greater than the clamp and therefore integrates whole in one call; and leg 3, which asserts that the epoch after the self-clear pays zero, now holds because `totalScore == 0` with a non-zero accumulator HOLDS the cursor, so the cumulative capture stays pinned at the leg-2 figure the assertion already names. A fixture written to pin the old forfeiture semantics would have gone red at leg 3; that it goes green is a positive control on the lapse arm that no test in `P1_E6.t.sol` supplies.

**The sweep axis this exposes is new and belongs in the register, not just in this rung.** PP4.2's recorded lesson is that a sweep must cover every DIRECTORY referencing a changed symbol, and that lesson was followed here — `test/`, `src/`, `script/`, `formal/` were all swept for `lastAccrualBlock`, `_accrueGlobal` and the changed comparison. `ZeroTvlDisposition.t.sol` names NONE of them. It collided with the fix because of a QUANTITY, the number of blocks it spans in a single accrual, and no grep over identifiers can find that. A clamp introduces a class of blast radius that symbol sweeps are structurally blind to: every existing caller whose span exceeds the new bound. The instrument that found it was the full Part-A gate, which is the argument for running the whole suite before declaring a clamp landed rather than only the files the rung touched.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.5q, 2026-08-28 — PP4.5 is BUILT and F-61 reads `Fixed at PP4.5`.** Commits: the lock and its plan row at `893844f` and `09459ef`, the first amendment and its row correction at `3a25736` and `b681856`; the code at `8b31a7f` (the `MAX_ACCRUAL_SPAN_BLOCKS` bound and the cursor NatSpec) and `332786b` (the non-strict guard, the cold-start fork and the clamp); the tests at `28ec156` (the PB-D19 (v) pin inverted to hold-at-genesis), `0c77363` (the done-criteria case), `a386c78` (the harness swap), `ee3ff63` (cold-start forfeiture and one-epoch drain) and `2bda4ce` (the two-sided conservation identity); the second amendment and its fallout at `9d223d0` and `30b2c4c`; the superseded prose at `a5fd340`; RB-030 at `4d2bb38`; the ledger at `fefd47b`. **Canon per PP-D43 is TWO clauses, not one, and the second is why this rung has a fork at all:** `11_formulas.md` F-6, "No emissions left unallocated", which the live branch violated by forfeiting a whole window; and F-1's equal 1/28 bootstrap split, which the NAIVE fix would have violated by handing that window to one pool. Gates: Part-A **1067 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped of 1068** across 90 suites, the skip being RB-024's deferral and the total being 1068 rather than 1065 because `P1_E6.t.sol` went from one case to four; and **13 of 13 fork files green**, file-scoped with the RB-023 sentinel reading 0 in the same shell — the roster being every fixture that EXERCISES `EmissionDistributor` per PP-D46 rather than the two this rung edited, which is `DeployStageH` 1/1, `DeployStageI` 1/1, `DissolutionGovernanceHandoff` 9/9, `RouterIntegration` 7/7, `StageGIntegration` 40/40, `StageHIntegration` 13/13, `StageIIntegration` 4/4, `StageKIntegration` 9/9, `StageLIntegration` 2/2, `StageMIntegration` 7/7, `StageNIntegration` 9/9, `StagePIntegration` 23/23 and `StagePRunRehearsal` 10/10. Six of those thirteen had no recorded figure anywhere before this rung and are now pinned.

**FIVE results not recoverable from the diffs.** FIRST, the cold-start fork is NOT defensive coding: without it the entire pending backlog is credited to ONE pool, structurally rather than as a race, because `_accrueGlobal` runs at step 2 of every mutating entry while the score write lands at step 10 — so the call that restores a score still observes `totalScore == 0` and holds, and the drain lands on the call after it, against a denominator of exactly one pool. An honest keeper batching all 26 `recordScore` calls produces that outcome; no ordering of honest calls avoids it. SECOND, the clamp collided with a canonical constant rather than landing in open space, and the full Part-A gate found it where no sweep could — filed as RB-030. THIRD, NO machine-checked formal property is falsified, and that was VERIFIED rather than assumed: `EmissionProofs.sol`'s four `lastAccrualBlock` assertions are all `channel.lastAccrualBlock()`, P-BC3's "accrue structural guards" is the CHANNEL's `accrue()` per `formal/RESULTS.md:203`, and P-ED1 through P-ED4 are distributor GOVERNANCE locks; the distributor's `_accrueGlobal` has no proof asserting it, so PP-D37's bar on re-running the bundle was never engaged. FOURTH, `ZeroTvlDisposition` leg 3 passes BECAUSE of the fix rather than despite it — it asserts the epoch after a self-clear pays zero, which now holds because the lapse arm HOLDS the cursor, making it a positive control on that arm which no case in `P1_E6.t.sol` supplies. FIFTH, the O(1) promise corrected at `IIncendiaryRegistry.sol:12` described a bucket-cached cumulative sum that NEVER SHIPPED: L-D23's direct epoch walk superseded the L-D9 crystallize-cache model, and the interface comment outlived the design it documented.

**What this rung DECLINED.** The Fenwick tree that an honest shape (a) requires, because `_placeBoost` writes only FUTURE epochs and tops the same epoch up until it begins, so a write-time prefix sum is unconstructible and the real cost is about forty lines of new data structure across two contracts; any registry edit at all; a permissionless drain entry, since every existing mutating entry already drains a chunk; raising the clamp to two epochs when `ZeroTvlDisposition` went red, which would have doubled the adjudicated misallocation bound to twenty-eight days to avoid editing one test; and preserving the launch window behind a mechanism that spreads its credit across the roster, which is the most canon-faithful answer available and is DEFERRED rather than refuted — it needs new per-pool state or a deferred-release design, and belongs to a design rung of its own.

**One process cost, and it is a single habit wearing four costumes.** Four expectations in this rung were wrong and the payloads were right every time: an em-dash tally that added a payload OCCURRENCE count to a file LINE count; a zero-hit sentinel whose pattern was never measured for REACH, so a legitimate second hit read as a failure; a `grep -B1` where solc prints the file:line AFTER the warning, which filtered out the very thing it was written to find; and a sentinel whose baseline was measured while its payload contribution was assumed. Every one is the same rule failing — a number asserted about a file without running the IDENTICAL command against both the payload and the baseline — and PP13 already names half of it. The cure applied from PP4.5m onward was to print BASE, OUT and IN for every sentinel in one table before drafting the verify block, after which no expectation missed. The compounding cost was worse than the errors: two full `forge build --force` rebuilds were spent chasing a warning that belonged to `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol:420` and `:431` all along, and the evidence identifying it was already in the session's own earlier census. Read the transcript before spending a rebuild to re-derive what it already holds.

## PP-D48 — E.4, E.2 and E.3 (PP4.6, rung 6): the ledger debit, the two drains, the yield pin, and the gate that stays

**PP-D48 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-28): rung 6 is PP-D13's chain E.4 then E.2 then E.3 — A.3's precondition was discharged at PP4.2 and is not a sub-step here — and it lands as FOUR settled changes plus ONE clause left deliberately OPEN.** (i) **E.4:** `routeYieldFeeToHook` DROPS its `amount` parameter, reads `_protocolFeeAmounts[pool][token]`, zeroes it, and approves exactly that, byte-mirroring `_withdrawProtocolFees:791-799` eleven lines below it; `minBptAmountOut` is passed as 0 because PB-D68 (xiv) already made it dead as a bound, a donation reverting on any non-zero BPT. Ripple MEASURED: 21 call sites in `test/unit/AureumProtocolFeeController.t.sol`, 6 in `test/fork/AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol`, and ZERO in `src/` or `script/` — the absence of production callers being E.2's own point. (ii) **E.2's drains:** `withdrawProtocolFees` and `withdrawProtocolFeesForToken` drop `authenticate` while KEEPING the recipient pin to `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK`; both are a pure `safeTransfer` to a hard-pinned address with no swap and no slippage, so permissionless costs nothing and self-heals. (iii) **E.2's ledger clause, which is load-bearing rather than tidy-up:** `collectAggregateFees` must forward the swap leg the way the hook path already does. The asymmetry is verified at HEAD — the keeper path `_receiveAggregateFees:315-316` credits BOTH `ProtocolFeeType.SWAP` and `ProtocolFeeType.YIELD` into `_protocolFeeAmounts`, while the hook path at `:423-425` credits YIELD only and forwards swap through `_receiveAggregateFeesSwapForward`. Without (iii), E.4's route-the-full-credit mixes STANDARD swap dust into a function whose name and whose canon clause are both about yield. (iv) **E.3:** the constructor pins `_globalProtocolYieldFeePercentage` to 1e17; `registerPool` forces `aggregateYieldFeePercentage = 0` when `pool == DER_BODENSEE_POOL`, mirroring D-D15's swap-side ignore-the-exempt-flag pin; and BOTH yield setters hard-revert exactly as the swap pair does at `:641` and `:661`. **Canon per PP-D43:** `10_constitution.md` §xxix L150—151, whose three obligations are the 10% rate, 100% of the skim to der Bodensee, and der Bodensee excluded from collection.

**Why the hard-revert is REQUIRED rather than an optional extra, and why the Bodensee gap is registration rather than collection.** §xxix declares its parameters immutable from block 0, so a pinned 10% with live setters is not the canonical shape — it preserves exactly the authorizer-change residual E.3's own row already corrected when it withdrew "can never be set" as one word too strong. The struck option was never the hard-revert standing alone; it was the queue's PAIRED phrase, hard-revert **and amend the constitution**, whose second half PP-D43 forbids. On the exclusion: D-D9 ALREADY blocks collection at `collectAggregateFees:271-276` with `BodenseeYieldCollectionDisabled`, so the surviving gap is the REGISTRATION STAMP at `:616`, `protocolFeeExempt ? 0 : _globalProtocolYieldFeePercentage`. That flag is a Vault-side registration parameter with NO setter anywhere in `src/` or `script/`, so once the global is non-zero der Bodensee inherits a live yield stamp unless the controller pins it by address. Trusting a factory to pass the flag is not a code guarantee.

**THE GATE STAYS, and this reverses the drafting recommendation that preceded this lock.** `04_tokenomics.md` L155 states it in terms: yield fees "are collected permissionlessly; routing into der Bodensee is governance-gated and happens at most once per epoch for any given pool" — which is OQ-20 and OQ-21 exactly, and FINDINGS records that making routing permissionless after the cooldown would be a NEW open question and a break from OQ-20. Dropping `authenticate` from `routeYieldFeeToHook` therefore does not fix E.2; it deletes the mechanism the specification names, and it would make the finding disappear by weakening the spec, which is the anti-shape PP-D43 exists to stop. The canon protects the fee DESTINATION as one of its five extras, and the destination is unchanged either way — what L155 additionally names is the GATE, and that is a `04_tokenomics` clause, so changing it is PP5.4 / PP-D23 territory rather than a silent third bullet inside E.2. **The post-K defect is a REACHABILITY failure, not a mandate:** `authenticate` resolves to `AureumGovernance`, which cannot EXPRESS the call under fixed dispatch, which is the C.2 class. The remedy retargets the CALLER and keeps the gate.

**CLAUSE (v) IS OPEN and no code in this rung may presuppose its answer.** Who becomes `routeYieldFeeToHook`'s reachable governor: the hook's `governanceModule` Safe, which is live today; a typed `YieldFeeRoute` proposal on `AureumGovernance`, which restores expressibility at the cost of a seventh proposal type on a surface F-22 already widened; or leaving the entry gated and completing the skim through `recoverStrandedFees`, which is destination-correct and reachable but is the PB3.10 exception rail rather than the yield route L155 names. RECORDED PREFERENCE, not a lock: keep `routeYieldFeeToHook` as the yield route and make its own caller reachable, leaving recovery as the exception. Until (v) closes, E.3 pins a live 10% skim whose routing leg is still gated to an unreachable principal, so the rung MUST NOT be read as completing the L150 path end to end — collection and the ledger drain become reachable, the route does not.

**What this rung DECLINES, and the first two are corrections to proposals made during its own pre-flight.** A rate-derived floor computed from `IVault.getPoolTokenRates` is REJECTED because it is not a price: `PoolDataLib.getTokenRate:152-162` returns `rateProvider.getRate()` for `WITH_RATE` and `FixedPoint.ONE` for `STANDARD`, and an ERC-4626 `getRate()` is shares-to-underlying, so the ratio `rate(feeToken) / rate(depositToken)` is dimensionally a price ONLY when both tokens share an underlying — which the yield route never does, since it moves LST or ETH or USDS shares through the originating pool onto a CHF rail. Such a floor sits either far below spot, never binding and leaving F-13 open, or far above it, reverting every honest route and stranding the skim again; and because `STANDARD` returns `FixedPoint.ONE` rather than a sentinel, the rate array cannot even detect a missing provider, which needs `TokenInfo.tokenType` from `getPoolTokenInfo`. `MAX_ROUTE_SLIPPAGE_BPS` is REJECTED under PP-D43's fifth obligation: no §xxix clause names it, and a rung that cannot cite canon for a constant stops rather than adjudicating a number. A depth-fraction cap on the input is REJECTED as the wrong quantity — it bounds the route's OWN price impact while a sandwich works by the attacker moving the price first. And inventing a lagged reference is REJECTED as already-settled ground: PB-D9 (ii)(a) records that none exists for arbitrary fee tokens on arbitrary pools, and that `quoteSvZCHF` is constellation-spot averaging that goes circular when the originating pool is the token's only venue.

**CLAUSE (v) RESOLVED at PP4.6j, 2026-08-28 (user-adjudicated): the gate STAYS and is RETARGETED to a scheduled keeper, with the Safe as a standing fallback.** Permissionless is REJECTED for the reason the clause was opened: `04_tokenomics.md` L155 gates routing into der Bodensee, so removing `authenticate` amends the spec rather than fixing E.2. A keeper is the shape that satisfies every constraint at once. It PRESERVES PB-D9 (iii) rather than falsifying it — that disposition is "caller-supplied bounds, governance computes them off-chain, Vault enforces", and a keeper computing `minDepositTokenOut` from a real price source IS that design with a scheduled principal in place of a human one. It satisfies L155 literally, since a governance-seated keeper is a gate and the `_lastRouteBlock` throttle already caps the cadence at one route per pool per epoch (OQ-21). And it is PRECEDENTED rather than novel: PP-D31 already adjudicated "a funded keeper named as a trust assumption" for `advanceTournament` and `updateMultiplier`, and this is the same category with the same disclosure.

**Four typed-domain decisions, settled here because §12 bars materialising them in code first.** (a) `yieldRouteKeeper` is STORAGE, not an immutable — keepers are rotated, compromised and replaced, and an immutable would force a controller redeploy to change one operational address. (b) It is ROTATABLE, never one-shot: a burned keeper slot is a permanently dead route, which is the trap PP3.12 avoided when `admissionAuthority` took the `GaugeRegistry.governance` shape instead of `setGaugeRegistry`'s burn. (c) The rotation authority is the hook's `governanceModule` Safe, NOT `authenticate` — an `authenticate`-gated keeper setter would be circular, since `authenticate` resolving to an inexpressible `AureumGovernance` is the whole defect. The controller reads it through `IAureumFeeRoutingHook`, which gains a `governanceModule()` declaration on the PB-D69 (xiii) precedent, the hook already holding it as a `public` slot at `:111`. (d) The gate admits EITHER the keeper OR that Safe, so a stalled keeper is an inconvenience rather than a stranded skim.

**One scheduling finding that cuts against the obvious design and belongs in the runbook, not just here.** Firing at the END of each epoch is the intuitive schedule and the worst one: a deterministic trigger block telegraphs a large, price-moving swap on a known pool for a known token to every searcher, which is precisely the trade `minDepositTokenOut` is the only defence against. Nothing decays at the epoch edge — once the throttle clears, the route stays available indefinitely until taken — so the keeper SHOULD fire opportunistically inside the eligible window rather than at its boundary, and the jitter costs nothing. The keeper reads `getProtocolFeeAmounts(pool)` and skips pools with no credit, which post-clause-(iii) is a yield-only figure.

**The residual is RELOCATED, not absent, and is recorded as such.** A hostile or compromised keeper can pass a slack bound and let a searcher take the difference; it CANNOT steal, because the recipient is pinned to `FEE_ROUTING_HOOK` and the hook donates into der Bodensee. That fails PP-D41's bar of "capability absent or bounded by code that holds against a hostile holder" — and PP-D41 is NOT the governing test here, because it constrains rows exiting Accepted-risk and E.2 exits **Fix**. The keeper is a named trust assumption in PP-D31's sense, and §xxix's no-treasury clause means it must be funded externally rather than from protocol revenue. **DEFERRED, and this corrects the drafting conversation that preceded this lock:** the carve-out making the non-swap branches permissionless — the same-token donation and the ZCHF-to-svZCHF ERC-4626 deposit, neither of which any swap can sandwich — CANNOT land in PP4.6. L155 gates routing with no branch distinction, so exempting a branch is a spec change belonging to PP5.4 / PP-D23, not a fix rung. It is recorded as the first thing to revisit there.

**E.3's TWO TYPED-DOMAIN AMENDMENTS, user-adjudicated 2026-08-28 at the PP4.6s pre-flight and both written before the code they govern.** (vi) **The hard-reverting yield setters revert a NEW `YieldSkimIsImmutable()`, not `SplitIsImmutable`.** Clause (iv) said "exactly as the swap pair does" and named no error, and reuse is barred by that error's own NatSpec at `:178-181`, which reads "both the global setter and the per-pool override revert with this error" and cites D-D15, OQ-1 and OQ-1a — swap-split provenance throughout. Reusing it would make that sentence false, four setters standing where it claims two, and would assert a 50/50 split for a rate that is a skim rather than a split. The new error cites `10_constitution.md` §xxix L150—151 as its own provenance. (vii) **`withValidYieldFee` at `:144` is DELETED rather than left declared**, its only two call sites being the setters clause (iv) converts, and the swap side is the precedent in both halves: `setGlobalProtocolSwapFeePercentage` is a bare `external pure` revert carrying no modifier, and its pinned value is covered by `test_constructor_pinsGlobalSwapFeePercentageAtCap`, whose sibling E.3 gains. **The ripple was MEASURED at `eccb089` and is far smaller than PP-D44's:** zero production callers of either yield setter in `src/` or `script/`, which is E.3's own premise confirmed rather than assumed; one consumer of the global, `registerPool:623`; `aggregateYieldFeePercentage` and `GlobalProtocolYieldFee` in ONE fork file, `StagePIntegration.t.sol`; and ZERO yield-fee proofs anywhere in `test/formal/` or `formal/`, so unlike PP4.1 nothing there goes semantically false.

**TWO INCIDENTAL-COUPLING FINDINGS the same pre-flight surfaced, both RB-029's class, both reserved for this rung's register step so neither is lost.** FIRST, the Bodensee registration pin clause (iv) adds is protected only by a guard on a DIFFERENT function: `updateProtocolYieldFeePercentage` is permissionless and resyncs any non-override pool to the global, so it would write the skim straight back over the zero, and the only reason it cannot is that `withLatestFees:160` calls `collectAggregateFees`, which D-D9 reverts for `DER_BODENSEE_POOL`. Nothing states that dependency, so E.3 carries a regression pin asserting `updateProtocolYieldFeePercentage(DER_BODENSEE_POOL)` reverts `BodenseeYieldCollectionDisabled`. SECOND, `invariant_allWithdrawalsTargetFeeRoutingHook` was VACUOUS before PP4.6g: its handler calls `withdrawProtocolFees` as itself, one fixed contract address that the authorizer never granted, so every inner call reverted into the `try/catch` and `successfulWithdrawRecipients` stayed empty. Removing the gate is what made that invariant load-bearing, and no test recorded the transition.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.6z1, 2026-08-29 — PP4.6 is BUILT and F-57, F-58 and F-59 read `Fixed at PP4.6`.** Commits: the lock and its plan row (`e8a77e1`, `64aa366`); E.4's ledger debit and its ripple (`382b812`, `8f6fad2`, `0cbbe91`, `77672c6`); E.2's two ungated drains, the swap-leg forward and the overload they orphaned (`d1b0d29`, `e885173`, `24c81bf`); clause (v) end to end, the interface getter, the slot, the Safe-gated setter, the keeper-or-Safe gate and its unit coverage (`7c539af`, `145f89f`, `1f9e02e`, `a094831`, `b50b7cf`, `ea7cfed`, `125db39`); E.2's done-criteria in both prongs (`c557397`, `4f51a01`) and the unit repair behind it (`c1c7168`, `eccb089`); the two amendments (`d409be5`); E.3's source in five steps (`92743ee`, `6a7f5ce`, `ab96c91`, `7be6dd8`, `25e9888`); the unit ladder that followed it (`92d6745`, `e52a6f4`, `587c8e3`, `861a03e`, `e37a75a`, `503eeca`); E.3's done-criteria (`12983e5`); the fork route repaired for the new gate (`6e0d6f7`); the ledger (`de68176`, `56afe09`, `6118408`); and the plan (`1959128`, `19d582c`). ONE SEQUENCING DEVIATION, recorded rather than hidden: PP4.5 ran closing-record then Completion Log then §11, and this rung landed the log row first at `PP4.6z`, so the remaining records take `z1` and `z2`. The row's `PP4.6a—PP4.6z` range covers them as it covers `q1` and `t2a`.

**FIVE results not recoverable from the diffs.** FIRST, the Vault's 24-bit aggregate-fee store round-trips `10e16` EXACTLY, because `10e16 / FEE_SCALING_FACTOR` is `1e6` and fits, so pilot pools read the skim to the wei and the fork case asserts equality rather than a bound. SECOND, der Bodensee's zero comes from the ADDRESS pin and nothing else: it registers through `wpf.create` with `protocolFeeExempt` false, so `isOverride` is false too, and an unprotected zero is exactly what `updateProtocolYieldFeePercentage` would resync away — the only thing stopping it is D-D9 guarding `collectAggregateFees` beneath `withLatestFees`, a guard on a DIFFERENT function that nothing states, which is why the RB-029 regression pin exists. THIRD, `test_swapLegFeesReachTheHook`'s all-zero ledger assertion survived the skim going live, because no rate moves inside that test's own block; that was a flagged risk, not a certainty. FOURTH, the controller ends with ZERO `authenticate`-gated functions, verified by a modifier-position grep rather than a keyword count — all six surviving mentions are NatSpec explaining the absence, which is why the `IAuthentication` import fell out of BOTH test files. FIFTH, the fork roster is 27 of 33 files, and the six excluded are excluded for two distinct reasons: three stand up no Vault at all, two bind the live Balancer mainnet Vault whose fee controller is Balancer's rather than Aureum's, and one is Sepolia-scoped per PP-D59.

**What this rung DECLINED, and the first is a standing carry.** Three dead items stay: `withValidSwapFee` and `withValidPoolCreatorFee` in the contract, orphaned by D-D15 and by Pass 4 respectively, and `_mockUpdateAggregateSwapFeePercentage` in the unit fixture, orphaned by the same D-D15 conversion. All three are PRE-AUDIT dead code unrelated to any finding, and PP-D2 requires this branch's diff from `9ec513d` to be exactly the remediation, so sweeping them would put non-remediation noise into the tree the seam-1 re-run consumes. They are a post-gate hygiene item. The queue was likewise NOT edited, and that is a finding rather than an omission: its reproduction column is a deliberate historical record, F.1's and E.6's cells still naming their pre-inversion cases, and all three done-criteria cells already named the cases this rung built and turned green.

**One process cost, and its two halves share a shape: compute the set, never check the suspects.** The fork roster is the loud half. PP4.5's 13-file gate was built from `EmissionDistributor` references; deriving PP4.6's fresh from `AureumProtocolFeeController` gave 27, and `test/fork/AureumFeeRoutingHook.t.sol` — absent from PP4.5's list — came back 13 passed and 2 failed. Inheriting would have shipped it. The quiet half is the same error caught by the same cure: PP4.6u1 deleted a test and orphaned three fixture helpers, which went unnoticed because the rung checked the helpers it suspected; a later pre-flight enumerated EVERY `function _*` in the file and its use count in one pass, which found all three and a fourth that was pre-audit. Both halves also explain the rung's wrong predictions, which were all derivations standing in for measurements: a `git diff --stat` predicted by adding payload sizes, when git reports a MINIMAL EDIT SCRIPT and elides lines common to both sides, wrong by 6 at PP4.6t2a and by 17 at PP4.6v; a `GOVERNANCE_MULTISIG` count reasoned from the code lines of a payload whose COMMENT also named the constant; and a `^## ` baseline asserted as unchanged without ever having been measured. PP13 names this and PP-D47's BASE/OUT/IN table is the cure; it worked wherever it was applied, and every miss came from a token the table was not built for.

## PP-D49 — G.5a and G.5b (PP4.7, rung 7): the phantom proposal, the stale readmission ticket, and a deadline that adjudicates no new number

**PP-D49 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-29): rung 7 is G.5a and G.5b, both in `src/gauge/VaultClassRegistry.sol`, both `governance` unit, and it lands as FIVE changes.** (i) **G.5b:** `vetoProposal` and `finalizeProposal` each gain `if (proposalId >= nextProposalId) revert UnknownProposal(proposalId)` as their FIRST statement. The defect is not merely a missing guard: `AdmissionType.ImplementationAddress` is enum value ZERO, so the zero struct a phantom id reads is a VALID admission type, and `createdBlock` zero makes `block.number <= 0 + VETO_WINDOW_BLOCKS` false for every block past 201,600 — so the window check waves it through and `address(0)` is admitted as a real implementation class. `vetoProposal` has the identical hole and reverts today only incidentally, on `VetoWindowExpired`, which is why the guard goes on BOTH entries rather than the one the reproduction drives. (ii) **G.5a, the finalize re-check:** `finalizeProposal` reverts `ClassAlreadyAdmitted` when `admittedClasses[proposal.admissionValue]` is already true, closing the duplicate-admission case. (iii) **G.5a, the deadline:** `finalizeProposal` additionally requires `block.number <= proposal.createdBlock + 2 * VETO_WINDOW_BLOCKS`, one further window to finalize after the veto window closes. NO new constant is declared, which is the point: PP-D43's fifth obligation stops a rung that cannot cite canon for a number, and this cites G-D19's existing lock instead of adjudicating a fresh figure, exactly as PP-D48 declined `MAX_ROUTE_SLIPPAGE_BPS`. (iv) **G.5a, the revocation stamp:** `revokeVaultClass` records `lastRevokedBlock[admissionValue] = block.number`, and `finalizeProposal` reverts when `proposal.createdBlock <= lastRevokedBlock[proposal.admissionValue]`. (v) **G.5a, the stale type:** `revokeVaultClass` also clears `admissionTypes[admissionValue]`.

**CLAUSE (iv) SUPERSEDES THE QUEUE'S FIX INTENT, and the reason is a defect in that intent rather than a preference.** The row asks for "one live proposal per `admissionValue`", and neither that nor clause (ii)'s re-check closes the sequence the row is named for. Propose, then let the class be admitted, then let governance REVOKE it: at finalize `admittedClasses[value]` is FALSE, so a re-check passes and the stale proposal re-admits the class, bypassing the governance decision that removed it. A uniqueness map does not help either, because the surviving live proposal is precisely the ticket. Only something that invalidates proposals predating a revocation closes it, which is what the stamp does and why it is one mapping rather than proposal bookkeeping. It also leaves honest concurrent proposals legal, which the uniqueness shape would not.

**Three typed-domain settlements, and one existing attestation withdrawn.** The errors are `UnknownProposal(uint256 proposalId)` and `FinalizeDeadlineExpired(uint256 proposalId)`, both new and both carrying the id, matching the file's existing convention at `ProposalAlreadyFinalized` and `VetoWindowExpired`; clause (ii) reuses the declared `ClassAlreadyAdmitted(address)` and clause (iv) needs a third new error, `ProposalPredatesRevocation(uint256 proposalId)`, because reusing either window error would misreport the cause. `lastRevokedBlock` is a `mapping(address => uint256)` in STORAGE, appended after the existing mappings, and zero is its own sentinel: a value never revoked has `lastRevokedBlock` zero and `createdBlock` is always at least one, so the clause-(iv) comparison is false by construction rather than by a guard. **WITHDRAWN:** `revokeVaultClass`'s own NatSpec states that `admissionTypes` is "left stale until a later finalize" per G-D17, and clause (v) makes that false; the NatSpec is corrected in the same rung. Clearing is safe because `admissionType()` has ZERO production consumers — the sole reader of this registry anywhere in `src/` or `script/` is `GaugeEligibility.sol:417`, and it calls `isAdmittedClass`.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.7l, 2026-08-30 — PP4.7 is BUILT and F-46 and F-47 read `Fixed at PP4.7`.** Commits: the lock and its plan row (`1b721c5`, `6ebea64`); the declarations, three errors and the `lastRevokedBlock` mapping (`4a7afbd`); G.5b's guard on BOTH entries (`66fe378`); G.5a's finalize gates and revocation stamp (`837b275`); G.5a's type clearing (`74096cd`); the two PoCs inverted to regressions (`f44989b`, `1ee6dee`); the two ledger rows (`f7052c9`, `9023d07`). Gates: **Part-A 1082 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped of 1083 across 90 suites**, the +4 over PP4.6's 1078 being the two inverted PoCs and nothing else, the suite count unmoved at 90 and the skip still RB-024's `DeploymentArtifactAgreement`; and **25 of 25 fork files green**, the roster computed FRESH for `VaultClassRegistry` per PP-D46 rather than inherited from PP4.6's 27. That roster is four edges, not one: direct references, the transitive `GaugeEligibility.sol:417` call to `isAdmittedClass`, the construction site at `DeployStageG.s.sol:152`, and the full fixture-inheritance closure. Eight files are excluded on stated grounds and a later reader should not re-hunt them — `AureumFeeRoutingHook`, `BroadcastNestingProbe`, `DeployAureumVault`, `DeployStageJ`, `DerBodenseeScriptWitness` and `Sanity` have zero reach; `PB52SepoliaOracleEvidence` is Sepolia-fork and its sole hit is a NatSpec line; and `StageJIntegration` binds `MockGaugeRegistry` from `CCBMocks`, so it never reaches the real eligibility path.

**FIVE results not recoverable from the diffs.** FIRST, **clause (v) is UNOBSERVABLE unless the admitted type is enum one**: `delete` on an `AdmissionType` yields zero, which IS `ImplementationAddress`, so a case that admits `ImplementationAddress` and then revokes cannot distinguish a cleared slot from a stale one. The reproduction got that discriminator for free from the duplicate overwrite that clause (ii) now blocks; the regression had to make its finalized proposal a `FactoryAddress` one to recover it. SECOND, **the stamp comparison is `<=`, so a proposal created in the SAME block as the revocation is invalidated too** — correct rather than an off-by-one, since it closes a front-run inside the revocation block, and it is why the positive control rolls one block before opening its fresh proposal. THIRD, the deadline is STRICT, so exactly `createdBlock + 2 * VETO_WINDOW_BLOCKS` is the last legal block and both sides of that boundary are pinned. FOURTH, **no assertion anywhere in the tree claimed `admissionTypes` survives a revocation**, so clause (v)'s withdrawal of the G-D17 NatSpec inverted nothing; this was checked before the gate rather than assumed, and the three `G-D17` hits under `test/fork/` are about `revokeGauge` on `GaugeRegistry`, a different contract. FIFTH, **the queue needed no rung**: PP4.1's F.1 precedent leaves the `reproduction PoC` cell naming the pre-inversion case as the historical record of what PP3.2 built, and both G.5 done-criteria cells already named cases that now exist, so the supersession is recorded in the ledger rather than by rewriting the queue's fix-intent column.

**One process cost, and it destroyed its own evidence.** In the 25-run fork roster `WK18ThinVenuePumpSim` reported two failures at position 25; run alone immediately afterwards, and again at position 5 of 5 in a fresh shell, it is 6 of 6. **The cause is NOT established and none is asserted here per PB22** — the failure text was destroyed by the loop's own `tail -1`, because forge prints its `Tip: run forge test --rerun` line AFTER the summary, so the single captured line was the tip while the `[FAIL]` rows and the tally scrolled past unread. The generalisation is the register candidate: a compact-summary loop must select what it keeps, filtering on the `FAIL` and `Ran N test suite` line forms, because a fixed tail silently converts a visible failure into an unreadable one and a zero-failure run into an indistinguishable success.

## PP-D50 — C.6 and E.5 (PP4.8, rung 8): the antagonism split by leg, the gauge-status bridge PB-D69 (ix) left open, and a delay that adjudicates no new number

**PP-D50 (user-adjudicated 2026-08-30): rung 8 is C.6 AND E.5 together, and the absorption is forced rather than convenient.** C.6's emission leg and E.5's remedy are ONE mechanism seen from two sides: `recordScore` already opens `if (!_gaugeRegistry.isGaugeApproved(pool)) revert NotApproved(pool)`, and E.5 exists BECAUSE that revert means a revoked pool's score can never be removed, so it keeps drawing while survivors are diluted by a frozen denominator.
