The queue's C.6 fix intent, a standing re-check in `recordScore`, would therefore have reproduced E.5's defect for a second predicate — the pool stops being re-scored, its last score stays in `totalScore`, and face 1 gets WORSE. Both rows are `emission-stack`, so it is one redeploy either way; rung 13 drops its E.5 item and F-39 and F-60 both close at PP4.8. (ii) **The antagonism resolves BY LEG, not by choosing**: the composition gate snapshots the rail conjunct, the emission leg acts on live state, and neither read is deleted. (iii) **Composition leg** — `Proposal` gains a fifteenth member, `bool railAdmittedAtPropose`, following `newAuthorizer`'s type-scoped precedent, written at `_createProposal` for `CompositionChallenge` and false for every other type; at execute the conjunct is satisfied from the snapshot rather than re-read. ONLY the conjunct is snapshotted — the hook check, the forbidden-token check, factory provenance and the 52% numerator STAY live, because re-checking those is the gate's purpose, and a later reader must not simplify this into snapshotting the whole gate. (iv) That moves `meetsCompositionQualityGate`'s signature, which is G16 scope: the `IGaugeRegistry` inheritor roster goes on the table BEFORE the build, not during it. (v) **Emission leg** — a new PERMISSIONLESS `revokeGaugeIfIneligible(address pool)` on `GaugeRegistry`, since `revokeGauge` at `:178` is `onlyGovernance` and is not reused; E.5's `deregisterScore(pool)`, permissionless and gated `!isGaugeApproved`, then drives `f5Score` and `poolScore` to zero through `_applySignedDelta`, reduce-only, with pre-revocation pending still claimable. (vi) The re-check EXCLUDES the TVL floor outright — a persistence window was considered and REJECTED as re-introducing the grief surface it was meant to close, and canon handles TVL failure by epoch tournament rather than ad-hoc revocation. (vii) A `recoveryPathAdmitted` revocation takes effect only after one full proposal lifecycle plus one block, 223,201, and **NO new constant is adjudicated**: `VOTING_DELAY_BLOCKS`, `VOTING_PERIOD_BLOCKS`, `EXECUTION_TIMELOCK_BLOCKS` and `EXECUTION_GRACE_BLOCKS` are already declared at `AureumGovernance.sol:28-31`, and PP-D43's fifth obligation is met by citing them exactly as PP-D49 (iii) cited G-D19. Those four are PROMOTED from `internal` to `public` and read by `GaugeEligibility` rather than re-derived there, because a second 223,201 in a second contract is precisely RB-026's found-once-never-propagated class; governance is already in this rung's scope for clause (iii). (viii) **Two-step rotation** with the INCOMING authority accepting — a handshake, not PP4.1's arming pattern. PB19 applies and the difference is named: `proposeIncendiaryRegistry` arms a slot, whereas this must stay REPEATABLE per PB-D69 (viii), since a burned authority leaves a dead map no rail-less pool could ever clear, so there is no one-shot, no burn, and a pending proposal stays replaceable. (ix) Rung 8 spans THREE redeploy units — `gauge`, `emission-stack` and `governance` — against PP-D40's one-unit-per-row vocabulary; that is RECORDED rather than relabelled, the queue's `emission-stack` naming the entry contract, and PP4.0's closure prices are re-read before the build so the redeploy cost is known going in.

**FOUR canon citations, read in the clone rather than recalled, and two of them are sharper than the argument that invoked them.** `10_constitution.md:49` says a composition challenge "deprecates the old pool (gauge revoked, emissions cease)" and `:53` says "Deprecation = gauge revoked only", the pool losing "AuMM emissions, CCB multiplier, and Miliarium Registry slot status" — so clause (v) implements a sentence canon already states rather than inventing a policy, and E.5 is a divergence from canon and not merely from taste. `:55` is the specified-pool model: the proposal "must reference the address of an already-deployed candidate pool" and "the 2/3 supermajority vote is binary on that specific pool", which is the SHARPER form of clause (iii)'s justification — a post-vote flag flip does not merely inconvenience the mandate, it annuls a vote canon defines as binary on a pool that has not changed. `:66` locks the two-way enforcement, programmatic checks for what the contract can verify plus semantic review by governance, which is the split clause (iii) preserves by keeping the pool-intrinsic checks live at execute while snapshotting the operator attestation. And **PB-D69 (ix) ANTICIPATED face 1 in terms**: `isGaugeEligible[pool]` is written `true` in `evaluateEligibility` and cleared nowhere in `src/`, a one-way latch, so flipping `recoveryPathAdmitted` to false "fails only a LATER `evaluateEligibility` or `meetsCompositionQualityGate`; an already-Active gauge keeps running until `GaugeChallenge` or `revokeGauge` takes it down". PP-D50 supplies the missing caller. It is the BRIDGE PB-D69 left open, not a reversal of it, and that is now verified from the clause rather than asserted.

**THREE consequences recorded here because they would otherwise surface later as findings.** FIRST, `10_constitution.md:58` states that the moment a gauge is revoked "by composition challenge, gauge challenge, 4-consecutive-disqualified-epoch automatic revocation, or any other path", that pool's AuMT "drops to zero governance weight at that block" — so `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` is ALSO a permissionless governance-weight-zeroing primitive. It is safe under its own predicate, which requires the admission authority to have revoked and then 223,201 blocks to elapse, but a reader meeting the entry cold would reasonably read it as alarming, so the predicate is the thing to state beside it. SECOND, that same line enumerates the 4-consecutive-disqualified-epoch automatic revocation as a live path when PP3.5 measured that it is NOT implemented; `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` is a different predicate and DOES NOT close that gap, and a later reader must not read rung 8 as having done so. THIRD, the `isGaugeEligible` latch stays one-way after this rung: `_checkEligibilityCriteria` is a reverting view and the latch is written by `evaluateEligibility`, so the re-check never touches it, and D.7's certificate-outlives-its-condition finding is untouched and still owned by its own row.

**TWO supersessions, both of this project's own prior text, and both recorded rather than silently overwritten.** PP-D26's emission clause is SUPERSEDED: its Decisions row at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:255` still reads "standing re-check in `recordScore`", which is the defective shape this lock rejects, and that row is amended when this lock lands. The queue's C.6 fix-intent column is likewise superseded and is NOT rewritten — the F-39 ledger row carries the supersession, exactly as F-46 carried PP-D49 (iv)'s. TWO items of documentation debt are ACKNOWLEDGED and deliberately not done here: `docs/STAGE_O_NOTES.md`'s O-D2 execute-time rationale should eventually note the rail-conjunct carve-out, and `recoveryPathAdmitted`, the delay, `deregisterScore` and `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` are absent from published `aumm-site` prose, which is PP5.4's class under PP-D23 rather than a blocker on this rung.

**AMENDMENT (x), user-adjudicated 2026-08-30 at the PP4.8d pre-flight and written before the code it governs: `meetsCompositionQualityGate`'s SIGNATURE DOES NOT MOVE, and clause (iv) is REVISED accordingly.** Clause (iv) anticipated a G16 sweep to PERMIT a signature change; the roster the sweep produced says the correct answer is not to make one. A second parameter `bool railAdmittedSnapshot` on a gate that is `external` on BOTH `IGaugeRegistry` and `IGaugeEligibility` would let any caller pass `true` and take a conjunct-free pass — reopening precisely the back door PB-D69 (x) closed by putting the conjunct inside the gate, so the fix would introduce a hole of the same family as the finding it closes. That governance is the only consumer TODAY does not make it safe: the interface is the security boundary, not the current call set. Instead the conjunct is SPLIT OUT into a new view, `feeRailConjunctSatisfied(address pool)`, returning `rail != address(0) || recoveryPathAdmitted[pool]`. The name is deliberate and `railAdmitted` was REJECTED as misdescribing it: the view is true for any railed pool that was never admitted at all, so a reader grepping for admission would find a function that is mostly not about admission. `meetsCompositionQualityGate` drops the conjunct at `:455` ONLY — `:435` in `_checkEligibilityCriteria` STAYS, that being the activation path clause (iii) does not touch — and `AureumGovernance` reads the view live at `:243` into `Proposal.railAdmittedAtPropose`, then checks the stored bool at `:429` before calling the now conjunct-free gate. PB-D69 (x)'s closure survives because governance is the sole production consumer, VERIFIED rather than assumed: `GaugeRegistry.sol:245` is pure delegation and `AureumGovernance.sol:243` and `:429` are the only call sites in `src/`.

**The G16 roster is THREE KINDS, not one list, and the third kind is the one that bites.** FOUR test mocks are `is IGaugeRegistry` — `test/unit/VotingWeight.t.sol`, `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol`, `test/fork/mocks/CCBMocks.sol` and `test/unit/CCBMultiplier.t.sol` — so an addition to that interface breaks them at COMPILE time, loudly. ONE is `is IGaugeEligibility`, `MockGaugeEligibility` at `test/unit/GaugeRegistry.t.sol:19`, and behaves the same way. The third is `test/unit/AureumGovernance.t.sol:68`, which declares `meetsCompositionQualityGate` with NO `override` and implements no interface at all: the compiler will not force it to gain the new view, but `AureumGovernance` will CALL that view on it, so it fails at RUNTIME in a test whose subject is unrelated. That mock is named here because a G16 roster built only from `is I<Name>` would have missed it, which is the roster heuristic's own blind spot. One further consequence of leaving the signature alone is worth stating as a benefit rather than an accident: `test/fork/StageKIntegration.t.sol:204` mocks by the literal signature string `meetsCompositionQualityGate(address)`, and `vm.mockCall` does not error on a mismatch, it silently stops intercepting — so a signature change would have degraded that fixture without any failure pointing at the cause.

**ONE mechanism superseded and ONE test that inverts BEHAVIOURALLY rather than by selector.** PB-D69 (x) states that the conjunct reverts INSIDE `meetsCompositionQualityGate`; amendment (x) supersedes that MECHANISM while preserving the POLICY in full — a composition challenge still cannot seat a rail-less, unadmitted pool, the check simply moves to governance, live at propose and from the snapshot at execute. And `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol`'s `test_P1_C6_admissionAuthorityAnnulsPassedCompositionChallenge` is a REPRODUCTION, so its inversion at PP4.8 is revert-to-SUCCESS and not a swap of revert selectors: after the fix beat 4 executes, the slot updates, and a passed two-thirds mandate on a candidate that qualified at propose is no longer voidable by a later flag flip. Its NatSpec at `:1250`, which pins beat 4 as the INNER `NoFeeRailAndNotAdmitted` rather than `CompositionQualityGateFailed`, becomes false and is rewritten rather than re-expected. The StageN ixAetheron pin at `:271`, `:273` and `:284` drives the composition gate and moves with `:455`; `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol:1236` and `:1256` are ACTIVATION sites and are unchanged; the `GaugeEligibility.t` PB-D69 block is sorted into composition and activation cases at build time rather than assumed here.

**AMENDMENT (xi), user-adjudicated 2026-08-30 at the PP4.8g2 pre-flight and written before the code it governs: propose REGAINS the fail-fast enforcement that (x) removed from the gate, and the two sites take DIFFERENT errors.** Amendment (x) moved the conjunct out of `meetsCompositionQualityGate`, which left `proposeCompositionChallenge` with no conjunct enforcement at all — a rail-less unadmitted pool clearing the 52% bar, the hook check and factory provenance would be accepted, its 1,000 svZCHF deposit donated irreversibly, a full vote run, and only `_executeProposal` would fail. That is the back door PB-D69 (x) closed, reopened by the fix, and O-D2's fail-fast-at-propose rule exists precisely against it. So (a) `proposeCompositionChallenge` reads `GAUGE_REGISTRY.feeRailConjunctSatisfied(newPool_)` into a local immediately after the existing gate check, reverts when false, and passes the local into `_createProposal`. (b) The propose-time error is a NEW `CompositionRailConjunctFailed(address pool)` declared on `AureumGovernance`: the contract carries sixteen errors and none fits, and it holds ZERO references to `GaugeEligibility`, knowing only `IGaugeRegistry`, so throwing `NoFeeRailAndNotAdmitted` would import a concrete contract it deliberately does not know AND would borrow an error whose NatSpec describes activation-path semantics. Reusing the sibling `CompositionQualityGateFailed` was REJECTED for the reason PP-D49 declared three new errors rather than reusing window errors: the quality gate passed, so naming it would misreport the cause.

**Clause (c): the execute site takes a DIFFERENT error, `RailConjunctSnapshotFalse(uint256 proposalId)`, and the reason is that the two sites do not check the same thing.** Propose evaluates a LIVE predicate on a pool and a failure means the pool does not qualify now; execute reads a STORED bool and, because propose enforces, a false there means a proposal exists that should have been impossible to create. Those differ in kind rather than in timing, and collapsing them would be actively misleading, since a pool can satisfy the conjunct live — a rail added, or `recoveryPathAdmitted` set — while the snapshot stays false. The check goes BEFORE the conjunct-free `meetsCompositionQualityGate` call in the `CompositionChallenge` branch, so an impossible proposal is diagnosed before the pool is re-qualified. ONE SUB-QUESTION was resolved by reading rather than assumed: `_executeProposal(Proposal storage p)` has NO `proposalId` in scope, so it gains one as a first parameter and its single call site passes the value `execute(uint256 proposalId)` already holds. Keying the error on the pool instead was REJECTED as re-introducing exactly the ambiguity the distinct error exists to remove. The error is UNREACHABLE in a correct deployment and that is the point rather than an objection — if it fires, the revert must read as an invariant violation and not as a routine rejection — so its regression drives `_createProposal` with `false` for a composition type through a harness rather than through the public entry. The five non-composition call sites pass `false`, and only the `CompositionChallenge` execute branch ever reads the field.

**AMENDMENT (xii), user-adjudicated 2026-08-30 at the PP4.8h3 pre-flight and written before the code it governs: clause (vii)'s revocation delay is a SCHEDULED TWO-STEP with a PERMISSIONLESS finalize, and `recoveryPathAdmitted` keeps its shape as a public mapping.** FOUR actions define it. `setRecoveryPathAdmitted(pool, true)` writes the mapping true AND clears any pending stamp, so re-admission cancels a scheduled revocation. `setRecoveryPathAdmitted(pool, false)` does NOT write the mapping at all: it stamps `revocationEffectiveBlock[pool] = block.number + REVOCATION_DELAY_BLOCKS`, and REVERTS `RecoveryPathNotAdmitted(pool)` when the pool is not admitted, because a stamp on an unadmitted pool would leave the finalize step writing false over false and emitting a revocation for something never admitted. Re-stamping while a schedule is already pending is LEGAL and is stated here rather than left implicit: it can only move the effective block later, cancellation is the `true` call, and the caller is the same authority either way. `finalizeRecoveryPathRevocation(pool)` is PERMISSIONLESS, requires a non-zero stamp and `block.number >= revocationEffectiveBlock[pool]`, then writes the mapping false, clears the stamp and emits. And the READERS do not change: `_checkEligibilityCriteria` and `feeRailConjunctSatisfied` keep reading `recoveryPathAdmitted` alone, so no delay logic enters a view. `REVOCATION_DELAY_BLOCKS` is the sum of the four `AureumGovernance` constants promoted to `public` at PP4.8h2 and is READ rather than re-derived, per RB-026.

**TWO shapes were REJECTED and clause (viii) lands in the same amendment.** The LAZY alternative — a view returning admitted-unless-a-matured-revocation-exists — is rejected because it moves both consumers off the public mapping AND re-opens the C.6 annulment class: during the delay window the mapping must stay TRUE so a composition mandate snapshotted at propose is still honourable at execute, which is clause (iii)'s entire purpose. An AUTHORITY-ONLY finalize is rejected because scheduling with no public execution path lets a revocation be recorded and then never executed, leaving the pool's status indefinitely ambiguous — structurally the same failure mode as revoking after a vote passes but before it executes, which is what this rung exists to close. **Clause (viii)** splits `setAdmissionAuthority` into `proposeAdmissionAuthority(newAuthority)`, written by the current authority into `pendingAdmissionAuthority`, and `acceptAdmissionAuthority()`, callable only by that pending address, which must be non-zero, and which writes `admissionAuthority` and clears the pending slot. It stays REPEATABLE with no one-shot and no burn per PB-D69 (viii), since a burned authority leaves a dead map no rail-less pool could ever clear, and its storage is separate from the revocation schedule so the two surfaces cannot interfere.

**AMENDMENT (xiii), user-adjudicated 2026-08-30 at the PP4.8h3h pre-flight: clause (vii)'s compile-time read through the `AureumGovernance` TYPE is INVALID SOLIDITY and is WITHDRAWN.** `public constant` on a CONTRACT produces instance getters at runtime; it does not make the member reachable as `AureumGovernance.VOTING_DELAY_BLOCKS` inside a constant expression, and solc rejects it with error 9582, member not found in `type(contract AureumGovernance)`. Only LIBRARY members work that way, which is why `AureumGovernance.sol:28-31` itself reads `AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_DAY`. The compiler caught it at PP4.8h3g's build gate, THREE RUNGS after the lock had already rested on it — a G10 miss, since an access pattern is a language-feature claim and G10 exists to say those are compile-probed before a closure lands on them. The replacement: `GaugeEligibility.REVOCATION_DELAY_BLOCKS` becomes the SAME `AureumTime` expansion governance uses for its own four lifecycle constants, plus one block — `BLOCKS_PER_DAY + BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH + 2 * BLOCKS_PER_DAY + BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH + 1`, still 223,201 at canonical figures — and the `AureumGovernance` import is DROPPED for an `AureumTime` one, which incidentally restores the interfaces-and-libraries-only import property this contract held before PP4.8h3a1 and which was flagged as a break when that rung landed.

**RB-026 is discharged by a PIN, and the pin's FORM is load-bearing rather than incidental.** The test asserts that `GaugeEligibility.REVOCATION_DELAY_BLOCKS()` equals the sum of `AureumGovernance`'s four PUBLIC GETTERS plus one. It must NOT re-expand `AureumTime` test-side: a mirror written that way passes unchanged if someone rewrites `EXECUTION_TIMELOCK_BLOCKS` as `3 * BLOCKS_PER_DAY` in governance while the gauge formula sits still, which is precisely the drift RB-026 names and the only failure this pin exists to catch. **PP4.8h2's promotion therefore STAYS and is NOT reverted**: those getters are what the pin reads, so promoting the four served the alignment after all, at runtime rather than at compile time, and returning them to `internal` would force the weaker mirror. h2 is recorded here as vestigial for the purpose it was built for and load-bearing for this one.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.8N, 2026-09-01 — PP4.8 is BUILT, F-39 and F-60 read `Fixed at PP4.8`, and rung 8 ran to SIXTY commits from `140aec5` to `9f77ce3`.** The lock and its two plan rows (`140aec5`, `c9c3275`, `3b36917`); amendment (x) and the conjunct split into `feeRailConjunctSatisfied` across both interfaces, both implementations and the five mocks (`91315dd` through `11e84df`); PP14 and its index line (`9a59f3e`, `93aeafa`); the composition leg — `railAdmittedAtPropose` threaded through `_createProposal`, the two (xi) errors, the five non-composition sites threading false, propose's fail-fast and execute's snapshot honour (`231924c` through `b1e0155`); E.5's `deregisterScore` (`59b0997`, `064130f`, `bd7dbae`); the four lifecycle constants promoted to public (`2474516`); amendment (xii)'s scheduled revocation and rotation handshake, thirteen rungs from the slots to the inverted dead-end PoC (`e7f37e8` through `996dc8b`); amendment (xiii)'s `AureumTime` mirror and the RB-026 pin (`9710a7e`, `70bea35`, `a2d9e33`, `bf270aa`, `77bf09a`); the emission leg's `GaugeStillEligible` and `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` (`b97591f`, `7c65efd`); the four fork PoCs inverted (`1d0c680` through `df6b6e1`); the two conjunct overrides the propose-time guard turned out to require (`8857ee1`, `8970bf7`); `deregisterScore` proven (`7082341`); and the two ledger rows (`be25ee0`, `9f77ce3`). Gates: **Part-A 1083 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped of 1084 across 90 suites**, the skip being RB-024's deferral as throughout this stage, and **32 of 32 fork files green** on a roster computed FRESH per PP-D46 across the three redeploy units, `PB52SepoliaOracleEvidence` excluded on chain grounds as the tree's only Sepolia-fork test.

**FOUR results not recoverable from the diffs.** FIRST, **the conjunct deletion had to be LINE-anchored and a content anchor would have been ambiguous**: before the edit `meetsCompositionQualityGate` and `_checkEligibilityCriteria` opened with EIGHT byte-identical lines, and the edit itself cut that run to two. Anchoring on the shared text could have matched either, and matching the wrong one would have deleted ACTIVATION's copy — the read clause (ii) requires to stay live — leaving the composition gate enforcing a conjunct it was supposed to stop enforcing and emissions enforcing nothing. The two are not near-duplicates by accident: PB3.12 rung c2 landed the same conjunct at both sites under PB-D69, so the hazard is a direct consequence of the fix this rung amends. SECOND, **`revokeGaugeIfIneligible` is deliberately CONCRETE-ONLY and the file has a precedent for it**: it and `advanceTournament` are the only two public functions `GaugeRegistry` declares that `IGaugeRegistry` does not, and `advanceTournament` is the other permissionless keeper entry, so the new trigger joins an existing convention rather than inventing one. Putting it on the interface would oblige all five `is IGaugeRegistry` implementers for no consumer, `AureumGovernance` knowing only the interface and never calling it. It emits the EXISTING `GaugeRevoked` — two emit sites now — so nothing downstream has to watch a second event to see a demotion. THIRD, **the propose-time guard's real blast radius was every fixture that MOCKS the composition gate, and only running the gates could find it**: `test/whitehat/P1_B1.t.sol` and `test/fork/StageKIntegration.t.sol` each mock `meetsCompositionQualityGate` and went red because the guard reads a SECOND view those mocks did not answer for, which no grep over the changed contract would have surfaced. Twelve test files now declare `feeRailConjunctSatisfied`. FOURTH, **D.7 is untouched and stays its own row.** No PP-D50 clause names the eligibility certificate and the scheduled revocation does not reach it: `isGaugeEligible` still has one write site writing `true` and no branch anywhere writing `false`, so a reader must not take C.6's closure as having closed the latch.

**FIVE process costs, and the last of them is one rule failing in three costumes.** FIRST, **the G10 miss cost three rungs**: clause (vii) specified `REVOCATION_DELAY_BLOCKS` as a compile-time read of `AureumGovernance`'s promoted constants, h3a1 built exactly that, and solc rejected it with error 9582 — `public constant` on a CONTRACT produces an instance getter and does NOT make the member reachable through the type in a constant expression, which is a property libraries have and contracts do not. The analogy to the `AureumTime.BLOCKS_PER_DAY` read three lines above felt safe and was not; amendment (xiii) withdrew the clause and h3h landed the `AureumTime` mirror instead. SECOND, **a §8e.1 carrying two payloads silently produced NOTHING, again**: h3a3 asked for two declaration blocks in one rung, twenty-two lines, and returned no error, no partial write and no file change; split into h3a3a and h3a3b it landed first try each. That is PP4.0 (4) recurring, and the verify block remains the only thing that separates it from success. THIRD, **blank separators were stripped twice**, at h3d and j1, which is PB4.3's known Cursor behaviour; both were repaired by blank-only insert rungs verified with `cat -e`, BSD `cat` having no `-A`. FOURTH, **a rung ends at its COMMIT, not at its verify**: h3i2 was verified green, the test gate was run instead of the commit, failures diverted attention, and the uncommitted save was found two rungs later by a `git diff --stat` that disagreed with HEAD — it landed as `bf270aa` naming both rungs. FIFTH, **PP13's rule failed in THREE costumes across the closing beats**, every save correct and every expectation wrong, each costing one reconciliation round: a payload backtick count asserted about a whole line that also had 34 in its untouched head; an em-dash occurrence count asserted through `grep -c`, which counts LINES; and a file-wide header count written with no pre-edit baseline because the string looked new, when PP-D45's record had opened with the same words since rung 2. The instrument for all three is identical and already written down — measure the FILE in the same command that measures the payload, and use `grep -o` piped to `wc -l` whenever the claim is about occurrences rather than lines.

## PP-D51 — G.2 and G.3 (PP4.9, rung 9): the incumbent frozen at propose, a poisoning that outlives it, and PP-D9's companion

**PP-D51 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-01): rung 9 is G.2 and G.3 with PP-D9's companion, landing as ONE rewrite of the six-line `CompositionChallenge` branch at `AureumGovernance.sol:434-440` plus two lines on the `GaugeChallenge` branch above it, `governance` unit throughout per PP-D30.** (i) **G.2, the incumbent freeze:** `proposeCompositionChallenge` stores `SLOT_REGISTRY.poolAtSlot(slot_)` in `targetPool`, a field that currently passes `address(0)` at `:250` and is therefore FREE for this type, so no sixteenth `Proposal` member is needed; execute then requires `poolAtSlot(p.slot) == p.targetPool` and revokes `p.targetPool` rather than re-resolving the slot. That serialises concurrent challenges BY CONSTRUCTION: B's frozen incumbent is the original occupant, so once A executes, B fails the pin instead of revoking A. (ii) **G.3, the propose gate is `slotOf(newPool_) == 0` and NOT `gaugeStatus == None`, which SUPERSEDES the queue's fix-intent wording** — recorded here in the PP-D49 (iv) pattern rather than by rewriting the column. Composition is about SLOT MEMBERSHIP, not gauge lifecycle, and the attack leaves the candidate `Active` with `slotOf == 0`; a `None` gate would refuse a pre-poisoned candidate its challenge altogether, saving the bond while leaving the High open, since `activateGauge` is permissionless for the anti-spam fee and NO writer anywhere returns a status to `None`. (iii) **The register site becomes a THREE-WAY rather than a skip:** `None` registers, `Active` SKIPS `registerGaugeFromComposition` — the tolerance, sited GOVERNANCE-SIDE so `GaugeRegistry` keeps its external behaviour and the rung keeps ONE redeploy unit — and `Revoked` REVERTS. `Revoked` earns the opposite treatment from `Active` because it is not attacker-reachable: `revokeGauge` is `onlyGovernance` and rung 8's `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` fires only once the rail conjunct has genuinely fallen, so it is a deliberate exclusion rather than a grief bought for 100 svZCHF. (iv) **PP-D9's companion:** the `GaugeChallenge` branch gains `slotOf(p.targetPool) == 0` before `revokeGauge`, because `proposeGaugeChallenge:233` evaluates that predicate at PROPOSE ONLY and a composition challenge executing in between seats a pool into a slot, after which a banked gauge challenge revokes a seated Miliarium pool. (v) **FOUR distinct errors, two of them serving BOTH sites, which is the INVERSE of PP-D50 (xi) and deliberate:** `CompositionIncumbentChanged` at execute for (i); `CompositionCandidateSlotted` at propose AND execute for (ii); `CompositionCandidateRevoked` at propose AND execute for (iii); `GaugeTargetSlotted` at execute for (iv). Amendment (xi) split its two sites because propose read a LIVE predicate while execute read a STORED bool — two different things, so two names. Here both sites evaluate the IDENTICAL predicate on the IDENTICAL value, so a second name would misreport rather than clarify, and a later reader must NOT "correct" this into a split. Both coupling sites carry a comment naming the other, per PP-D9's lock. (vi) **A regression witness is MANDATORY for PP-D9, which carries no queue row of its own:** bank a `GaugeChallenge` against an `Active` unslotted pool, seat that pool through a composition challenge, and assert the banked challenge reverts at execute. Without it PP-D9 is documented and unproven, which is the companion-obligation shape rung 8 met with its own RB-026 pin. Done-criteria: G.2 to `test_challengeIncumbentFrozenAtPropose`, G.3 to `test_challengeSurvivesGaugeActivation`, and PP-D9 to the new case, all three in `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol` per D35.

**CLOSING RECORD at PP4.9j2, 2026-09-02 — PP4.9 is BUILT, F-44 and F-45 read `Fixed at PP4.9`, and rung 9 ran to TWENTY-ONE commits from `3e55e88` to `70770e4`.** The lock and its Decisions row, BOTH landed before any code per the §12 ambiguity gate (`3e55e88`, `ba24441`); the four errors declared (`32f82bf`); the propose gate on slot membership with the incumbent hoisted into `targetPool`, plus the NatSpec forbidding a later tightening to `None` (`404ccda`, `b89ef6e`); PP-D9's companion on the `GaugeChallenge` branch (`8ca58a0`); the `CompositionChallenge` branch rewritten ONCE per PP-D30, carrying the incumbent pin, the candidate re-check, the `Revoked` revert and the three-way register (`dda0175`); the two coupling comments, deferred one rung so neither named code that did not yet exist (`9c536bd`, `7839055`); G.2 inverted, renamed and re-documented (`82470b3`, `007ee38`, `0144e12`); G.3 the same (`53d8cb4`, `416ab0a`, `43c57d1`); PP-D9's mandatory witness and its NatSpec (`1ff857a`, `fa9ea4d`); the unit pin following `targetPool` and the two tests proving the new gates fire (`7b173ae`, `c53e628`); and the two ledger rows (`36e49de`, `70770e4`). Gates: **Part-A 1085 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped of 1086 across 90 suites**, the skip being RB-024's deferral as throughout this stage, and **16 of 16 fork files green at 146 tests**, on a roster computed FRESH per PP-D46 for `AureumGovernance` alone, with `PB52SepoliaOracleEvidence` excluded on chain grounds.

**SIX results not recoverable from the diffs.** FIRST, **the queue's fix intent was SELF-CONTRADICTORY and resolving it meant deciding which half survived**: `gaugeStatus == None && slotOf == 0` at propose CANNOT coexist with tolerating an already-`Active` candidate at execute, because a `None` gate rejects a pre-poisoned candidate at propose and execute is never reached, leaving the tolerance dead code. The slot predicate is the half that closes the High, and PP-D51 (ii) withdrew the other rather than refining it. SECOND, **`Revoked` earns the OPPOSITE treatment from `Active`, which turned the register from a two-way skip into a THREE-WAY** — decided in the lock, before any code, and the asymmetry is a capability claim rather than a preference: `Active` costs an attacker 100 svZCHF through a permissionless `activateGauge`, while `Revoked` is reachable only through `onlyGovernance` `revokeGauge` or a `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` whose rail conjunct has genuinely fallen. THIRD, **TWO of the four errors serve BOTH propose and execute, which is the INVERSE of PP-D50 (xi) and deliberate.** Amendment (xi) split its sites because propose read a LIVE predicate while execute read a STORED bool; here both sites evaluate the IDENTICAL predicate on the IDENTICAL value, so a second name would misreport rather than clarify, and a later reader must NOT "correct" this into a split. FOURTH, **the two propose gates had ZERO tests until PP4.9i2 and no fork witness could have supplied them**: G.3 proves only the PERMISSIVE direction, that an already-`Active` candidate is accepted, while nothing anywhere drove `CompositionCandidateSlotted` or `CompositionCandidateRevoked`. An untested guard is indistinguishable from an incidental revert, which is PP-D49 (i) in another costume. FIFTH, **the only thing that flagged `targetPool` changing meaning was a unit test asserting it was EMPTY**: `test_proposeComposition_happyPath_depositsAndRecords` failed on a real address against `address(0)` the moment the field started carrying the incumbent. No grep over the changed contract would have surfaced it, because the field was being pinned precisely for being unused. SIXTH, **the fork roster is 13 by the exercise relation plus 3 declared margin, and the margin was MEASURED unnecessary.** `DerBodenseeScriptWitness` and `StagePRunRehearsal` only MENTION `StagePIntegrationFixture` in comments, both standalone per PB-D25, so the first drops and the second is in the roster on a different edge, driving `DeployStageP.run()`; `DeployStageG`, `StageGIntegration` and `StageIIntegration` hold no governance instance and all three passed, so the relation held rather than the margin saving it.

**FIVE process costs, and the first is different in kind from the rest.** FIRST, **an expected value in a USER VERIFY block was FABRICATED and asserted as measured.** At PP4.9g1 the em-dash line count was written as unchanged at 3, with the words measured just now rather than assumed attached to it; the true count was 30, and no measurement had been taken — a range figure of 2 from an earlier rung had been carried across into a file-wide claim. The save was correct and the check's SHAPE was sound, unchanged before and after, so nothing broke; what failed is that a number with false provenance reads exactly like one with true provenance, which is PB22 arriving from the drafting side. The rule adopted mid-rung and held for the remaining fourteen sub-steps: every number in a verify block is measured in the tool call IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING it, and where a value has not been measured the block says so and asks for it rather than supplying a figure. The rule then failed a SECOND way inside this very paragraph: its own character count was measured, the wording was revised afterwards, and the stale figure shipped, three bytes adrift. So the measurement must be of the text actually handed over and not of a draft that preceded it. SECOND, **a SINGLE one-line payload produced NOTHING, which CORRECTS the account PP4.8 left behind**: that record blamed the silent no-op on a rung carrying two large payloads, and PP4.0's original entry says the same, but PP4.9h1b was one line replacing one line and still returned no error, no partial write and no file change. The trigger is therefore broader than either record states, and a re-issue phrased as a literal two-string replacement, the old line in full and the new line in full, landed first try. THIRD, **three commits were skipped and every one was caught by checking rather than by reading the paste**: at d2, h1c and h2b the verify output was returned a second time in place of the commit result, which is indistinguishable from success unless `git log` is actually read. This is PP4.8's h3i2 lesson, that a rung ends at its commit and not at its verify, and the fix that stopped the recurrence was structural — emit the commit block ALONE, with no dispatcher line beneath it, so exactly one thing is there to copy. FOURTH, **blank separators were stripped again, at h2a**, the fourth instance in this stage; repaired by a blank-only insert rung verified with `cat -e`, and the sixty-line PP-D9 witness that followed carried seven blanks through intact once the payload's blank count was stated as an explicit check. FIFTH, **putting `git diff --stat` FIRST in every verify block is what made the no-op announce itself**: an empty diff line is unambiguous, where the same failure inferred from two greps at the bottom of a block reads as a puzzle. That ordering held for the rest of the rung.

## PP-D52 — D.5, D.1, D.3, D.6 and D.4 (PP4.10, rung 10): the sample floor, the decay that ignored elapsed time, the venue that degrades, and a fold that must not revert

**PP-D52 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-02): rung 10 is the PP-D31 keeper chain, five rows across THREE redeploy units — `governance` for D.5, `ccb` for D.1, D.3 and D.4, `tvl-oracle` for D.6 — in the locked order D.5, D.1, D.3, D.6, D.4.** (i) **D.1, the sample floor:** `EMASampler` gains a per-pool `sampleCount` mapping, incremented on every successful update including the cold-start seed, and `IEMASampler` gains its getter. Maturity measured in TIME cannot distinguish two samples sixty days apart from sixty daily ones, which IS the finding, so a derivation from `emaSeedBlock` alone was rejected as unable to express it. (ii) **`MIN_SAMPLES` is 60**, written as a literal on `EMASampler` with a NatSpec citation to `432_000 / 7_200`, the existing maturity window in days. NO new constant is adjudicated, per PP-D43's fifth obligation and the PP-D49 (iii) precedent, and it is NOT read from `EmissionDistributor` or `VotingWeight`: those declare `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS` as `internal constant` on a CONTRACT, which solc will not expose through the type, which is PP-D50 (xiii)'s G10 miss and must not be repeated. (iii) **The triplication is NOT cleaned up here.** `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS` and `EMA_STALENESS_BLOCKS` already exist in three copies, at `EmissionDistributor.sol:57` and `:60`, `VotingWeight.sol:39` and `:41`, and `IncendiaryRegistry.sol:44` and `:47`; consolidating them into `AureumTime` is RB-026's remedy and becomes a register item, because five rows across three units is enough for one rung. (iv) **D.3, the decay:** a BOUNDED CATCH-UP LOOP applying the F-4 step `min((block.number - last) / BLOCKS_PER_DAY, CAP)` times against one spot read, with `CAP = 60` numerically aligned to `MIN_SAMPLES` rather than adjudicated separately. The closed form over `LogExpMath.pow` is the named fallback if a later gas probe on the folded `claim` shows sixty iterations too heavy; it is not preferred now because it adds a dependency `EMASampler` does not have. (v) **D.5 ships THREE of its four clauses.** Clause 1 holds the previous checkpoint rather than writing zero, clause 3 folds sampling onto the economically motivated path, and clause 4 makes `_createProposal` refuse a bond at `totalSupply() == 0` — verified unbuilt at HEAD, `_createProposal` at `:219` carrying no revert and no supply read, so F-21's guard exists ONLY at `_voteSucceeded:387` and this is a THIRD site, distinct from the `VaultClassRegistry.vetoProposal` denominator that B.7 carries at rung 11. **Clause 2, restricting third-party pokes to position-caused reductions, is EXPLICITLY DEFERRED to rung 12**, because it is B.2's push-reset and implementing half of it here would fight that rung. (vi) **D.6 is 6a ONLY:** try/catch on the three unguarded external reads in `_venueRatio` at `TVLOracle.sol:328`, `:329` and `:330`, each degrading to `(0, false)` with a `VenueReadFailed` event. That extends an idiom the function already runs at `:325`, `:327` and `:332` rather than introducing a return vocabulary, and try/catch is valid inside an `internal view` because all three are external calls. **6b is DEFERRED** — the sampler stamp semantics, keeper-alive versus sample-valid — unless a post-6a reproduction still shows the stamp freezing, since once sibling venues skip, a healthy pool's `tvl()` succeeds and the second PoC face disappears. `NoElectorate`, a venue-removal primitive and an N-block bounded skip are out of scope and stay in the done-criteria text. (vii) **THE FOLD USES `updateEMAIfDue`, NEVER A BARE `updateEMA`, and this AMENDS PP-D31's fold bullet.** `updateEMA` reverts `TooEarly` before `last + BLOCKS_PER_DAY`, and both fold targets are permissionless and repeatable within a day, so a bare call would revert the SECOND `claim` or `recordScore` of any day on that pool — a liveness regression strictly worse than the idle staleness being fixed. A shared internal core serves both entries: `updateEMA` keeps its revert for the keeper set and for the 88 `updateEMA(` bindings under `test/`, five of which assert `TooEarly` directly in `EMASampler.t.sol`; `updateEMAIfDue` returns `(false, tvlEMA[pool])` when not due. Fold placement is fixed here so no sub-step guesses it: in `recordScore` at `:537` it runs AFTER `_accrueGlobal` and BEFORE the `_gatedTvlEMA` read at `:541`, and in `claim` at `:691` after `_accrueGlobal`. Per-site eligibility branching was REJECTED as RB-026's propagation class, and a non-reverting `updateEMA` was rejected for silently retiring the cadence contract those tests pin. (viii) **D.4** puts a `_gatedTvlEMA`-shaped helper on `CCBMultiplier`'s three ungated reads at `:213`, `:228` and `:232`; PP-D12's "redeploys with D.8" is redeploy BATCHING, not a rung-10 blocker, so D.4's source lands here and D.8 stays at rung 14.

**AMENDMENT (ix), user-adjudicated 2026-09-02 at the PP4.10c pre-read and written before the code it governs: clause (v)'s D.5 clause 1 is HOLD-ONLY, its revert half is WITHDRAWN as barred by an existing lock, and the hold is per-holder and downward-only.** FIRST, the queue's fix intent offers "hold the previous checkpoint OR revert on staleness", and the second disjunct is FORECLOSED: `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:185` records INV-8 under PP-D16, that `poke` cannot revert because every read is a storage getter or an unguarded view, and it is preserved precisely because B.2's push-reset at rung 12 depends on it; `poke` carries zero reverts at HEAD. An implementer must not reach for that disjunct. SECOND, `_positionPower` becomes `(uint256 power, bool staleZero)`, with `staleZero` set ONLY on the staleness branch at `VotingWeight.sol:168` and never on the maturity branch at `:167`, the zero-LP short-circuits, or the F-17 read-cap — every one of which legitimately yields zero and must keep doing so. It is `internal view`, so `IVotingWeight` is untouched and the change stays inside the `governance` unit. THIRD, `poke` ORs `staleZero` across the roster enumeration and applies shape (A prime): it returns BEFORE the writes at `:144` to `:151` when `newWeight < oldWeight` AND any pool contributed a staleness zero, which is exactly the ratchet, while an upward move and a non-staleness downward move both write as before, so recovery and genuine withdrawal are unaffected and a new qualifier can still enter during an outage. The two rejected shapes are recorded rather than left implicit: skipping EVERY write while any pool is stale also blocks upward writes, and a per-pool-per-holder mapping adds hot storage for a case the scalar policy already covers. The accepted conservatism is that a partial recovery yielding a lower weight while some pool is still stale keeps the old checkpoint, overstating against truth until full recovery or a non-staleness decrease — which is what "hold the previous" means and is strictly better than banking a zero denominator into history. FOURTH, this does NOT encroach on clause 2, which stays at rung 12: (A prime) keys on the CAUSE and bars a staleness-driven reduction from every caller including self, whereas clause 2 keys on the CALLER and restricts third parties to position-caused reductions. Strictly stricter, non-overlapping, and it leaves B.2's recorder push and PP-D18's caller attribution entirely to that rung.

**AMENDMENT (x), user-adjudicated 2026-09-02 at the PP4.10f pre-read and written before the code it governs: clause (ii) fixed WHERE `MIN_SAMPLES` lives but not HOW a consumer reads it, and the answer is an interface getter.** The gap is the same foreclosure PP-D50 (xiii) hit and clause (ii) itself warns about, arriving a third time: `MIN_SAMPLES` is a `public constant` on the CONTRACT `EMASampler`, while both D.1 consumers hold an `IEMASampler` reference — `EmissionDistributor._gatedTvlEMA` at `:522-527` and `VotingWeight._positionPower` at `:174-182` — so `_emaSampler.MIN_SAMPLES()` does not compile. `IEMASampler` therefore gains `function MIN_SAMPLES() external view returns (uint256);`, which the production contract satisfies for free through the auto-generated getter its `public constant` already emits, exactly as `tvlEMA`, `lastEMAUpdateBlock`, `emaSeedBlock` and `sampleCount` are satisfied. THE INTERFACE GETTER IS THE ONLY WAY A CONSUMER MAY OBTAIN THE THRESHOLD, which is what clause (ii) left unsaid. The three `MockEMASampler` implementations each declare it `pure` returning 60, the same ripple `sampleCount` carried at PP4.10e3a. TWO shapes were REJECTED and are recorded rather than left implicit. A per-consumer `MIN_SAMPLES` constant would deepen precisely the `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS` triplication clause (iii) declined to fix, adding a fourth and fifth copy of a figure that already has three. And moving the predicate into the sampler as `isMature(pool)`, though it needs one member rather than two and would let a mock answer without faking sixty samples, splits maturity policy across two contracts: both call sites still own seed presence, seed age and staleness locally, so the sampler would hold one slice of a gate story the consumers hold the rest of. `CCBMultiplier` stays OUT of D.1 scope; its three ungated reads are D.4 under clause (viii).

**AMENDMENT (xi), user-adjudicated 2026-09-03 at the PP4.10g pre-read and written before the code it governs: clause (vi)'s `VenueReadFailed` event is WITHDRAWN as unemittable, the three degradations are SILENT `(0, false)` exactly like the three skips they extend, and the observability gap that leaves is a register obligation rather than a rung-10 build.** THE FORECLOSURE IS IN THE LOCK'S OWN TEXT: clause (vi) places the event inside `_venueRatio`, which is `internal view` at `TVLOracle.sol:323`, and emitting an event is on solc's documented list of state-modifying operations a `view` function may not perform. The property holds for the whole chain, not one frame — `_constellationRatio` at `:249`, `_directRatio` at `:263`, `_twoHopRatio` at `:298` and `_venueRatio` at `:323` are all `internal view`, and `tvl` at `:359` is `external view override` against an `ITVLOracle` that declares it `view` at `src/ccb/ITVLOracle.sol:14`. `VenueReadFailed` was never built: it has ZERO hits in `src/`, `test/` and `script/` at HEAD and appears only in this lock, its plan-side Decisions row, the queue's D.6 fix-intent cell and the plan's D.6 row, so the withdrawal removes nothing from code and costs no ripple. TWO ways out were weighed and the second is REJECTED. Dropping the event makes the three new degradations indistinguishable from the established skips at `:325`, `:327` and `:332`, which already return `(0, false)` with no signal, and that is the right consistency: a caller cannot tell today why a venue was skipped and D.6 does not change that, while the try/catch itself stays valid inside a `view` because all three targets are external `view` calls, as clause (vi) already states. Making the chain non-`view` so it could emit was rejected as a cascade through the read paths: `GaugeEligibility._checkEligibilityCriteria` at `:500` is `internal view` and reads `tvl()` at `:509`, so every `view` consumer of the oracle, and `ITVLOracle` itself, would have to surrender that qualifier to carry a diagnostic. THE LOSS IS REAL AND IS RECORDED RATHER THAN GLOSSED: a venue that reverts persistently becomes invisible on chain, so the queue's "removal primitive or bounded skip for a venue reverting for N blocks", which clause (vi) already scopes out, has NO on-chain signal to key on, and the eventual remedy needs an observability answer before it can be specified. The one candidate named, and NOT chosen here, is a non-`view` diagnostic entry on the oracle that re-walks a single venue and emits; the sampler cannot supply it, because `EMASampler._update` at `:185` is not `view` but sees only the summed `tvl()` and no per-venue detail. That gap is filed at the rung's register beat beside clause (iii)'s triplication item, as the class a `view` pricing mean cannot escape — degradation without a diagnostic — with 6b and the N-block bounded skip named as the remedies that wait on it. Clause (vi)'s scope is otherwise UNCHANGED: 6a only, the three reads at `:328`, `:329` and `:330`, and the done-criteria case `test_revertingVenueDegradesNotBricks` stands. ONE obligation on that case is fixed here so the regression proves the finding and not the double: the reproduction raises its throw from `getNormalizedWeights`, the third read, through the double at `test/whitehat/P1_D6.t.sol:23`, while its own NatSpec at `:17-18` records that production's throw originates in a third-party `getRate()` reached inside `getPoolData`, the second — so the regression must drive the `:329` read to revert as well as the `:330` one it inherits, or it is PB19 in a test costume, a property borrowed from the case that happened to be easy to build.

**AMENDMENT (xii), user-adjudicated 2026-09-03 at the PP4.10h pre-read and written before the code it governs: clause (viii) covers BOTH faces of D.4, the helper's thresholds come from `AureumTime`, a gated read of the pool's own EMA is a third revert gate, and the global baseline goes per-pool.** FIRST, scope. Clause (viii) named the helper alone, while the plan's D.4 row at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:125`, the queue's fix intent and the PoC's second case all carry a second face — a per-pool cadence at `CCBMultiplier.sol:206-207` guarding one global baseline written at `:246`, so the second updater in an epoch compares the current aggregate to itself and loses its global step, a one-STEP_SIZE gap the PoC pins exactly. Both faces live in ONE contract of ONE unit and both land here. The queue's third item, the distributor falling back to neutral when `lastMultiplierUpdateBlock` is old, is DEFERRED to rung 14 with D.8, where `CCBMultiplier` and `EmissionDistributor` are both open: it belongs in `getMultiplier` on the `ccb` unit rather than in the distributor, and its window is a new constant PP-D43's fifth obligation requires adjudicating before it exists. SECOND, the helper: `_gatedTvlEMA(pool)`, `private view` on `CCBMultiplier`, mirrors `EmissionDistributor._gatedTvlEMA` at `:522-532` check for check — seed presence, seed age, the sample floor read through `MIN_SAMPLES()` per amendment (x), staleness — and replaces all three raw reads at `:213`, `:228` and `:232`. THIRD, thresholds. `CCBMultiplier` declares neither `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS` nor `EMA_STALENESS_BLOCKS`, and the three copies at `EmissionDistributor.sol:57` and `:60`, `VotingWeight.sol:39` and `:41`, `IncendiaryRegistry.sol:44` and `:47` are constants on CONTRACTS, unreachable through the type (G10, PP-D50 (xiii)). `AureumTime` gains `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS = 60 * BLOCKS_PER_DAY` and `EMA_STALENESS_BLOCKS = BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH`, each with a value pin and a relation pin in the existing `test/unit/AureumTime.t.sol` form, and `CCBMultiplier` reads them from the library, which a library exposes through its type where a contract does not. The three contract copies stay byte-identical; this advances clause (iii)'s deferral by landing the library home and leaves RB-026 exactly three aliasings. A fourth copy was REJECTED as the class the register tracks, and sampler-side getters were rejected on amendment (x)'s own ground: the consumers own maturity policy. FOURTH, the pool's own read. A gated zero at `:232` is a THIRD revert gate, `EmaNotReady(address pool)`, in the existing order — (1) membership, (2) cadence, (3) readiness — evaluated once immediately after the cadence gate with the value reused at the intra comparison, writing nothing and consuming no cadence, exactly as `TooEarly` behaves. A zero fed to the intra comparison would read as a pool far below the mean and step it UP by `STEP_SIZE` every epoch, the mirror image of the distortion being fixed; F-D19's neutrality rule is no signal, no step. A silent no-op was rejected because the contract emits no event, measured at zero `event` and zero `emit`, so a keeper could not tell a held pool from an updated one. The two aggregate reads do NOT revert: a gauge or Miliarium pool that fails the gate contributes zero to its sum. FIFTH, the baseline. `lastProtocolAggregateEMA` becomes `mapping(address => uint256)`, written per pool at `:246` and read per pool at `:217`, so each pool measures the global trend over its own cadence window, and the F-D18 cold-start sentinel becomes per-pool: a pool's first update yields `deltaGlobal = 0`. The epoch-keyed alternative, one global slot re-keyed at most once per epoch, was rejected because it needs a genesis anchor the contract does not hold and its first updater still races the slot. The public getter changes arity: 14 call sites in `test/unit/CCBMultiplier.t.sol` and 2 in `test/fork/StageJIntegration.t.sol` gain the pool argument, their expected values carrying over since a pool's baseline after its own update IS the sum it saw; `ICCBMultiplier` exposes `getMultiplier` alone and is untouched; no script reads the slot. SIXTH, the blast radius, measured per I13 rather than predicted: 58 `updateMultiplier(` call sites across six files. `test/unit/CCBMultiplier.t.sol`, 44 tests, and `test/whitehat/P1_D8.t.sol` share a `MockEMASampler` at `test/unit/CCBMultiplier.t.sol:40-75` that stamps `sampleCount` on first write but leaves `emaSeedBlock` at zero and returns whatever `setLastEMAUpdateBlock` set, once, in one test, so under the gate every read is zero and every call reverts at gate (3). The PP4.10f3 remedy extends: on first `setTVLEMA` the mock also stamps `emaSeedBlock = 1`, and `lastEMAUpdateBlock` returns `block.number` when unset so the one explicit setter keeps its meaning; both fixtures raise `START_BLOCK` from `200_000` to at least `EMA_MATURITY_BLOCKS + 1` so a block-one seed is mature. `test/fork/StageJIntegration.t.sol`, three calls, and `test/fork/CCBEngine.t.sol`, one, seed the REAL sampler once and update; each needs a sixty-sample daily maturation loop before `updateMultiplier`. `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol`, three calls, matures through `_matureStack` at `:568-585`, which is TWO samples, a seed and a refresh across one `432_000` roll — a shape D.1's floor at PP4.10f2 ALREADY invalidates for the `recordScore` at `:584`, unmeasured because no fork gate has run since f2. The same sixty-sample loop inside `_matureStack` closes both; six fixtures inherit it, so its end block stays `seed + 432_000`. The D.1 fork ripple is recorded here as a rung-10 carry that is NOT D.4's: `WK18ThinVenuePumpSim` samples 21 days, and any fixture below sixty samples now reads zero at both consumer gates. SEVENTH, formal: `formal/act/ccb_multiplier.act:47` declares `uint256 lastProtocolAggregateEMA := 0` and the residual prose at `:90-97` states a raw, ungated sum against a single stored baseline; both become false and are rewritten to the mapping and the gated, per-pool form, with `formal/RESULTS.md` carrying the PENDING-REATTESTATION marking per the PP4.1 precedent. No `prove_` function covers `updateMultiplier`, residual per PB-D17 (ii), so no proof is falsified. EIGHTH, D.8: gated zeros inside `miliariumAgg` change what the literal 28 at `:231` divides; D.4 does not touch the divisor, and rung 14 must decide whether its walked-count divisor counts gated-out pools. Recorded as an input to D.8, not resolved here. NINTH, bookkeeping: the queue's D.4 done-criteria gains a second case for the second face, `test/whitehat/P1_D4.t.sol::test_perPoolBaselineGivesEveryUpdaterTheGlobalStep`, beside `test_multiplierGatesOnEmaMaturity`; both PoC cases invert; the NatSpec at `CCBMultiplier.sol:71`, which says the contract calls `tvlEMA` and `lastEMAUpdateBlock`, is corrected to the five reads the helper makes; and the walks now make five external calls per gauge where they made one, a gas rise measured at the gate rather than bounded here.

**PP4.10h3f-carry — D.5's StagePIntegration reproduction is stale, not a D.4 regression, and stays open for its own closure.** The full `StagePIntegration.t.sol` fork suite ran for the first time since PP4.10c/d landed the D.5 fix, and one of its 24 cases failed: `test_P1_D5_staleEmaLetsAnyonePermanentlyRatchetTheElectorateToZero` at `:1619`, asserting `vw.governanceWeight(voter) == 0` after a stale-window poke, actual `1989191237476477889`. That is the fix working exactly as amendment (ix) specifies — `poke` now HOLDS the checkpoint rather than writing zero — hitting a case whose own NatSpec at `:1615-1618` already says it "runs against PRE-FOLD code" and reproduces "the unconditional zero-write," and whose queue row at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_QUEUE.md:66` names a DIFFERENT done-criteria case, `test_staleEmaHoldsCheckpointNotZero`, never written. No commit between `c2eed7f` (PP4.10a) and this one touched `StagePIntegration.t.sol` for D.5; `git log --grep` confirms it. Twenty-three of twenty-four cases in the same run passed clean under the new gate, three of them exercising `CCBMultiplier` directly (`test_legD_ccbMonthWalkEvolvesMultiplier`, both `P1_C5` cases, `test_swapLegFeesReachTheHook`), so this is isolated to D.5's own uninverted PoC. Closure is D.5's, not D.4's: invert `test_P1_D5_staleEmaLetsAnyonePermanentlyRatchetTheElectorateToZero` to the queue's own `test_staleEmaHoldsCheckpointNotZero` name, asserting the held nonzero weight and the surviving nonzero denominator, at the rung's docs-and-inversion beats alongside D.1 and D.3's own inversions.

## PP-D53 — B.7, B.8 and B.5 (PP4.11, rung 11): the veto fraction, the read-time rule, and one amendment to PP-D18

**PP-D53 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-04): rung 11 is the reader-side group, three rows in ONE redeploy unit (`governance`) with no hook dependency, and it ships TWO fixes rather than three.** (i) **B.5 IS THE READ-TIME RULE ALONE:** `VotingWeight._positionPower` changes its F-17 read-cap at `:198-199` from `if (held < lp) lp = held;` to `if (held < lp) return (0, false);`. The cap handles the SHARE and never the CLOCK, which is the whole finding: after the cap a desynced holder still reads `effectiveQualBlock` at `:187-190` and applies `timeFactor` at `:205-208`, so an out-of-band mover keeps a mature clock on their residual BPT while an honest `recordWithdrawal` has already been reset to zero at `:675-678`. The row's "~12.9x" is that ratio and NOT a share ratio: `ON_RAMP_PERIOD_BLOCKS / QUALIFICATION_PERIOD_BLOCKS` is `1_296_000 / 100_800`, 12.857, a full on-ramp against a just-cleared fourteen-day cliff. `P1_B5.t.sol:29` states the same disposition in its own NatSpec, that both findings close via this one rule. (ii) **PP-D18's `_syncDown` CONJUNCT IS AMENDED AWAY, and this is a NAMED-DECISION amendment rather than a queue supersession.** PP-D18 at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:247` reads "`_syncDown` resets the clock only on full drain (B.5)", and that clause is WITHDRAWN. It is canon-hostile under PP-D43, not merely queue-hostile: `04_tokenomics.md:105` reads "Any withdrawal resets everything to zero" and "even 1%", so `_syncDown:612` matching `recordWithdrawal:678` IS the honest path, and a full-drain-only sync would let a synced partial exit keep seniority the honest partial withdrawal loses — direction B returning in a new costume. The clause was folded in from a different subject: constraint 10's own body at `:187` governs who may write a CHECKPOINT downward (B.3, B.2 and D.5's poke rule), while the clock is a different quantity, and D.5's conjunct is already largely discharged by PP-D52 (ix). The precedent class is PP4.10's amendment (xi), a lock clause withdrawn because the tree contradicts it, and NOT PP-D49 (iv), which superseded a queue cell. TWO bookkeeping corrections ride here: the repo's "§viii withdrawal reset" label is NUMBERING DRIFT, the canonical paragraph sitting under the heading at `04_tokenomics.md:99` which is section ix, so cite I-D14 plus L105 rather than the section number; and the residual this leaves is NEW rather than the filed finding — after the read-time rule a desynced holder already carries zero live weight, so no stranger can destroy live weight one block before a snapshot, and what survives is that buying BPT back to `held >= lp` would restore the old clock without passing the recorder, which `syncPosition` can still convert into a sticky reset. Harm drops from destroying live weight to denying a buy-back self-heal; it is RECORDED and does not drive a `_syncDown` rewrite. (iii) **B.7 SHIPS ALL FOUR REMEDIES, and the fourth is not optional.** `VaultClassRegistry.vetoProposal` at `:297-301` banks ABSOLUTE `governanceWeight` across the window and divides by a LIVE `votingWeight.totalSupply()`, so a falling denominator carries an already-banked numerator over threshold, a `weight == 0` caller still sets `hasVetoed` and still re-runs the comparison for free, and at zero supply the division PANICS so every veto reverts. The remedies are: bank the vetoer's FRACTION at their own call; reject a zero-weight veto BEFORE the `hasVetoed` write; an F-21-style zero-denominator guard on the veto path; and `finalizeProposal` at `:308-320` REFUSING when the window elapsed at zero supply. The fourth is what actually closes "the class finalizes unopposed", which `NOTES:230` records as F-21's condition at a second call site: a veto-side revert cannot close it, because `finalizeProposal` reads no supply today and at zero supply nobody can bank a fraction anyway. **THE DISPOSITION IS FAIL-CLOSED — no vote means NOT approved** — so an admission whose finalize window elapses entirely at zero supply becomes unfinalizable and dies at the existing `FinalizeDeadlineExpired` deadline rather than admitting by default. (iv) **THE FRACTION IS BANKED IN 1e18 FIXED-POINT, not basis points.** The queue says "fraction" and not "bps", and a bps accumulator truncates downward against a `VETO_THRESHOLD_BPS` of 1000, which can zero a real slice of a legitimate coalition; 1e18 makes the truncation negligible while the threshold stays expressible as `VETO_THRESHOLD_BPS * 1e14`. (v) **B.8 SHIPS NO CODE.** F-34's disposition column already reads `Accepted-risk` and PP3.3b is the authority — capability rather than conduct, the corrective being permissionless, with the gain measured at `P1_B8.t.sol:94` and its self-heal at `:124`. The exponent is ALREADY applied at read time inside `_positionPower:209-211`; the laziness is only that `governanceWeight` and `getPastVotes` read stored checkpoints. TWO artifacts are therefore stale and are marked rather than edited: the done-criteria cell `test_eraExponentAppliedAtReadTime` is VESTIGIAL, a PP3.1 guess at a fix PP3.3 declined, and the fix-sequencing line at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:213` still reads "B.8 read-time exponent" against that verdict. "At minimum document" is discharged by this paragraph. (vi) **B.7 DOES NOT CLOSE ON ITS OWN TERMS.** PP-D14 at `:243` requires B.7 to inherit the invariant that B.1's rung-12 redesign must state, since `totalSupply()`'s meaning changes there and B.7's call-time fractions are measured against it; the fraction-bank ships now and the check is inherited at rung 12. B.1's replacement invariant is NOT invented here. (vii) Ordering: PP-D12 governs B.5 and B.8 as the PP4.0 redeploy map and is NOT a shape constraint, and B.7's only ordering cell is PP-D14.

## PP-D54 — B.1, B.2 and B.3 (PP4.12, rung 12): PP-D17 resolved, PP-D14 amended, B.1 reopened onto PP-D23

**PP-D54 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-05): rung 12 is the electorate trio, THREE High rows in ONE redeploy unit (`governance`), and it ships ONE fix, B.2, because PP-D17 defers B.3's build to the hook unit and B.1's close leaves this decision entirely.** (i) **PP-D17 IS RESOLVED: record the seed, ONCE, for both consumers.** `poolTotalLP` stays the share denominator on BOTH sides, governance `share` and emission `poolAccRewardPerLP`, and is FILLED at initialize rather than swapped for `IERC20(pool).totalSupply()`. The row's two options were never equal: exclusion makes the consumers agree by both omitting seed and then the first trusted-router depositor still owns the pool, failing the recommendation's second half; the queued `share / totalSupply()` is the third path constraint 9 at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:186` exists to forbid, governance and emission apportioning the same position differently. Implementation is NOT this rung's. `getHookFlags` at `AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:339-341` sets after-swap, after-add and after-remove only; recording seed is `shouldCallAfterInitialize` plus a recorder credit, which constraint 11 at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:188` already names as rung 15's hook-unit item "if PP-D17 says so", beside B.6 because both are attribution questions about who the LP is on a Vault callback. A TYPED-DOMAIN LOCK is owed BEFORE any `onAfterInitialize` §8e.1 and is absent from PP-D17's one-liner: who is `user` on the seed `recordDeposit` — the initialize recipient, a protocol dead address or the seeder EOA; whether that position pokes and votes; and whether emissions accrue to it or that share is stranded in `poolAccRewardPerLP`. Deciding record-through-hook while leaving `poolTotalLP` in place PRESERVES agreement between the consumers; what it leaves until rung 15 is first-depositor ownership, which is deferred B.3 exactly as the queue already reads it. The batching argument for folding initialize into this rung is EMPTY: generation 4 has not broadcast, so PP-D12's second-hook-version cost is post-deployment rather than per git rung, and the hook is already dirty from PP4.6. (ii) **B.3 IS DEFERRED TO RUNG 15, and of its three fix-intent limbs only the share formula's NAME is withdrawn.** `test/whitehat/P1_B3.t.sol::test_shareDividesByBptTotalSupply` names the branch (i) declines and goes, on the same principle as B.1's contested name: a PoC is not inverted onto a denominator the decision just rejected; that limb is also HALF STALE by rung 11's own doing, since its `min(userLP, balanceOf)` term IS the F-17 read-cap PP4.11c replaced with `return (0, false)`, and `lp = held` no longer occurs in `src/governance/VotingWeight.sol`. The second limb, `poolTotalLP <= totalSupply`, becomes a rung-15 pin once seed is recorded, asserting AGREEMENT rather than a ceiling — and asserting it AT REST, after `syncPosition`, because B.3's own flash-inflation face lets `poolTotalLP` exceed `totalSupply` until a stranger heals it, so an unconditional equality would be falsified by a legitimate transient. The third limb, the third-party-poke rule, is PP-D18's surviving half and binds in (vii). B.3's verdict cell stays `—`. Resolving PP-D17 LIFTS THE OPEN-DECISION HOLD on A.1 — its ordering cell names PP-D10, locked at rung 3's row, PP-D17, and PP-D24, a Stage-R build item rather than an open row — but does NOT write A.1's verdict; that write is a named PP3.3 bookkeeping beat, A.1 stays `—` until it, and this decision's census movement is B.1 ALONE. (iii) **PP-D14 IS AMENDED, and the amendment withdraws its first clause's PREMISE rather than satisfying it.** PP-D14 at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:243` requires B.1's redesign to "state the invariant that replaces INV-1", wording that presupposes the recorder-side aggregate denominator — the queue's primary remedy, which breaks INV-1 by construction. THE AGGREGATE IS WITHDRAWN, on a ground sharper than PP-D14's denial-primitive argument: `_positionPower` depends on `timeFactor` and live EMA state, so ANY stored checkpoint is stale-low from the block after it is written, and a holder entered at `recordDeposit` is parked at immature — often zero — weight until someone pokes; a write-side push does not even reach the truth it would cost INV-1 to buy. With the aggregate gone, INV-1 is RETAINED, not replaced: `poke` writes `_holderWeight[holder]` and `_totalQualifiedWeight` in lockstep and pushes both traces in the same transaction (`VotingWeight.sol:153-160`), so `Σ getPastVotes == getPastTotalSupply` holds by construction and B.1 NEVER FALSIFIES IT. The inflation refutation is re-run against that retained identity and holds a fortiori: PLAN L77's citation is REWRITTEN from the deleted F-17 cap (`:180-181`) to PP4.11c's `return (0, false)` at `VotingWeight.sol:204`, which dominates `lp = held` at every input. B.7 inherits THAT check — call-time fractions banked against the same checkpointed reader — and NOT an absolute veto floor, which after PP4.11 would be a new clause about a live per-call denominator. B.1's CLOSE IS REMOVED FROM THIS DECISION. This is a named-decision amendment in PP-D53 (ii)'s shape on PP-D18, recorded at the row rather than by rewriting it. (iv) **B.1's PP3.3b FIX IS REOPENED INTO DEFERRED ON PP-D23 — the C.1 / D.7 / H.1 pattern — and it is NOT an Accepted-risk.** What B.1 falsifies is the GLOSS that `getPastTotalSupply` denotes the qualified electorate, and that gloss is canon's own denominator: `10_constitution.md:43` under §xxvii, "20% of total qualified voting power", restated at `04_tokenomics.md:117`, whose purpose sentence at `:119` is B.1 in one line — the floor "prevents a small coordinated group from pushing through structural changes while the broader LP community is inactive." F-9 at `11_formulas.md:216` is sharper still: `holder_LP / pool_total_LP` is "total, not qualified-only: weight forfeited by holders still inside the qualification period vanishes rather than redistributing", whereas a poked-subset denominator does the OPPOSITE for mature-unpoked LP, redistributing their share of the 20% bar to whoever enrolled — `test_P1_B1_solePokedHolderCarriesTheTwoThirdsBarWhileTheRestOfTheLpNeverEnters` is that redistribution. "Qualified" in canon is F-9's predicate — gauged pool, mature EMA, past the fourteen-day cliff — and never "has a checkpoint". §xxix at `:198-203` carries no quorum floor and no denominator definition; its one governance hit, `vetoThreshold ≤ governanceQuorumThreshold` at `:183`, is a RELATION among bars. The Low-Turnout Safeguard therefore sits OUTSIDE PP-D43's protected set and is still canon through `04_tokenomics.md` as a citing source, and where code and canon disagree the CODE yields unless PP-D23 adjudicates that divergence the other way — the H family's business and no other rung's. PP-D23's worklist GAINS a divergence: checkpointed-subset `getPastTotalSupply` versus "total qualified voting power". PP5.4 decides whether the spec yields, poke-enrolment becoming the operational definition of qualified, or the code yields to a later bounded construction. Three candidate closes were REJECTED and must not return: an absolute floor on `totalVotes` or `snapshotSupply` is a SIZE bound the 10% whale already clears — B.1 is a representation defect, not a dust defect — and promoting it would both re-litigate the `quorum-denominator-has-no-absolute-floor.md` file F-28 records as NOT cited and invent a constant neither §xxvii nor §xxix contains, PP-D43's a-rung-that-cannot-name-a-canonical-clause-STOPS case; a read-time denominator over the qualifying set is unbounded iteration in E.7b's condemned shape; and keeping Fix with companions alone while the PoC still passes is a dishonest Fixed. B.1's done-criteria name `test_quorumDenominatorCountsUnpokedHolders` stays contested, now on PP-D23 rather than PP-D14. (v) **B.4 AND G.4 PARK WITH B.1.** The composite rule at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_NOTES.md:228` — a composite defers until EVERY constituent locks, its closure path breaking if any constituent resolves away from Fixed — puts B.4, Critical, closing via B.1 + B.3 + C.4, behind PP-D23 beside PP-D17 and PP-D25, and gives G.4, closing via C.2 + G.2 + B.1 + a global challenge rate limit and already deferred on C.2's PP-D22, a SECOND constituent off Fixed. The census moves for neither; the closure path does. That is correct, not a reason to keep a fake Fix. (vi) **PP-D31's FOLD SET IS NOT RECORDED AS THE CONFORMANCE PATH.** Folding `poke` into the recorder enrols economically ACTIVE LP; the safeguard's purpose is that INACTIVE qualified LP still sit in the denominator so 20% is hard. ## PP-D55 — B.2 (PP4.12, rung 12): the build shape — per-pool weight storage, the revocation limb withdrawn, the staleness classification reordered

**PP-D55 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-06): B.2's build shape, settled before any code — the fork closes on PER-POOL WEIGHT STORAGE, two limbs of the queued fix intent are withdrawn, and the recorder gains a typed pointer it does not have today.** (i) **THE REFERENCE RUNS ONE WAY, SO B.2 IS A TWO-CONTRACT CHANGE.** `VotingWeight` holds `IEmissionDistributor public immutable RECORDER` at `src/governance/VotingWeight.sol:59`, taken as constructor argument `recorder_` at `:94` behind a zero-check at `:100` and assigned at `:105`; `EmissionDistributor` holds NO `VotingWeight` typed slot and every hit in that file is NatSpec. The order is proven by the scripts rather than by a decision code — `new EmissionDistributor(` at `script/DeployStageH.s.sol:173` and `new VotingWeight(` at `script/DeployStageK.s.sol:106` — so a reverse pointer CANNOT be an immutable and a storage slot plus a setter is forced. K-D9 does NOT say this and must not be cited for deploy order; it is the `aumm.setMinter(router)` decision per `docs/STAGE_K_PLAN.md:50`. The queue's `redeploy unit` cell reads `governance` and is NOT relabelled — the fix edits `governance` and `emission-stack`, and the divergence is recorded here and at F-29 on PP4.8's precedent, where three units were recorded against PP-D40's one-unit vocabulary rather than the cell being rewritten. (ii) **THE FORK IS LOCKED AS PER-POOL WEIGHT STORAGE, because a whole-registry recompute RELOCATES B.2 rather than closing it.** An `onPositionClosed` sharing `poke`'s loop inherits the hold at `:152`, and `anyStaleZero` is an OR over the entire Miliarium enumeration: a holder who exits pool A while still recorded in a stale pool B reads `(0, false)` for A at `:188` under (iv)'s reorder and `(0, true)` for B at `:186`, so `newWeight == 0 < oldWeight` with the flag set and the write is REFUSED — the checkpoint keeps `A_old`, and a closed position still inside `getPastVotes` and `getPastTotalSupply` IS B.2's harm. The shield is PORTFOLIO-SELECTABLE rather than incidental: after the reorder ANY `eqb != 0` in a stale pool arms the flag, sub-cliff or dust alike with no need to clear F-9, so one wei left recorded in a neglected pool covers every other close. It is defender-clearable by `updateEMA(B)` then a `poke` while the EMA can still move, and NOT clearable under D.6, where `tvl()` reverts so `updateEMA` cannot run — `_positionPower` reads the STORED `tvlEMA`, so D.6 does not revert the view, it only blocks the heal. NO patched recompute survives: keeping `:152` keeps the shield; ignoring it and force-writing banks B's stale zero, which is the D.5 defect PP-D52 (ix) exists to prevent; and skipping stale pools without stored parts cannot split `oldWeight` into `A_old` and `B_old`. (iii) **A LIVE TARGETED SUBTRACT IS NOT A CHEAPER EXACT PATH, and its failure is what makes the map load-bearing.** Subtracting `_positionPower(A, holder)` before the clock zero handles A-fresh-B-stale with no new state, but returns 0 when A ITSELF is stale and leaves `A_old` banked — and the EXITED pool being stale is the original `:186` finding rather than a corner. It also couples to call order, having to run before `src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol:678`, and its `A_live` differs from the banked `A_old` by whatever drift accrued since the last poke, which is an underflow surface against the aggregate. Once B.2 must close with A stale, `A_old` must be in storage and the live subtract is redundant. (iv) **THE `:186` / `:188` REORDER IS B.2's OWN EDIT AND IS NOT OPTIONAL, including under (ii).** `:186` is the ONLY `(0, true)` in `_positionPower` and `:188` is the closed-position test `eqb == 0`, so today a withdrawn holder in a stale pool never reaches the classification that would let the checkpoint ratchet down; the function's own NatSpec at `:172-173` already says every non-staleness zero is a legitimate absence that must still ratchet the checkpoint down, and the ordering is what prevents that sentence from being reachable. Moving the `eqb` read and its test above `:186` is a two-line move. It does not weaken D.5 — a holder with a LIVE position in a stale pool still reaches `:186` and is still held, and the reduction PP-D52 (ix) guards is one CAUSED BY a stale EMA, which a closed position is not. It also narrows a second over-freeze rung 10 could not see: today a single stale pool anywhere in the enumeration arms `anyStaleZero` for holders who have NO position in it, freezing every downward poke protocol-wide, and after the reorder only holders actually recorded in a stale pool are held. Without this edit a stranger's heal stays broken for the whole outage window even under (ii). (v) **THE REVOCATION LIMB IS WITHDRAWN.** The queued limb names the revocation branches, which `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:82` identifies as `revokeGauge` — `src/gauge/GaugeRegistry.sol:181-186`, with the PP-D50 (v) permissionless twin `revokeGaugeIfIneligible` at `:193-199`. BOTH take a pool, flip `_gaugeStatus`, drop `_activeGauges` and emit; NEITHER is holder-keyed, `GaugeRegistry` has zero `VotingWeight` or `IVotingWeight` hits, and there is no holder enumeration anywhere, so pushing checkpoints for a revoked pool's holders is the unbounded iteration PP-D54 (iv) rejected for B.1 in E.7b's condemned shape. It is also unnecessary: `_positionPower:175` returns `(0, false)` on an unapproved gauge, so revocation already zeroes that pool's contribution at READ time and only the checkpoint lags, which a poke clears. Building it would make B.2 a THREE-unit rung by adding `gauge`. Withdrawn as a fix-intent supersession recorded at F-29 in PP-D49 (iv)'s shape, not by rewriting the queue. (vi) **BOTH RECORDER HOOKS ARE REQUIRED AND THE PAIR IS NOT REDUNDANT.** `_syncDown` returns at `:607` when `recorded <= referenceBalance` and only then zeroes the clock at `:612`; `recordWithdrawal` passes `balanceOf(user) + amount`, the PRE-debit live total, so an honest trusted-router exit NO-OPS `_syncDown` and the clock and debit land separately at `:678` and `:681`. Hooking only `_syncDown` therefore misses the ordinary exit, and hooking only `recordWithdrawal` misses the out-of-band paths, `_syncDown` having four callers — `syncPosition` `:620`, `recordDeposit` `:632`, `recordWithdrawal` `:667` and `claim` `:698`.
