(vii) **THE DISTRIBUTOR'S `VotingWeight` SEAT IS `setMintRouter`-SHAPED: `onlyGovernance`, `ZeroAddress`, `AlreadySet`, one-shot, zero ILLEGAL.** The sibling is `src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol:280-285`; `VaultClassRegistry.setVotingWeight` at `src/gauge/VaultClassRegistry.sol:235-240` is the same type with a different seal, burning `votingWeightSetter` at `:239` rather than testing the value slot, and `setMintRouter` is the closer copy because it carries no dedicated setter role. PP-D44's two-step is REJECTED here: it exists because `address(0)` is a legal H-D29 valve for the incendiary registry, whereas a zero `VotingWeight` pointer would silently disable the push-reset and regress B.2. This is NOT E.9, which is `EfficiencyOracle.setEmissionsRecorder` at `src/emission/EfficiencyOracle.sol:158-162`, a different contract, unvalidated and multi-shot — so seating this slot does not occupy rung 15's work; it is the same CLASS one contract over, and answering it in `setMintRouter`'s shape is what keeps it that way. (viii) **THE MAP'S SHAPE AND THE INVARIANT THAT MAKES THE SUBTRACTION SOUND.** The new state is a per-holder per-pool weight mapping on `VotingWeight`, written INSIDE both of `poke`'s existing guards — the equality short-circuit at `:146` and the staleness hold at `:152` — because a write outside them would zero stale B's stored part while the aggregate is held, and the sum invariant would die on the spot. The invariant is that the per-pool parts sum to `_holderWeight[holder]` at `:73`, and it needs its OWN test: it is what makes the subtraction sound, and it is NOT a restatement of INV-1, which is the cross-holder identity. Only non-zero parts are written and a close DELETES the slot rather than writing zero — a lock clause and not an implementation detail, since materialising 28 zeros on a first poke is the difference between a bounded and an unbounded first touch. The honest bill is that `poke`, a PP-D31 FOLD SET entry meant to stay cheap, pays up to one cold SSTORE per contributing pool on a first touch, while the EXIT path where B.2 actually sits pays one SLOAD and two SSTOREs plus the two history pushes. (ix) **`onPositionClosed` DOES NOT CONSULT `anyStaleZero` AND IS A DIFFERENT ENTRY FROM `poke`.** It reads the stored part for that pool, subtracts it from `_holderWeight` at `:73` and `_totalQualifiedWeight` at `:75`, deletes the part, and pushes both histories at `:77` and `:79`. It makes NO oracle call, so it cannot revert on a hostile EMA and PP-D16's storage-only arm is honoured directly rather than through a wrap — the wrap PP-D16 also permits is UNUSED for this entry, which must not be read as PP-D16 being weakened. INV-8 is "poke cannot revert" and is untouched, `poke` keeping both early returns. An early return when the stored part is zero avoids a pointless pair of history pushes. Same-block closure is what closes the second PoC: `getPastVotes` is `upperLookup` at `:120` and OZ `Trace208` overwrites on an equal key, so a zero written in the withdrawal transaction is what `getPastVotes(holder, withdrawalBlock)` returns, whereas `test_P1_B2_withdrawalAtTheSnapshotBlockCannotBeCounteredByALaterPoke` rolls to `withdrawalBlock + 1` before its stranger poke and therefore cannot. (x) **PP-D52 (ix) IS REFRAMED, NOT AMENDED.** It shipped PER-POOL hold semantics on a PER-HOLDER flag, an approximation invisible at rung 10 because nothing then subtracted one pool's contribution while holding another's; B.2 is the rung that discovers it, and (viii)'s map is what makes those semantics expressible. No clause of PP-D52 (ix) is withdrawn — the hold, the `staleZero` flag and their D.5 rationale all stand, and (iv) only lets a closed position be classified before the flag is reached. (xi) **`castVote` STAYS OFF THIS BEAT** per PP-D54 (ix): that function is touched ONCE, and the live-weight re-gate and the zero-weight revert are decided at the build beat after the recorder paths above are read, not here. (xii) **BOOKKEEPING THIS DECISION OWES.** PLAN: this row in the Decisions table, and `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md:82`'s `src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol:579-598` corrected to `:666-685`, that range now being `deregisterScore` rather than `recordWithdrawal`, with `revokeGauge` noted as `GaugeRegistry`'s. Queue: B.2's cells are NOT edited — the redeploy-unit divergence and the withdrawn revocation limb are recorded at the ledger row per (i) and (v). Ledger: F-29 records both, and moves to `Fixed at PP4.12` when the build lands. NOTES: the B-family harness paragraph gains the two-contract note at the rung's close. §11 at the rung's close. (xiii) **AMENDMENT, written before the code it governs: `poke` CLEARS a part that falls to zero, and (viii)'s "only non-zero parts are written" means only that a zero is never written where the stored part is ALREADY zero.** The rule is that `poke` writes a pool's part if and only if the new value DIFFERS from the stored one, and a new value of zero is written as a `delete`. Without it the invariant dies silently and then fatally: a holder who leaves pool A out of band, or whose gauge is revoked, has `_holderWeight` ratchet down on the next `poke` while A's stored part keeps its old value, so the parts no longer sum to the aggregate — and the next `onPositionClosed(A, holder)` subtracts that stale part from an aggregate that no longer contains it and UNDERFLOWS, reverting on the liquidity exit path, which is the one place (ix) exists to keep revert-free. The first-touch bound (viii) buys is untouched, since a pool the holder was never in has a stored zero and a new zero and is therefore not written. (xiv) **AMENDMENT, written before the code it governs, and recording a production gap the rung's first twenty-eight commits left open: the sink is SEATED BY `DeployStageK._wire`, as wire (5) immediately after (4) `EmissionDistributor.setMintRouter`, under the same `onlyGovernance` principal, and the chain runs to TEN calls.** At `f15ae4a` the tree carried ZERO call sites for `EmissionDistributor.setVotingWeight` in `script/` and `test/` — every `setVotingWeight` hit is `VaultClassRegistry`'s same-named setter, wire (1) — so the production spine left `votingWeight` at zero, both push sites at `src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol:636` and `:715` no-op behind their unbound guard, and B.2's remedy was dead on chain. That is PP-D46's `DeployStageL` shape behind a sharper mask: there the accept entry was absent from a script that proposed, whereas here a grep for the setter's NAME returns hits, the sibling's, which is why a name grep read as coverage. The same absence is why Part-A is green at HEAD with the fix unexercised — no fixture binds the sink, so `onPositionClosed` has never executed in any test, and both B.2 PoCs still PASS for that reason alone. (vii)'s own NatSpec already places the bind at Stage K after `VotingWeight` is constructed against the recorder; what was never given a rung is the script line that performs it. Position is not load-bearing, K-D9 keeping emission-layer governance at the multisig through the handoff, so (5) could sit anywhere in the chain; it sits beside (4) because the two are the distributor's only wiring calls. Witnesses: `test/fork/DeployStageK.t.sol` gains a case asserting `emissionDistributor.votingWeight()` reads the deployed handle, on `test_B_governanceHandoff_gaugeRegistryVCR`'s shape; `test/fork/StagePRunRehearsal.t.sol` gains a post-condition on `test_postCondition_incendiaryRegistryBoundAfterAccept`'s shape, `DeployStageP.run()` composing `DeployStageK.deploy` and the rehearsal being the spine's only in-process replay; `test/fork/StageKIntegration.t.sol` STAYS UNBOUND by design, constructing `VotingWeight` directly at `:49` and pinning the pull-only poke semantics in `StageKVotingWeightPokeTest`, the push path being proven on the StageP chain where the seat lands through the script. After dissolution the setter is one more `onlyGovernance` distributor entry with no proposal type, stranded in C.2's sense but already SPENT at K, so it is a note for PP5.1 under PP-D22 and not a row. The detection rule joins the lesson index at the rung's close: a new one-shot setter is proven seated by grepping its EVENT — `VotingWeightBound` has zero hits outside `src/` at `f15ae4a` — or its contract-qualified call, never its bare name, which a same-named sibling setter satisfies. (xv) **AMENDMENT, written before the code it governs: the rung's TEST ARCHITECTURE, four decisions taken at the PP4.12af pre-flight after the recorder paths and both PoC fixtures were read.** FIRST, the per-pool map's READER is a test harness and NOT a public getter: `test/unit/harness/VotingWeightHarness.sol` inherits `VotingWeight`, adds `extHolderPoolWeight(address holder, address pool)` over the `internal` mapping and nothing else, on the `EmissionDistributorHarness` H-D34 pattern of a pass-through constructor with no added state and no override — so every existing case in the fixture it retypes is unaffected by construction. A concrete-only public getter is REJECTED on D.7's ground: `isEligible`, `isGaugeEligible` and `lastSnapshotEpoch` are that row's Info downgrade for having ZERO readers in `src/` and `script/` outside the getter's own body, and a `holderPoolWeight` view would be a fourth, since (ix)'s subtraction reads the mapping directly and no production caller wants the parts. SECOND, the sum invariant (viii) requires lands in `test/unit/VotingWeight.t.sol` beside INV-1's `test_Poke_TotalSupply_InvariantHolds` at `:383`, with that fixture's `vw` retyped to the harness, and NOT in the whitehat file, which stays typed `VotingWeight`: `MockRecorder` at `:100` exposes `setUserLP`, `setPoolTotalLP` and `setEffectiveQualBlock` per pool, so one pool's part can be moved while the others are pinned, which the real-distributor fixture cannot do without minting and recording through `MockBpt`. The parts-sum identity is NOT INV-1 restated per (viii), so the two cases sit adjacent and neither replaces the other. THIRD, the queue's `castVote` limb, its defence-in-depth re-gate on live weight, is WITHDRAWN — recorded at F-29 in (v)'s shape and not by rewriting the queue. PP-D54 (ix) reserves that function for exactly ONE touch and (xi) deferred the choice to this beat; with the push-reset writing a same-block zero per (ix), a live-weight re-gate would fight the F-06 snapshot read `getPastVotes(msg.sender, p.snapshotBlock)` at `src/governance/AureumGovernance.sol:346` rather than defend it, and the zero-weight revert is B.1's participation face, parked on PP-D23 with that row. `castVote` is UNTOUCHED this rung and its single touch stays available to whichever rung earns it. FOURTH, PP-D16's fork witness attaches to `test/fork/RouterIntegration.t.sol`, not to a new file and not to `StageKIntegration`, which (xiv) keeps unbound: it already drives a REAL seated Router through `removeLiquidityProportional` at `test_PostSeat_RouterRemoveDecrementsRecorder`, and per PP10 its whole inheritance chain is shim-free, `RouterIntegrationTest` extending `StagePIntegrationFixture`, which extends `Test` directly under P-D36's explicit no-shim policy — so the hostile EMA is built by the D.5 regression's own outage shape, one `BLOCKS_PER_EPOCH` past the last `updateEMA`, with a stranger's HELD poke as the positive control before the exit. The done-criteria case `test_withdrawalClearsVotingWeight` needs NO PP-D42 path correction: `test/whitehat/P1_B2.t.sol` already wires the real recorder against the real reader with `initialGovernance_` at the test contract, so it seats the sink itself and drives the close through `recordWithdrawal` inside the unit tree.

## PP-D56 — rung 13 (PP4.13): four rows, the producer moves to rung 15, the tournament splits in two, the cap re-clamps at the head of settle, and the registry gains an immutable back-reference

**PP-D56 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-08): rung 13's scope and the build shape of every row in it, settled before any code.** (i) **SCOPE IS FOUR ROWS, NOT FIVE.** E.9's verdict cell reads `—` and the row is held by **PP-D22**, which the plan still marks **OPEN** and schedules at PP5.1, where it also gates rung 17. E.7a, E.7b, E.7c and E.8 ship here; E.9 does not, which is B.1 and B.3's shape from rung 12 one family over. The plan's order-13 sequencing line still lists E.9 among its items and is STALE exactly as order 12's was before PP-D54; it takes a marker rather than a rewrite, on that precedent. (ii) **PP-D34 IS SETTLED RATHER THAN UNLOCKED, AND ONLY ITS RUNG MOVES.** Carrying no NOTES heading is NORMAL for the PP-D10—PP-D37 band, which the plan indexed and locked at sub-step rather than by adjudicated section, and PP-D34 also carries no OPEN marker, so it is an ordering constraint and not an open design. Its constraint stands unchanged — wiring `setFeeRecorder` and fixing the G17 C3 numerator-versus-denominator vintage are ONE rung — and this decision amends only WHICH rung: **15, beside the producer**, not 13. (iii) **E.7a: THE PRODUCER STAYS OFF THIS RUNG, THE SKIP LANDS REGARDLESS, AND THE DECIMAL CLAUSE NARROWS TO A PIN.** Measured rather than inferred: `setFeeRecorder` has ZERO call sites in `script/`, and `recordFees` has ZERO callers anywhere in `src/`, only unit tests calling it, so no production contract produces fee data and every efficiency numerator is structurally zero on chain. "Wire the feed" is therefore not a script line but a PRODUCER that does not exist, and the natural one is the swap and yield route inside the hook callbacks that B.6, C.8, C.9 and D.2 already rewrite under constraint 11's single-hook batch; splitting it 13-then-15 would be two constructor ripples on one contract for no deploy reason, since no broadcast separates them, while the batch is what keeps the producer from being forgotten as rung 15 rewrites those same functions. Seating the hook as `feeRecorder` WITHOUT a `recordFees` call would be **PP15 in its exact form**, a named setter that greps as coverage while the numerator stays zero. The skip is also the only limb that survives the dissolution handoff, `setFeeRecorder` being among C.2's stranded authenticated writes, so a wiring line is unreachable afterwards where a skip inside `computeEpochSnapshot` is not. **THE DECIMAL ITEM IS NOT A BUG AT HEAD and the queue phrase is about a caller that does not yet exist:** `recordFees` already treats its argument as Balancer `amountScaled18`, its NatSpec says so, and `quoteSvZCHF` multiplies by a 1e18 ratio, so there is no scaling defect in `EfficiencyOracle`. What the phrase names is a PRODUCER obligation, that whoever later calls it must pass Vault `balancesLiveScaled18` units rather than raw 6- or 8-decimal amounts. Rung 13 therefore adds NO decimals helper and at most a NatSpec pin that the argument is scaled18 and that a raw-amount caller is a defect; enforcement belongs to the producer at 15. Only an audit file naming a DIFFERENT mismatch reopens this clause. What rung 13 owns is confined to `GaugeEligibility` and `EfficiencyOracle`: the `numeratorSma == 0` skip beside the existing denominator skip, placed BEFORE the ratio computation and BEFORE the address tie-break, plus that pin. Done-criteria `test_zeroNumeratorSkippedSymmetrically` is a rung-13 artifact; wiring and vintage need a rung-15 case, and rung 13 does NOT invert E.7a's ranking PoC into a live-feed tournament, having no feed to drive. (iv) **E.7b: TWO-PHASE IS FORCED, THE ROW'S ALTERNATIVE IS NOT ONE, AND PAGES MUST INDEX RATHER THAN COPY.** Cap tiers are percentile bands over the FULLY ranked set — `favoredCount`, `cap10Count`, `cap50Count` and `cap100Count` all derive from `nRanked` and the tier test compares each index against `nRanked` minus a count — so no page can assign a cap until both the size and the order of the whole set are known. Bounded-work selection of three tier boundaries instead of a full sort does NOT remove pagination: the measured cost is about 38,238 gas PER POOL, 1,152,749 at 30 and 3,447,068 at 90 extrapolating to roughly 34M at 890, and that is the ORACLE-READ loop rather than the insertion sort, so quickselect still walks every pool in ONE transaction and still exceeds a 30M block; PP-D31 already placed `advanceTournament` in the funded keeper set and a keeper cannot pay it either. **`advanceTournament` STAYS PERMISSIONLESS** and is SPLIT rather than gated; no `onlyGovernance` is added. **PAGES MUST USE `at(i)`, NEVER `_activeGauges.values()`**, since that copy is the same unbounded walk in another costume. The persistent state is: on `GaugeRegistry`, a `tournamentCursor` holding the next `at` index and an `accumulationEpoch` holding the epoch in progress with zero as idle; on `GaugeEligibility`, a storage scratch for the ranked list carrying pool, ratio and the two SMAs the transition events already emit, plus `nRanked` and a matching `accumulationEpoch`. The entries are a permissionless `accumulateTournament(uint256 maxPools)` and a permissionless `finalizeTournament()`. The first accumulate of an epoch applies today's `TournamentNotActive` and `TournamentEpochNotElapsed` guards and sets `accumulationEpoch`, but does NOT stamp `lastTournamentEpoch`; later pages require `accumulationEpoch == currentEpoch` and `cursor < length`; finalize requires `cursor == length`, so every Active gauge has been visited INCLUDING those the skip paths `continue` past, then assigns caps, stamps `lastTournamentEpoch`, and clears the scratch and cursor. Incomplete accumulation is unfinalizable BY THE CURSOR CHECK and not by a second boolean. Today the stamp is written at `GaugeRegistry.sol:240` BEFORE any work, which a revert rolls back but a successful first page would burn, and moving it to finalize is what makes the split safe. Rewriting G-D23 (iv) and T-T3's address-stable ordering is DECLINED in the same breath: pagination discharges the gas finding and a new selection algorithm is a second defect surface. (v) **E.7c: A LIVE RE-CLAMP AT THE HEAD OF `_settlePool`, NOT A CHECKPOINT AND NOT AN ALLOCATION CLIP.** The clamp at `EmissionDistributor.sol:576-584` solves share-against-cap using a LIVE `totalScore` in the caller's block and then WRITES the result into `poolScore`; that write IS the latch, and when other scores later fall the realised share exceeds the cap, which is both PoC faces. A per-epoch `totalScore` checkpoint is REJECTED because it keeps a latch and only changes which frozen denominator was chosen, leaving the second face untouched since intra-epoch movement continues after the checkpoint. Clipping `poolAllocation` at settle is REJECTED on a sharper ground: without a remainder path it BURNS the excess where F-16 requires redistribution pro-rata to uncapped pools per the contract's own comment and spec L125, and the score-side clamp achieves that only because the clamped wei never enter `totalScore` at all, whereas an allocation `min()` rebases debt against the full accumulator and STRANDS them, which is H-D24 stranded allocation and canon-hostile under PP-D43 unless the lock invents a remainder path, which it declines to do. **THE INSERTION POINT IS THE HEAD OF `_settlePool`, BEFORE `poolAllocation = deltaAcc.mulDown(poolScore)`, and this is load-bearing rather than stylistic**: `recordScore` already settles and THEN writes the new score, so with the uncapped reshape written after settle that interval still allocates the OLD score, which is today's behaviour, and the new score is realised at the NEXT settle, which re-clamps first. That is the latch breaking. A clamp at the TAIL of settle would allocate the uncapped value once, which is E.7c again. The clamp signed-deltas `totalScore` in lockstep with `poolScore`, including on a stranger's `syncPosition`. There are SIX direct `_settlePool` sites and not the four the H-D21 NatSpec names — `recordScore` at `:561`, `deregisterScore` at `:599`, `_syncDown` at `:626`, `recordDeposit` at `:660`, `recordWithdrawal` at `:695` and `claim` at `:731` — and `_syncDown` is additionally reached from `syncPosition` and from deposit, withdrawal and claim BEFORE their own settle, so those three paths settle twice, which is harmless while the clamp is idempotent. `deregisterScore` still zeros after settle, so a clamp on a score about to be cleared is wasted and fine. Running on deposits, withdrawals and claims is THE POINT rather than extra surface, those being the realisation paths that credit `poolAccRewardPerLP`. This clause does NOT split the rung: same contract, same formula, one insertion. (vi) **E.8: A CONSTRUCTOR IMMUTABLE, A CURSOR CONFIRMATION THAT IS NARROWER THAN THE QUEUE'S ADVERB, AND THE REGISTRY FACE REPRODUCED HERE.** `IncendiaryRegistry` holds NO distributor reference, all seven mentions in the file being NatSpec, and `buyBoost` reads no binding at all. Deploy order runs Stage H before Stage L, so the distributor exists when the registry is constructed and the reverse pointer CAN be `IEmissionDistributor public immutable DISTRIBUTOR` behind a `ZeroAddress` guard, with no setter, no seating and no PP15 exposure — the INVERSE of B.2's case one rung earlier, where the order ran the other way and forced a storage slot plus a one-shot, and that contrast is why the two shapes differ rather than an inconsistency. The guard is `DISTRIBUTOR.incendiaryRegistry() == address(this)` inside `buyBoost`, which after F.1 reads the live slot including `address(0)` when unbound. The arity change from 8 to 9 ripples through base-constructor invocation, which solc reports as error 2973 and which no `new IncendiaryRegistry(` grep follows per PP-D44's lesson, so it is priced first: SIX files, ONE inheritor and FOUR construction sites at HEAD — the declaration in `src/incendiary/IncendiaryRegistry.sol`; the sole `is IncendiaryRegistry` inheritor `test/unit/harness/IncendiaryRegistryHarness.sol`, which fails 2973 if skipped; `new IncendiaryRegistryHarness(` in `test/unit/IncendiaryRegistry.t.sol` and in `test/whitehat/F07_emaStalePricing.t.sol`; `new IncendiaryRegistry(` in `test/fork/StageLIntegration.t.sol` and in `script/DeployStageL.s.sol`. The production compose through `DeployStageP` and the rehearsal reach it VIA `DeployStageL` and are not a fifth construction. Re-grep at the edit beat; this is the HEAD set. **THE CURSOR LIMB IS CONFIRMED IN A NARROWER SENSE THAN THE QUEUE WORDS IT.** The write `poolBoostCursor[pool] = block.number` sits OUTSIDE `if (boostDue > 0)` and INSIDE `if (registry != address(0))`, and the NatSpec's "advancing unconditionally" means even when `boostDue == 0`, NOT even when unbound: while the registry is unbound the cursor still FREEZES. That freeze is correct and must not be rebuilt into an unbound advance, which would skip a window buyers had already paid for; after the `buyBoost` guard no new sales occur, and F.1's clamp still bounds a rebind of the pre-unbind remainder. So this limb is confirmed against the quoted guard rather than against the queue's looser adverb, and no cursor code is written. The registry-side face was left unreproduced at PP3.2 for a HARNESS COLLISION, two files each declaring a `MockGaugeRegistry`, and not for scope, so this rung REPRODUCES it and then inverts it; shipping only the distributor-side cursor cases would leave the done-criteria `test_buyBoostRequiresLiveBinding` a name with no `buyBoost` anywhere in its file. (vii) **BOOKKEEPING THIS DECISION OWES, AND WHY F-62 CLOSES AT 13 DESPITE THE PRODUCER MOVING.** F-62 reads `Fixed at PP4.13` because the skip closes the STATED harm, the collapse to address order that clamps the highest-addressed pool: a first tournament in which every numerator is zero now skips every pool, and a skipped pool keeps its prior cap, which defaults to zero and therefore uncapped, strictly better than clamping by address. Producer and vintage are F-10 LIVENESS, owned by the PP-D34 amendment at rung 15, and are not leftover F-62 work. F-63, F-64 and F-65 likewise move to `Fixed at PP4.13` as their rows land; F-66 stays `Confirmed` and deferred. Queue: E.7a's `GaugeEligibility.sol:296` citation is corrected to `:365`, line 296 now being `finalizeRecoveryPathRevocation` after PP4.8's ripple, which is F-29's drift class recurring; no other queue cell is edited and E.9's row is untouched. PLAN: this row in the Decisions table, a PP-D34 amendment marker recording that its rung is 15, and a marker on the order-13 sequencing line recording E.9's deferral. NOTES: this section. §11 at the rung's close. (viii) **AMENDMENT, written before the code it governs: BOTH skips move AHEAD of the cold-start stamp, which adopts E.7b's relocation limb and settles the ordering (iii) and (iv) between them left open.** The queued E.7a skip and the queued E.7b limb "move the zero-denominator skip ahead of the cold `SSTORE`" pull against each other inside ONE loop, because the stamp at `GaugeEligibility.sol:357` sits between the oracle read and both skip sites; the lock as first written addressed neither side of that, and this clause is what makes the loop expressible. **THE ORDER AFTER THE EDIT IS: oracle read, `numeratorSma == 0` skip, `denominatorSma == 0` skip, cold-start stamp, warmup gate, ratio, insertion into the ranked list.** Both skips therefore precede the ratio AND the address tie-break, which (iii) requires, and also precede the stamp, which the E.7b limb requires. **`firstTournamentEpoch` CHANGES MEANING and the NatSpec is restated rather than left to drift:** it becomes the first epoch in which a pool had USABLE data rather than the first epoch in which it was seen, which is what the smoothing window was always for, since a pool with no numerator or no denominator has accumulated nothing to smooth and a grace clock started against absent data measures nothing. G-D23 (v)'s documented precedence — cold-start, then warmup, then zero-denominator — is superseded by the order above and its NatSpec at `:337` is rewritten in the same edit. TWO consequences must not be re-derived. A pool whose feed goes live at rung 15 takes the cold-start branch then waits `SMOOTHING_EPOCHS`, a BOUNDED three-epoch delay and not a stall, and it does NOT repeat: the stamp is written only when the slot is zero, so a pool that later loses its feed is skipped while KEEPING its grace epoch and re-ranks without re-warming once data returns. And the gas win is the point rather than a side effect: a cold `SSTORE` is avoided for every skipped pool, which at the row's 890-pool figure is the same order as the budget pagination is fighting for. **E.7b THEREFORE SHIPS THREE OF ITS FOUR QUEUED LIMBS** — pagination per (iv), this relocation, and the gas-regression test over a several-hundred-pool roster, whose fixture is largely built since `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol` already stands up 30- and 90-pool rosters and whose job is to pin the per-page bound so a later roster growth cannot silently re-cross the block limit — while the bounded-work tier-boundary selection stays DECLINED per (iv). One item is CHECKED AT THE EDIT BEAT rather than assumed here: four test helpers hardcode the three-skips-then-rank epoch arithmetic, `_advanceWarmup` looping literally three times, and their pools appear to carry efficiency inputs before the first advance and so to have never been on a skip path, which would leave them unaffected; that is a prediction until the fixtures are read, not a finding. **(ix) AMENDED at PP4.13, and the amendment exists because (viii)'s own closing PREDICTION was too NARROW in scope rather than wrong in substance.** Measured at the edit beat: `_advanceWarmup` has TWELVE callers and not the four that clause guessed, and all twelve DO seat efficiency inputs before their first advance, so for that helper's callers the prediction HELD. What it could not see is what it never looked at, three fixtures reimplementing the same three-advances-then-measure arithmetic WITHOUT the helper, all three on a skip path. `testColdStartGraceWithZeroDenominatorDoesNotRevert` went outright RED and was repaired at PP4.13i; `testWarmupWindowSkipsWithoutEventOrRevert` and `testZeroDenominatorSkippedPostWarmup` stayed GREEN while measuring gates their pools no longer reached, repaired at PP4.13j and PP4.13k, and that vacuous-but-green pair is the dangerous class, since a passing test is invisible where a failing one is not. PP12 is the shape: the tool ran and was correct, over the wrong universe. **THE FOURTH IS THE SUBSTANTIVE ONE AND IS NOT A UNIT FIXTURE.** `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol::test_P1_E7b_tournamentGasScalesWithTheUnboundedActiveSet` FAILED at the PP4.13s Part-A baseline, extrapolating 3,916,000 against its 30,000,000 bar, because that fixture leaves `feeRecorder` unset per the E-family harness and every one of its 30 and 90 pools therefore skips at the numerator gate: TWO of the THREE cost terms E.7b's own NatSpec names, the cold `SSTORE` on first sighting and the insertion sort over ranked survivors, never execute, so the run measured the SKIP PATH rather than the tournament. **E.7b IS NOT CLOSED BY E.7a AND MUST NOT BE READ AS CLOSED**: the cost returns in full the moment numerators are nonzero, which is when the feed wires at rung 15. What (viii) does deliver is PERMANENT and is one of those three terms, the cold `SSTORE` no longer paid by any dead gauge, which is the gas win (viii) claimed and which now has a measured magnitude. **THE ROW'S FIGURES ARE SUPERSEDED** and must be RE-MEASURED on the repaired fixture rather than restated: 1,152,749 gas at 30 pools, 3,447,068 at 90, a marginal 38,238 and an extrapolated 34,031,820, carried in `CLAUDE.md` §11 and in the queue's E.7b reproduction cell. TWO adjudications, both user-decided at PP4.13s. FIRST, the reproduction regains its signal by wiring a REAL `feeRecorder` and pushing `recordFees` per pool against the REAL `EfficiencyOracle`, NOT by swapping in `MockEfficiencyOracle`: E.7b alleges a per-pool EXTERNAL CALL as one of its three terms and a mock would delete that term from the marginal, while the `never call setFeeRecorder` line at `P1_E7b.t.sol:65` is E.7a's invariant inherited through the shared harness and binds E.7a's file rather than this one. SECOND, pagination STAYS ON RUNG 13 even though its trigger arrives at 15: the bound is independent of the feed, shipping it before the trigger exists is the safe order, and rung 15 already carries PP-D12's hook batch plus E.7a's producer, wiring and vintage under PP-D34 as amended by (ii). **(x) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated: FINALIZE IS PAGINATED TOO, AND `advanceTournament` IS REMOVED RATHER THAN KEPT AS A WRAPPER.** (iv) bounded ACCUMULATION and left `finalizeTournament()` a single call over a scratch holding every ranked pool, which does not discharge the finding: at the row's 890 the scratch read ALONE is four cold slots per entry, on the order of 7.5M gas before one cap is written, and (iv) declines to change the sort, so the unbounded walk would reappear one entry over. THE RESOLUTION IS TWO CURSORS AND A SORTED SCRATCH. `finalizeTournament(uint256 maxPools)` takes its own `finalizeCursor`; accumulation INSERTS each ranked pool at its SORTED POSITION per G-D23 (iv) and T-T3 rather than appending, so the order is complete the moment accumulation is and finalize needs no global pass. Finalize's first page requires `tournamentCursor == length` exactly as (iv) states, later pages require `finalizeCursor < nRanked`, and the LAST page, the one on which `finalizeCursor` reaches `nRanked`, is what stamps `lastTournamentEpoch` and clears the scratch, both cursors and both `accumulationEpoch` slots. Incomplete work stays unfinalizable and un-restartable BY THOSE CURSORS and still by no boolean. Cap tiers are unaffected by the split: `favoredCount` and the three `cap*Count` all derive from `nRanked`, which is final the moment accumulation ends, so every finalize page recomputes the same counts from stored state and the percentile bands are identical to the single-call form. Transition events still fire exactly once per pool per epoch per G-D5, on the page that decides that pool's cohort. THE COST MOVES RATHER THAN VANISHING, and the gas-regression test must say so: the insertion shift is O(nRanked) worst case and is now paid inside a page, so a page costs `maxPools` times oracle-read-plus-shift, `maxPools` is the caller's lever, and the test must pin a per-page bound under a DESCENDING-ratio arrival order, which is insertion sort's worst case, rather than only the ascending best case the fixture seeds today. `advanceTournament` is REMOVED and not retained as a one-call convenience: a wrapper that accumulates everything and then finalizes IS the unbounded path this row is about, and a caller would reach for it. The ripple is MEASURED at HEAD rather than predicted, four files and 23 references: six in `src/gauge/GaugeRegistry.sol` counting the declaration and its NatSpec, nine in `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol`, five in `test/whitehat/P1_E7a.t.sol` and three in `test/whitehat/F16_emissionCapTournament.t.sol`. There is NO fork host and NO script among them, and `IGaugeRegistry` does not declare it, so removing a public entry touches no interface, which is the property §11 already records for this function and for `revokeGaugeIfIneligible`. **(xi) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated, and the FIRST half is a correctness gap neither (iv) nor (x) covers.** Pagination lets one accumulation span an epoch boundary. `_seatAccumulationEpoch` gates only an epoch's FIRST page and early-returns on every later one, while `EfficiencyOracle.efficiencyInputs` derives its three-epoch lookback from `block.number` at the moment of the call, so pages taken either side of a boundary read DIFFERENT WINDOWS and pools are ranked in one tournament against inconsistent data. The registry's `accumulationEpoch` pins the registry's own notion of the epoch and says nothing about the oracle's. The single-call form could not have this defect, so pagination INTRODUCES it and it must be closed in the same rung. RESOLUTION: every page re-derives the current epoch and reverts a new `AccumulationEpochStale` unless it still equals `accumulationEpoch`. An accumulation that does not finish inside its own epoch is therefore ABANDONED rather than completed across two, and reseats fresh at the next epoch; the tournament either reads one consistent window or does not run, which is exactly the outcome the unbounded form had whenever it could not afford its own call, so this trades no liveness the row did not already lack. The snapshot alternative, having later pages ask the oracle for a NAMED epoch, is DECLINED here because `efficiencyInputs` takes no epoch argument and adding one pushes work into `EfficiencyOracle`, which this rung otherwise leaves untouched. SECOND, THE SPLIT'S GAS COST IS MEASURED RATHER THAN PREDICTED AND IT IS WORSE THAN (x) IMPLIED. At PP4.13ae3 the two-phase path measured 3,977,883 gas over 30 pools and 11,670,092 over 90 for a marginal 128,203, against the single call's 38,123, extrapolating to 114,100,670 at the row's 890 rather than 33,929,470: a factor of 3.36 on the per-pool cost. The cause is structural rather than incidental, `RankedEntry` being FOUR storage slots that accumulation writes and finalize reads back, which is what spanning transactions costs. RESOLUTION: `numeratorSma` and `denominatorSma` are packed as a `uint128` pair sharing one slot, taking the entry from four slots to three for roughly a quarter off the added cost, with no semantic change and no re-read. The bound is stated rather than assumed: both legs are svZCHF `scaled18`, and `uint128` holds about 3.4e38 wei, which is 3.4e20 whole tokens. Dropping the two SMAs entirely and re-reading the oracle for crossing pools only would have halved the cost instead, and is DECLINED because the re-read can return a different window than the one that decided the ranking, so an event's payload could disagree with the ordering that produced it, which is the FIRST half of this clause in another costume. THE DoS ITSELF IS CLOSED REGARDLESS, since both phases page and no single call need exceed a block; total epoch cost was never what the row alleged. ONE OBLIGATION STAYS OPEN and must not be read as met: `test_P1_E7b_tournamentGasScalesWithTheUnboundedActiveSet` still carries the REPRODUCTION assertion, that the extrapolation exceeds a block, which the split makes MORE true rather than less, so it is no evidence of a fix. Per (x) the regression owes a PER-PAGE bound, and that is the queued done-criteria `test_advanceTournamentPaginates`. **(xii) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated: `computeEpochSnapshot` IS DELETED TOO, and its test ripple is the rung's missing coverage rather than a cost on top of it.** (x) removed `advanceTournament` and said nothing about the function it called. That function is now UNREACHABLE in production, its only caller gone and its gate `onlyGaugeRegistry`, but leaving it would keep an unbounded whole-array walk in a deployed contract for a future registry to reach for, which is (x)'s own argument one layer down. It is therefore deleted. THE DECIDING ARGUMENT IS COVERAGE, NOT DEAD CODE: `GaugeEligibilitySnapshotTest`'s twenty-one cases drive `computeEpochSnapshot` directly while the paged entries have ZERO unit coverage, so repointing those calls moves existing assertions onto the live path instead of leaving the suite aimed at a function nothing can invoke. The ripple is MEASURED at HEAD, 55 references across nine files. Three touch INTERFACES and all three are `@dev` prose rather than declarations, in `IGaugeEligibility`, `IEfficiencyOracle` and `IGaugeRegistry`, so the deletion touches no interface, which is the property §11 already records for this function. The unit file holds 33 real call sites in exactly THREE shapes, which is what makes this tractable as replace-alls rather than 33 edits: 26 of `(pools)`, 5 of `(empty)`, and 2 of `(new address[](0))`. THE LAST TWO ARE NOT LIKE THE OTHER 31 and must not be swept with them: they are the `OnlyGaugeRegistry` caller-restriction tests, which sit behind `vm.expectRevert`, so they take a single renamed `accumulateEpochSnapshot` call and NO finalize, since a second call would fall outside the armed cheatcode. The other 31 each become accumulate, a fresh `vm.prank(gaugeRegistry)`, then finalize, which leaves every existing `vm.prank` line above them untouched because the first call consumes it. Each is a COMPLETE accumulation, so a per-call epoch counter is correct: finalize clears `accumulationEpoch` and the next call seats a new one. `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol` carries 6 more and moves the same way. Deleting the function also clears the `nRanked` shadow warning that has ridden since PP4.13aa3, the local and the state variable ceasing to coexist. **(xiii) AMENDED at PP4.13, and the amendment exists because (xii) classified the unit file's call sites by their LEXICAL form rather than by what each call sits behind.** (xii) states that 31 of the 33 sites become accumulate, a fresh `vm.prank(gaugeRegistry)`, then finalize, and that `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol` moves the same way; both are false, measured at `586fe03`. `vm.expectEmit` arms against the NEXT call, and under the split the transition events fire in finalize while accumulate emits nothing, so a site behind an armed `vm.expectEmit` swept mechanically binds its expectation to accumulate and fails. FOUR unit sites sit behind one, the transition-event tests at `:746`, `:773`, `:793` and `:989`, and so does ONE fork site, `StageGIntegration.t.sol:694`. Each takes accumulate HOISTED to immediately above its first `vm.expectEmit` under a NEW `vm.prank(gaugeRegistry)` of its own, while the existing prank stays with finalize below the armed block. The anchor is the first `vm.expectEmit` rather than the top of the test because at `:773` and `:694` the oracle inputs change on the lines just above it, and accumulate is the call that reads them. **A FIFTH SITE DEFEATS LEXICAL CLASSIFICATION OUTRIGHT.** `:575` has the `(pools)` form and so was counted among the 31, but it is the body of `_SnapshotAttacker.attack`, a plain contract with neither `vm` nor `gaugeRegistry` in scope, driven behind `vm.expectRevert` at `:626` by the THIRD `OnlyGaugeRegistry` test, which (xii) did not count. It is rename-only exactly like `:614` and `:620`, so that set is THREE and not two; all three take `accumulateEpochSnapshot` with a second, epoch, argument that is never read because `onlyGaugeRegistry` reverts first, and none takes a finalize. In the fork file `:437` sits inside `_warmupTournament`'s `vm.startPrank(address(gaugeRegistry))` loop, so its pair takes NO fresh prank, the active one already covering both calls; that file's pranks name `address(gaugeRegistry)`, and it needs an epoch counter of its own, `_snapshotEpoch` living in `GaugeEligibilitySnapshotTest`. THE CORRECTED SHAPE: unit, 21 mechanical of `(pools)`, 5 mechanical of `(empty)`, 4 armed and 3 rename-only, for 33; fork, 4 mechanical, 1 armed and 1 under `vm.startPrank`, for 6. **SEQUENCING FOLLOWS FROM IT: the armed and rename-only sites are edited FIRST and the mechanical replace-all runs LAST**, because `:575` and the four armed sites carry the same call text as the 21, all 26 answering one grep, so a replace-all taken first would sweep all five. The rest of (xii) stands, including the per-call epoch counter for each complete accumulation. PP12 is the shape: a proximity check ran correctly over the lines near each call and could not see a cheatcode armed in a different function. **(xiv) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated: THE SPLIT AS BUILT COULD BE BRICKED PERMANENTLY, TWO WAYS, AND THE RESEAT (xi) STATED WAS NEVER BUILT.** Found at `29c0cd1` while reading `GaugeRegistry` for the re-home the prose sweep owed, before any broadcast, so it is a defect in this rung's own fix rather than a ledger finding. FIRST, an accumulation still incomplete when its epoch ends: `_seatAccumulationEpoch` reverts `AccumulationEpochStale` on every call once the epoch has moved, the only write clearing `accumulationEpoch` is finalize's done branch, and finalize requires the cursor to have reached the active-set length, so accumulate reverts Stale and finalize reverts Incomplete forever. One `accumulateTournament(1)` in the last blocks of an epoch whose tournament has not run triggers it for the price of a transaction, and an honest keeper paging a large roster across the boundary triggers it by accident; the harm is E.7b's own, caps and cohorts frozen, made permanent. (xi) stated the intended outcome, that an unfinished accumulation reseats fresh at the next epoch, and the code built only its revert half. `GaugeEligibility.accumulateEpochSnapshot` blocks independently, reverting `AccumulationEpochMismatch` on any new epoch while an old one is seated. SECOND, the active set changing under an open accumulation: `_activeGauges.remove` is swap-and-pop and runs unguarded in `revokeGauge` and in the permissionless `revokeGaugeIfIneligible`. A removal after the last accumulate page leaves the cursor past the length, so finalize reverts Incomplete and accumulate underflows `to - from` in `_buildPage`, stuck for the epoch and Stale forever after; a removal below the cursor swaps the unvisited last member into a visited slot, unranked for that epoch. Neither error had a single test anywhere in the tree. **RESOLUTION, RESEAT ON STALE: an accumulate call finding a seat from an EARLIER epoch whose accumulation is INCOMPLETE re-applies the two first-page gates and reseats at cursor zero rather than reverting**, and the elapsed gate passes because an abandoned accumulation never stamped `lastTournamentEpoch`. `GaugeEligibility` reseats whenever handed an epoch other than its seated one, zeroing `nRanked` and `finalizeCursor` and so discarding the abandoned scratch, and `AccumulationEpochMismatch` is deleted as unreachable, the registry being its only caller and never passing a different epoch except on a reseat. The no-mixed-window property of (xi) survives intact, since a reseat discards every earlier page, and the cold-start stamps an abandoned accumulation wrote are not orphaned, carrying `currentSnapshotEpoch + 1`, which the reseated accumulation uses too. **ONE CASE STAYS A REVERT, added here beyond the question as put:** a seat from an earlier epoch whose accumulation is COMPLETE reverts `AccumulationEpochStale`, narrowed to that meaning, because the right move is to finalize it, which is permissionless, paged and reads no oracle, so the revert is never a stall; reseating instead would discard a finalize that may already have written caps and fired crossings for some pools, and a second run could fire a second crossing for the same snapshot epoch against G-D5. **RESOLUTION, TOLERATE SET CHANGES: finalize accepts `tournamentCursor >= length` rather than equality, and accumulate clamps a cursor past the end to an empty page.** The costs are bounded and accepted: a pool swapped behind the cursor is skipped for that one epoch and keeps its prior cap per P-D15 (4), and a pool revoked after being ranked is still finalized for that epoch, its cap inert once revoked and its presence shifting a tier boundary by at most one place. Freezing the set was declined because it couples governance and the anti-spam activation path to keeper timing while anyone can open an accumulation. THE FIX OWES regression cases for all four paths, a stale incomplete accumulation reseating and completing, a stale complete one reverting and finalizing late, a removal after completion finalizing, and a removal mid-accumulation neither reverting nor ranking the swapped pool, plus the eligibility-side reseat; the prose sweep stays paused until they land, since the re-home would otherwise document the semantics this clause replaces. **(xv) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated: BOTH PAGED ENTRIES SATURATE THEIR PAGE BOUND INSTEAD OF OVERFLOWING IT.** `accumulateTournament` computes `from + maxPools` and `finalizeEpochSnapshot` computes `i + maxPools` before either clamps to its length, so `type(uint256).max`, the natural way to ask for every remaining entry, overflows with panic `0x11` on any page but the first. Recorded at al3c as pre-existing, loud and stateless and left out of scope; demonstrated at al4g, when the mid-accumulation regression case, written to that idiom on its second page, reverted before reaching a single assertion. It bricks nothing, a smaller page size succeeding, but a keeper writes that idiom first, and a keeper whose every continuation page reverts never completes an accumulation that needs more than one page. RESOLUTION: each bound is taken against the remaining span, `len - from` on the registry, which the al3c clamp keeps non-negative, and `nRanked - i` on the eligibility side, where the finalize cursor never passes `nRanked` because it is clamped every page and zeroed together with it, and a gauge activated mid-finalize is cold-start on first sighting and so is stamped rather than inserted behind the cursor. The bound becomes the length whenever `maxPools` reaches past it, so every page size is legal and `maxPools` keeps its meaning as the caller's gas lever. No state, event or error changes. The al4g case is kept exactly as written, being now the registry half's regression, and the eligibility half owes one more case, a finalize page taken at `type(uint256).max` after a first page of one, added beside the eligibility-side reseat case (xiv) already owes. **(xvi) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated: (vi)'s BINDING TYPE DOES NOT COMPILE, AND BECOMES AN ADDRESS IMMUTABLE WITH A CONCRETE CAST.** (vi) locks `IEmissionDistributor public immutable DISTRIBUTOR` and a `buyBoost` guard reading `DISTRIBUTOR.incendiaryRegistry() == address(this)`, but `IEmissionDistributor` declares no such getter, its only mentions of that slot being the H-D29 and PP-D44 events and their NatSpec, so the locked shape is not expressible. Measured at `27b51f4`, the interface's implementor radius is exactly TWO, `EmissionDistributor` and `MockRecorder` in `test/unit/VotingWeight.t.sol`, so extending the interface would touch a shared type and a test mock and would additionally need a G10 probe on whether a public state variable satisfying a newly declared interface function requires an `override` specifier at the pinned solc. RESOLUTION: `IncendiaryRegistry` stores `address public immutable DISTRIBUTOR` behind its existing `ZeroAddress` guard, and the guard reads `EmissionDistributor(DISTRIBUTOR).incendiaryRegistry() == address(this)`. That is **G-D16d**'s address-immutable-with-casts-at-call-sites rule, and it is this rung's own precedent one contract over: `GaugeRegistry` holds `gaugeEligibility` as an address immutable and casts the CONCRETE `GaugeEligibility` for exactly the reason that the paged entries are absent from `IGaugeEligibility` per (iv) and (x). The cost is one concrete import into `IncendiaryRegistry`, compile coupling carrying no runtime code since the read stays an external call. Everything else in (vi) stands. The arity still moves from 8 to 9, and the ripple is the SIX files re-measured at `27b51f4`: the declaration, the sole `is IncendiaryRegistry` inheritor `test/unit/harness/IncendiaryRegistryHarness.sol`, its two construction sites in `test/unit/IncendiaryRegistry.t.sol` and `test/whitehat/F07_emaStalePricing.t.sol`, and the two real ones in `test/fork/StageLIntegration.t.sol` and `script/DeployStageL.s.sol`, whose NatSpec mention at `:17` carries no arity. The cursor limb still ships no code. **(xvii) AMENDED at PP4.13: THE E.8 GUARD'S TYPED DOMAIN, locked before any code because neither (vi) nor (xvi) names the revert.** THE ERROR is `error NotBoundToDistributor(address boundRegistry);`, declared in `IncendiaryRegistry` beside its ten existing errors, none of which live on the interface. Its parameter is the distributor's live `incendiaryRegistry` at the call, `address(0)` after an unbind and another registry after a rebind, so the revert itself says which. The name is chosen distinct from `IncendiaryRegistryNotBound()` in `script/DeployStageP.s.sol`, a separate scope that would compile but would put two meanings behind one name in any trace. THE PLACEMENT is last among `buyBoost`'s four preconditions, directly after `ZeroAmount` and before `_valueInAuMM`, so the preconditions stay one block and the one external call runs after the cheaper checks. Measured at `890fe49`, that placement bounds the blast radius exactly: of the unit file's six `buyBoost` tests the four revert cases never reach the guard, while `test_buyBoost_revert_emaStale` and `test_buyBoost_happyPath_svzchf` do, and both fork callers, `StageLIntegration` and `DeployStageL.t.sol`, buy only after their fixtures accept the binding. THE UNIT STAND-IN is a one-function getter on `IncendiaryRegistryTest` returning `address(registry)`, which answers the concrete cast for the `address(this)` that fixture passes since ao1; the file carries no `incendiaryRegistry` symbol to collide with, and F07 never calls `buyBoost` and needs nothing. The cast brings one import, `EmissionDistributor`, which imports only `IIncendiaryRegistry` and so creates no cycle. THE ORDER is reproduction first, because the registry-side face (vi) owes has never been reproduced and a done-criteria case written after the guard would be born green. A PoC named `test_P1_E8_*` lands against the current code in `test/whitehat/P1_E8.t.sol`, asserting that a purchase still succeeds after the distributor's two-step unbind to `address(0)`. The guard then lands as one unit with that PoC inverted to expect `NotBoundToDistributor(address(0))` and with the done-criteria case `test_buyBoostRequiresLiveBinding` added, the F.1 precedent of an inverted PoC beside a separate done-criteria case. **(xviii) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated: THE RECORD-TIME CLAMP IS DELETED RATHER THAN KEPT AS A FIRST PASS, AND THE SETTLE-HEAD CLAMP EMITS WHEN IT BINDS.** (v) fixes WHERE the clamp runs and WHAT ARITHMETIC it uses, and leaves two typed-domain choices open; both are settled here before any code. FIRST, the clamp is DELETED rather than retained ahead of the new one, and its extent is `EmissionDistributor.sol:573-585`, which CORRECTS the `:576-584` that (v) records: that range opens the month-13 gate at 576 and stops at 584, leaving the outer brace at 585 unclosed, and it omits the three `F16f / P-D13 (4)` comment lines at 573-575 that describe the very mechanism being removed. The `:490-501` carried by the E.7c queue row and the F-64 ledger row is the `9ec513d` audit pin and is legitimately stale rather than a third claim about HEAD. Keeping the block would leave a SECOND latch against a caller-chosen `totalScore - oldEffective`, which IS E.7c's defect surface, and (v)'s own reasoning that the next settle re-clamps first holds ONLY while `poolScore` stores the UNCAPPED reshape. Three consequences are recorded here rather than discovered at code time: `ScoreUpdated` reports the uncapped effective score, so after this edit no event carries the cap unless one is added, which is the second half of this clause; the denominator becomes the LIVE `totalScore - poolScore[pool]` read at settle rather than the record-time latch; and THE UNIT BLAST RADIUS IS ONE CASE, not the four the family name suggests. `test_RecordScore_EmissionCapClamp_ReducesEffectiveWhenOverCap` at `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol:671` is the only case asserting BOTH a clamped `poolScore` AND `ScoreUpdated(..., expectedCapLimit)`, so it alone goes red; its three siblings at `:698`, `:711` and `:725` assert that the clamp does NOT fire, stay GREEN after the deletion, and are therefore VACUOUS, measuring a mechanism that has left `recordScore`. All three are RETARGETED at the settle-head clamp rather than deleted, the gate, zero-cap and under-cap paths all surviving the move intact, because a passing test that measures nothing is exactly the class (ix) already names as the dangerous one. SECOND, the settle-head clamp EMITS `EmissionCapApplied(address indexed pool, uint256 uncappedScore, uint256 cappedScore)`, fired from the clamp at the head of `_settlePool` ONLY when it reduces the score, so an idempotent second settle in the same interval emits nothing and the double-settle paths `_syncDown` reaches stay silent. THE DECLARATION SITE IS THE INTERFACE, not the concrete contract: `EmissionDistributor` declares NO event of its own, all eleven living on `IEmissionDistributor` between `:30` and `:101`, and an added interface event imposes nothing on either inheritor because an event needs no implementation, so G16's sweep is nil here and `MockRecorder` at `test/unit/VotingWeight.t.sol:101` is untouched. The done-criteria case then names it `IEmissionDistributor.EmissionCapApplied` exactly as the existing clamp test names `IEmissionDistributor.ScoreUpdated` at `:687`. That is the OPPOSITE disposition from (xvii)'s concrete-only error and from the concrete-only `poolBoostCursor` slot, and the difference is the house pattern for each kind rather than an inconsistency: this contract keeps every event on the interface and has never kept one off it. The queue's E.7c fix intent already reads "emit when it binds" while (v) is silent on it, which is why it is settled here rather than left to the build; declining it would need a stated reason on the F-64 ledger row, and adopting it needs no queue edit. Nothing else in (v) moves: the month-13 gate and the `capBps != 0` read, the formula, the insertion BEFORE `poolAllocation`, the six direct settle sites plus `_syncDown`'s reach, the wasted-and-fine clamp on `deregisterScore`, and the done-criteria `test_capEnforcedAtSettle` with both PoCs inverted are all as (v) left them. **(xix) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated, and it WITHDRAWS (xviii)'s deletion half rather than refining it.** The gatekeeper's pre-flight of the settle-head insert found what (xviii) did not. With the record-time clamp gone, the UNCAPPED reshape enters `totalScore` at `recordScore` and stays there until that pool's next `_settlePool`, while `_accrueGlobal` divides every contribution by that live aggregate. Over such a window the numerators sum to `T_others + C` against a denominator of `T_others + U`, so `(U - C)` times the interval's accumulator increment is credited to NO pool: the capped excess is FORFEITED rather than redistributed. That is the exact outcome (v) refused when it rejected the allocation clip, "H-D24 stranded allocation and canon-hostile under PP-D43", and it is what the contract's own `F16f` comment and the spec forbid, both requiring the excess to reach uncapped pools pro rata. THE RESOLUTION IS TO CAP WHAT ENTERS `totalScore` AND TO KEEP THE SETTLE RE-CLAMP BESIDE IT. The block at `EmissionDistributor.sol:573-585` STAYS, so no uncapped wei ever reaches the aggregate, and (v)'s clamp lands at the head of `_settlePool` as locked, re-deriving the limit against the LIVE denominator on every settle. BOTH E.7c FACES STILL CLOSE, which is why this narrows the fix rather than withdrawing it: the drift face, where `totalScore` falls after the latch and the realised share climbs above the cap, is corrected at that pool's next settle; and the two-identical-pools face, where the caller chose the instant each latched, is corrected because every settle re-derives instead of trusting the stored value. SIX THINGS (xviii) RECORDED ARE WITHDRAWN, each falsified by keeping the block rather than by any new measurement. THREE ARE ITS STATED CONSEQUENCES. `ScoreUpdated` continues to report the CAPPED effective score, not the uncapped one. The denominator does NOT become the live `totalScore - poolScore[pool]` IN PLACE OF the record-time latch: the retained block still solves against `totalScore - oldEffective` at `:580`, so there are now TWO live denominators, each governing its own clamp, which is the two-clamps sentence below stated from the other side. And the unit blast radius is NOT one red case, since `test_RecordScore_EmissionCapClamp_ReducesEffectiveWhenOverCap` at `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol:671` and its three siblings at `:698`, `:711` and `:725` all stay GREEN, so this rung's unit work is the NEW settle-side cases rather than the repair or retargeting of the old ones. TWO ARE ITS GROUNDS for deleting, which would otherwise stand as live objections to the shape this clause locks: that keeping the block "would leave a SECOND latch against a caller-chosen `totalScore - oldEffective`", and that (v)'s re-clamp reasoning "holds ONLY while `poolScore` stores the UNCAPPED reshape". Both fall to the same one-line ground: a stored value that EVERY settle re-derives against the live denominator is no longer a latch, whatever it was at the instant it was written, so the record-time write becomes an entry condition on the aggregate rather than a claim about the pool's share. THE SIXTH is not a consequence (xviii) stated but a sub-step this rung had planned: as3b, the deletion, is CANCELLED and must not be run. WHAT (xviii) RECORDS THAT STANDS: the extent correction, `:573-585` being the balanced block against the `:576-584` (v) cites, now a citation fix rather than a deletion target; the event `EmissionCapApplied` with its three-parameter shape; its declaration on `IEmissionDistributor`, landed at `:108`; and the emit condition, only when the clamp reduces the score. THE TWO CLAMPS ARE NOT A REDUNDANCY and must not be collapsed later by a reader who finds two cap sites in one contract: the record-time one keeps `totalScore` free of uncapped wei, a property of the AGGREGATE, and the settle-time one keeps a stored score honest against a limit that has since moved, a property of the POOL; deleting either reopens a different face of E.7c. **THE SETTLE CLAMP STRANDS A BOUNDED AMOUNT, ACCEPTED HERE ON A CORRECTED FIGURE, and (xviii)'s "redistribution is preserved exactly as it is today" is WITHDRAWN with it.** The clamp reduces a stored score BEFORE the elapsed interval is allocated, per (v)'s head placement, so that interval accrued against a denominator carrying `C` while the pool realises `C'`, and `(C - C')` times that interval's accumulator increment reaches no pool. THE BOUND IS ONE SETTLE INTERVAL TIMES THE FULL CAP OVERHANG, NOT A DRIFT, and the first draft of this clause said otherwise and was falsified at pre-flight: `finalizeTournament` writes `poolEmissionCapBps` through `GaugeEligibility._finalizeOne` with no score change anywhere, so a pool UNCAPPED at its last record can be assigned a cap at a tournament boundary and the next settle then strands its whole overhang with `S_others` never having moved. The zero-condition is therefore that the LIMIT has not moved, covering `capBps` and `S_others` alike, and not that the denominator has not moved; the maximum case is the uncapped-to-capped transition at a finalize, once per newly capped pool per tournament, a cap TIGHTENING being strictly smaller. The interval itself is unbounded in duration, so a dormant pool's residual scales with its dormancy, and every instance still self-closes at that pool's next settle. The two alternatives were put to the user and declined: allocating the elapsed interval first and clamping prospectively amends (v)'s placement and pays a newly capped pool at its pre-cap rate for one interval, which is what the cap exists to prevent; and a remainder path is the mechanism (v) explicitly declined to invent. `EmissionCapApplied` is the on-chain signal of every instance, which is what makes the residual observable rather than silent, and RB-031 files the class. **PP-D43 DISPOSITION, user-adjudicated and owed because (v) rejected the allocation clip on exactly this ground: THIS RESIDUAL IS NOT A CODE-VERSUS-CANON DIVERGENCE.** PP-D43 protects §xxix of `10_constitution.md` wholesale plus five named invariants, and the redistribution rule is in neither set, living at `08_bootstrap.md:125` with zero occurrences of the rule anywhere in the constitution. What the rule governs is WHERE a capped pool's excess goes, and it still goes to uncapped pools pro rata: the aggregate never carries uncapped wei, because the record-time cap is retained precisely for that. What this clause accepts is narrower and of a different kind, a one-interval settlement lag at the moment a limit moves, bounded, signalled by an event and self-closing at the next settle. That is not the allocation clip's defect, which burned the excess on EVERY settle of a capped pool with no bound and no signal. **THE CITATION IS CORRECTED BY canon, which returned DIVERGE on the draft of this clause.** The excess-redistribution rule is `08_bootstrap.md:125`, "**Excess emissions are redistributed.** When a pool is capped below its CCB-derived share, the excess goes to uncapped pools pro-rata by CCB share", under the § Emission Efficiency Tournament heading at `:110`; `10_constitution.md` line 125 is BLANK, and that file states the rule nowhere. The repo's bare "spec L125" convention therefore resolves to the bootstrap doc, which its sibling "spec L129" at `GaugeEligibility.sol:83` independently confirms against `08_bootstrap.md:129`. The distribution key is NOT divergent though it reads so: the spec says pro rata by CCB share and the contract says by score, and those are the same proportion, because F-6 at `11_formulas.md:140` defines CCB share as score over the sum of scores and the clamp is gated past Year 1 where F-3's α is 1, leaving the effective score the pure F-5 score. Nothing in this rung restates "F-16" as a spec formula: the canonical set is F-0 through F-12, this mechanism is **F-10**, and `F16f` is the in-repo Stage-F marker at `EmissionDistributor.sol:573`. **THREE OBLIGATIONS THIS CLAUSE LEAVES NAMED rather than open.** FIRST, the `EmissionCapApplied` NatSpec landed at `IEmissionDistributor.sol:104` still carries (xviii)'s withdrawn story, asserting that the record-time clamp is deleted and that `ScoreUpdated` will carry the uncapped reshape; both halves are now false and the correction is owed by its own sub-step BEFORE the clamp lands, since the interface would otherwise document semantics the contract does not have. SECOND, RB-031 owes its register row, the class being a settlement lag at a limit change rather than the stranding (v) refused. THIRD, canon surfaced a SPEC-INTERNAL conflict while checking this clause and it is PP5.4's rather than this rung's: `08_bootstrap.md:114-121` caps the BOTTOM 15% by efficiency, while F-10 at `11_formulas.md:224` and `:251` caps the TOP 15% and calls the bottom-privileging reading explicitly wrong, and the two carry different redistribution shapes, a flat pro-rata against a two-stage favored-then-residual waterfall. The repo implements the bootstrap form, which `GaugeEligibility.sol:83` shows in its cap ladder. Recording it inside this decision alone would lose it, PP5.4 iterating the thirteen G41 rows rather than PP-D56, so it takes a marker on the `H.1` queue row and on the plan's PP-D23 row, on the same marker-rather-than-rewrite precedent (i) set for the order-13 line. No code in this rung depends on which reading wins, and nothing here adjudicates it. **(xx) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated, and it settles the one thing (v), (xviii) and (xix) all left open: THE SETTLE-HEAD CLAMP'S COMPLETE EMIT SET IS `EmissionCapApplied` ALONE.** The gatekeeper raised it at the pre-flight of the interface NatSpec correction, before any file moved: that correction's closing clause asserts the new event is the only signal that the clamp bound, and no clause of this decision had said so, while BOTH of the contract's existing `poolScore` writes pair the write with an emit, at `EmissionDistributor.sol:587-588` and at `:605-606`. An implementer following that house pattern would have emitted `ScoreUpdated` at settle and falsified the interface line on landing, which is the very failure (xix) names when it requires that line corrected before the clamp. FOUR MEASUREMENTS DECIDE IT, and none of them is a preference. FIRST, `_settlePool` at `:452` EMITS NOTHING TODAY: the eleven emit statements in the file sit at `:237` through `:301` on the setters, `:588` in `recordScore`, `:606` in `deregisterScore` and `:682`, `:718` and `:743` on the deposit, withdrawal and claim paths, and not one falls between `:452` and `:557`, a span WIDER than the function itself; so there is no settle-side emit pattern to follow and the house pattern cited above is a RECORD-TIME one. SECOND, `poolScore[pool]` has exactly TWO write sites, `:587` and `:605`, both outside `_settlePool`, so the clamp becomes the THIRD and the FIRST inside settle. THIRD, `ScoreUpdated`'s OWN `@param oldScore` at `IEmissionDistributor.sol:28` reads "The prior F-5 score before this `recordScore` invocation", so firing it from settle would falsify THAT line too and owe a SECOND interface correction in the same rung, which is this rung's own defect reproduced rather than avoided. FOURTH, `EmissionCapApplied(pool, uncappedScore, cappedScore)` already carries BOTH endpoints of the transition, exactly the pair `ScoreUpdated` would carry, so the second event would be strictly redundant rather than additive. THE CONSEQUENCE IS A BAR ON THE NEXT SUB-STEP rather than a note: the clamp adds `EmissionCapApplied` and NO other emit, `ScoreUpdated` stays a `recordScore`-only event, and its NatSpec block at `IEmissionDistributor.sol:24-29`, the six lines above its declaration at `:30`, is NOT edited by this rung. THE BAR PRESERVES NOTHING THE CLAMP FALSIFIES, which is why it is a bar rather than a deferral: `:24` states a SUFFICIENT condition, that the event fires when `recordScore` writes a score, and not a completeness claim over every `poolScore` write, while `:25-26` and `:29` describe the record-time path alone. THE ACCEPTED COST IS STATED HERE so a later reader does not read it as an oversight: an indexer tracking `poolScore` from `ScoreUpdated` alone goes stale at exactly the settles where the clamp binds, and must read `EmissionCapApplied` beside it, which is sufficient rather than merely available because that event carries both endpoints and fires on every instance. This clause settles the emit set ONLY. It moves nothing else in (v), (xviii) or (xix), and in particular the retained record-time clamp at `EmissionDistributor.sol:573-585` keeps its own `ScoreUpdated` at `:588` unchanged. EVERY `EmissionDistributor.sol` LINE NUMBER IN THIS CLAUSE IS AS MEASURED BEFORE THE CLAMP LANDS, which inserts at the head of `_settlePool` and shifts every line below it; they date the clause rather than track the file. **(xxi) AMENDED at PP4.13, and it CORRECTS (xix)'s FRAMING of the spec conflict rather than the conflict itself.** The gatekeeper's pre-flight of the two markers (xix) orders found IN-TREE RECORDS that (xix) did not consult, and they change both what PP5.4 is being asked to decide and where the divergence is carried. FIRST, `docs/STAGE_P_NOTES.md:135` records P-D13's "**Deferred — favored-cohort anti-concentration caps (follow-up P-D).**", stating that F-10's top-side caps "are a different axis from F-16 (a bottom-cohort attack)". THE FRAMING CORRECTION FOLLOWS FROM IT. (xix) described the two documents as capping OPPOSITE cohorts, which reads as an EITHER/OR that PP5.4 settles by choosing one document over the other. P-D13 treats them as TWO COEXISTING AXES: a bottom-cohort ladder, which is BUILT and is what `GaugeEligibility._capBpsFor` writes into `poolEmissionCapBps`, and a top-side anti-concentration cap, which is DEFERRED and has never been withdrawn. On that reading the repo is not implementing one of two rival rules; it has built one axis and still owes the other, and the redistribution-shape difference is the part that remains a genuine either/or. THE CONSEQUENCE IS WHY THIS IS RECORDED: a PP5.4 acting on (xix)'s framing alone could close the divergence by editing `11_formulas.md` to delete the within-favored caps, and would thereby resolve away a DEFERRED BUILD OBLIGATION that no decision has withdrawn. That is the RB-027 acceptance-premise class arriving inside this rung's own bookkeeping. P-D13 is the ONLY DECISION that defers the obligation, but it is not the only RECORD of it: `docs/white_hat/AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md:63`, the F-16 row, states in its own words "Deferred, not implemented: … the favored-cohort top-side anti-concentration caps", citing P-D13 rather than deferring independently, so a PP5.4 spec edit staleness-checks that row as well. SECOND, AND SHARPER, THE DIVERGENCE IS CARRIED IN-TREE AND NOT ONLY BETWEEN THE TWO SPEC DOCUMENTS. `docs/STAGE_G_NOTES.md:28` is G-D3 (iv), whose parenthetical attributes the ladder "15/10/5 → 1%/0.5%/0.1%" to **`11_formulas.md` F-10** — and F-10 SUPPLIES NO CAP NUMBERS AT ALL. Measured: `0.1%` occurs ZERO times in `11_formulas.md` against TWO in `08_bootstrap.md`; the string `15/10/5` occurs zero times in either spec document, being the repo's own shorthand; and F-10's single reference to cap magnitudes, at `11_formulas.md:243-244`, defers them, reading "numeric cap values set at contract deployment". The 1%/0.5%/0.1% ladder is the BOTTOM-band table at `08_bootstrap.md:116-121`, which sets 1% for the bottom 15-10%, 0.5% for 10-5% and 0.1% below the 5th percentile. NO TOTAL OF SITES IS STATED HERE and none should be inferred: this clause miscounted that total twice before it was measured, which is PP13 inside the bookkeeping, and what is offered instead is the measured CARRIER SET at HEAD. `15/10/5` occurs SIX times across `docs/`, and FOUR of those attach the ladder to F-10 — G-D3 (iv) at `docs/STAGE_G_NOTES.md:28`, the ONLY DECISION among them, plus three planning references that decide nothing: `docs/STAGE_G_PLAN.md:93`, `docs/STAGE_G_PLAN.md:280`, which says "from `11_formulas.md` F-10" outright, and `docs/STAGE_F_PLAN.md:90`, which compounds it by calling the bands "top-15/10/5-percentile" and so fuses F-10's cohort direction to the bootstrap document's numbers inside one phrase. The other two are NOT carriers and must not be swept up: `docs/STAGE_G_PRECHECK_AUTO_GAUGE.md:17` attributes the bands to `STAGES_OVERVIEW` rather than to F-10, and `docs/FINDINGS.md:131` gets the provenance RIGHT, citing "Bootstrap §xxiii line 1502". That last is the sharpest fact in this clause: the tree already records the correct source in one place and the wrong one in four, so PP5.4 inherits an edit surface rather than a question. WHAT G-D3 (iv) ACTUALLY LOCKS, stated precisely because (xix) and the first draft of this clause both overstated it: it locks COHORT DIRECTION and nothing else, its own closing sentence reading "Whether to retain, drop, or replace caps is **deferred** to Stage G **`G1.x`** sub-steps — only cohort direction is locked here". G-D15b at `docs/STAGE_G_NOTES.md:448` then deferred the caps AGAIN, to Stage H, still without deciding retain, drop or replace — and A FOURTH FACT FOR PP5.4 IS THAT IT LANDED AT G2.0 rather than in the `G1.x` sub-steps (iv) named, per its parent's own "Resolution at G2.0 entry" at `docs/STAGE_G_NOTES.md:444` and the Stage-G completion log at `docs/STAGE_G_PLAN.md:589`, so the deferral target (iv) set was never honoured as such and the caps have now been deferred twice across two stages. The direction (iv) locks is F-10's, caps being "**anti-concentration controls WITHIN** the favored cohort" that "cap top performers", while the ladder the protocol actually built caps the BOTTOM bands; so on cohort direction there is an unwithdrawn in-tree decision on one side and the shipped code on the other, and that is a fact for PP5.4 rather than a finding of this rung. WHAT THIS STAGE HAD AND HAD NOT RECORDED, measured across ALL THREE of its artifacts rather than one, and stated at the scope the instruments actually cover. The anti-concentration AXIS and G-D3 are named NOWHERE in the stage: `anti-concentration` and `G-D3` each occurred ZERO times in `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_NOTES.md`, `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_PLAN.md` and `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_QUEUE.md` alike, which is the whole stage set, every count taken immediately before this clause was appended and the NOTES readings necessarily raised by the clause itself. P-D13 IS DIFFERENT and the difference is stated rather than glossed, ACROSS ALL THREE ARTIFACTS so that a later grep meets no unreconciled figure. In the stage NOTES `P-D13` reads SIX, of which `PP-D13` accounts for FIVE as substring over-matches, and the ONE genuine hit sits on line 649 ITSELF, in an earlier clause of this same decision, as the source-comment token `F16f / P-D13 (4)` pointing at that decision's clause (4), the `recordScore` clamp. In the stage PLAN `P-D13` reads ELEVEN and `PP-D13` reads ELEVEN at the IDENTICAL lines 73, 137, 138, 139, 176, 177, 204, 208, 242, 277 and 360, so every hit there is an over-match and NONE is genuine. In the stage QUEUE both read FOUR at the identical lines 16, 78, 79 and 80, so again none is genuine. The DEFERRAL paragraph at `docs/STAGE_P_NOTES.md:135`, which is what this clause rests on, had therefore never been consulted anywhere in this stage, while the id itself had been named exactly once, for an unrelated purpose, inside this very decision. WHAT DOES NOT MOVE. Every measured fact (xix) records stands: `08_bootstrap.md:114-121` caps the bottom bands, `11_formulas.md:224` makes the top 15% the favored cohort, `:251` states caps constrain top performers and never privilege low-efficiency pools, the two documents carry different redistribution shapes, and the repo implements the bootstrap ladder. The disposition does not move either — the direction is PP5.4's, this rung adjudicates nothing, and no code in it turns on the outcome. AND THE ROW COUNT DOES NOT MOVE: this divergence is NOT one of PP-D23's thirteen G41 rows, verified against the audit's own enumeration under its `# G41` heading, whose thirteen subjects are the yield skim, the genesis fee, der Bodensee's fee band, the VaultClass params, the four-epoch auto-revocation, deprecated-pool emissions, the 28, the multisig authorities, `routeGovernanceDeposit`, `distribute()`, the `IVotingWeight` header, `GaugeActivationFailed.reason` and the anti-spam fee, none of which touches the tournament; so it is an ADDITION to PP5.4's work rather than a fourteenth row, and nothing is renumbered. THE TWO MARKERS THIS DECISION ORDERS MUST CARRY THIS CORRECTED FRAMING and must be worded identically on it — this clause BINDS them rather than describing them, since both are later sub-steps and their drafts predate it. Each must name P-D13's deferral and the in-tree carrier set, must state the kind NEUTRALLY rather than calling it spec-side, because PP-D23 recommends SPEC CHANGES for its rows 2-4 and 7 and borrowing that label would import a disposition this rung is barred from making, and must cite this clause rather than stopping at (xix). **(xxii) AMENDED at PP4.13, and it NARROWS (xxi) THREE TIMES on evidence (xxi)'s own instrument could not reach.** The gatekeeper's pre-flight of the two markers (xxi) binds found all three, and they are one failure in three costumes: a claim stated wider than the instrument that produced it. THIS CLAUSE THEREFORE STATES NO COUNT OF CARRIERS AND NO TOTAL, and the reason is its own first draft, which is worth recording because it is the same defect one level up. That draft carried three figures drawn from three different instruments under three different self-inclusion conventions, and one of them was not a measurement at all: `0.5%` appeared to reach `docs/STAGE_P_NOTES.md:157`, a line containing no such string, because the unescaped `.` wildcarded across the en dash in "bottom 10–5%". That is PB20's reach failure occurring inside the very clause written to correct a reach failure, and the remedy is to name KNOWN CARRIER LINES and nothing else. FIRST, THE CARRIER SET IS NOT ENUMERABLE BY ANY INSTRUMENT TRIED. The misattribution is spelled at least three ways — the slash form `15/10/5`, the percentage form and the basis-point form — and the three genuinely PARTITION rather than overlap, the literal `0.5%` reaching `docs/STAGE_P_NOTES.md:119` and `:127` while `50 bps` reaches `:157`, neither reaching the other's lines and `15/10/5` reaching none of them; `F-10` itself is named in 24 of the 56 markdown files under `docs/` against the slash form's six, so it OVER-MATCHES the carrier set by a wide margin rather than enumerating it. WHAT FOLLOWS IS AN OPEN SET OF KNOWN CARRIERS AND NOT A CLOSED ONE. Reached by `15/10/5` and already named in (xxi): `docs/STAGE_G_NOTES.md:28`, G-D3 (iv); `docs/STAGE_G_PLAN.md:93`; `docs/STAGE_G_PLAN.md:280`; `docs/STAGE_F_PLAN.md:90`. NOT reached by it: `docs/FINDINGS.md:1177`, which reads "F-10 … tiers gauge-eligible pools by efficiency rank with hard caps at 1% / 0.5% / 0.1% (15th / 10th / 5th percentiles)", and `docs/STAGE_P_NOTES.md:115`, `:119`, `:127` and `:157`. SO (xxi)'s READING OF `docs/FINDINGS.md` IS HALF RIGHT AND MUST NOT BE QUOTED ALONE: that file records the provenance CORRECTLY at `:131`, citing "Bootstrap §xxiii line 1502", AND carries the misattribution at `:1177`. One file does both, which is a sharper fact than either half. SECOND, AND SHARPER, P-D13 IS ITSELF A CARRIER, so (xxi)'s "G-D3 (iv), the ONLY DECISION among them" is true of its instrument's set and FALSE of the tree. Inside P-D13's own body, which runs from its heading at `docs/STAGE_P_NOTES.md:115` to P-D14's at `:139`: `:115` titles the decision "wire the F-10 efficiency-tournament emission caps"; `:119` has F-10 "hard-caps the least efficient" and carries the ladder in percentages; and `:127`, the tier-assignment clause, writes it in both spellings, "above 15th → 0 (uncapped); bottom 15–10% → 100 (1%); bottom 10–5% → 50 (0.5%); bottom 5% → 10 (0.1%)".
