So the very decision (xxi) rests on for the DEFERRAL, at `:135`, attributes the bottom-band reading to F-10 elsewhere in that same body, at each of the three lines just named. AND IT DOES NOT STOP AT ONE DECISION, which is the part no instrument in (xxi) could have shown: `:157` reads "P-D13 (3) locks the F-10 percentile cap table (above 15th → uncapped; bottom 15–10% → 100 bps; bottom 10–5% → 50 bps; bottom 5% → 10 bps)", in the basis-point spelling, and that line sits under **P-D15** at `:155`, whose own heading declares it "refines P-D13 (3)". The misattribution is therefore carried inside TWO decisions, one restating the other, and PP5.4 inherits the divergence as having reached the decision record itself rather than as a documentation tidy-up. THIRD, THE REDISTRIBUTION EITHER/OR HAS A CODE SIDE AND (xxi) LEFT IT UNSTATED. (xxi) calls the redistribution-shape difference "the part that remains a genuine either/or" without saying which side the protocol already implements. It implements the BOOTSTRAP side, and BOTH of its cap comments say so. The RETAINED RECORD-TIME clamp in `recordScore` carries a three-line comment whose middle sentence ends "to uncapped pools pro-rata by score (spec L125)". THE SETTLE-HEAD CLAMP THIS RUNG ADDS CARRIES THE CITATION IN FULL rather than in the repo's shorthand: its own comment block, explaining why the record-time cap beside it is KEPT, states that that one "holds uncapped wei out of `totalScore`, so a capped pool's excess still redistributes to uncapped pools pro rata per `08_bootstrap.md:125`". So the commitment is not merely inherited from older code — this rung's own insert names the bootstrap line explicitly, and `08_bootstrap.md:125` occurs exactly once in the contract, there. NO LINE NUMBER IS RECORDED FOR EITHER COMMENT DELIBERATELY: this rung's own uncommitted insert sits above the record-time one and moves it, so a number written here would be stale against HEAD on the day it is read, which is (xx)'s dating caveat applied rather than merely repeated. A PP5.4 decision for F-10's favored-then-residual waterfall is therefore CODE WORK and not only a spec edit, and the markers' phrase "its clamp arithmetic is indifferent to both" must be narrowed to the CAP DIRECTION alone, which is what the measurement supports: the cap magnitude arrives from `poolEmissionCapBps` whatever the direction, while the excess routing is already committed to one reading in two comments. WHAT DOES NOT MOVE. Every figure (xxi) states about the two canonical documents stands, every line number it prints is correct, its `15/10/5` measurement of six occurrences across five files reproduces exactly at HEAD, and its disposition is unchanged: the direction is PP5.4's, this rung adjudicates nothing, and no code in it turns on the outcome. The third point above qualifies only what a later choice would COST, not what this rung decided. AND THE MARKERS ARE BOUND BY THIS CLAUSE AS WELL AS BY (xxi): each must name known carriers with NO count and NO completeness claim, must not call G-D3 (iv) the only decision, must not quote `docs/FINDINGS.md` as correct without also recording `:1177`, and must state the clamp indifferent on cap DIRECTION rather than indifferent generally. **(xxiii) AMENDED at PP4.13, and it RULES on what a PoC citation MEANS, fixes when one moves, and files three pre-existing violations this rung did not cause.** The question arose because the E.7c reproductions were inverted and renamed, leaving the F-64 ledger row citing two cases that exist nowhere. THE RULING IS THAT THE LEDGER'S PoC COLUMN IS A LIVE POINTER, and it rests on the file's own column definitions rather than on argument: `docs/white_hat/AUREUM_WHITEHAT_OUTPUT.md:30` defines **PoC** as "Path to the Foundry test that proves/kills it", an artifact locator in the present tense, against `:27` defining **Explanation** as "The attack path / mechanism" and `:29` defining **Fix** as "Commit hash of the fix", both historical by construction. That distinction is why the F-64 row's source citation `(:490-501)` is CORRECT and must not be touched: at the audited commit `9ec513d` those lines are exactly the F-16 clamp, the year-1 gate at 490 and the paired writes closing at 501, so an explanation is snapshot-relative while a PoC path is tree-relative. THE QUEUE AND `CLAUDE.md` GO THE OTHER WAY, and that is measured rather than assumed: `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_QUEUE.md`'s reproduction cell retains the PRE-INVERSION name on every fixed row checked — E.1, E.2, E.3 and E.5 all cite cases that exist nowhere in the tree — while the ledger carries the INVERTED name for those same findings, so the two columns have OPPOSITE conventions and the queue's cells are correct as written. `CLAUDE.md:457` is the same kind of record, a roster of what PP3.2 reproduced. NEITHER IS TO BE "CORRECTED", and the same holds for a test file's own NatSpec: `test/fork/StagePIntegration.t.sol:1326` and `:1525` name the G.3 and G.2 reproductions in comments that point back at them, which is history inside a fixture and not a stale pointer. WHEN A CELL MOVES: at the CLOSURE beat, together with the `Fixed at PP4.NN` stamp, since that is when the commit hash the annotation carries first exists. The usual annotation is `(inverted at <hash>)`, which occurs 19 times across 18 lines, but THE CONVENTION IS NOT UNIFORM AND MUST NOT BE STATED AS IF IT WERE: F-39 records its inversion in prose instead, and F-60 RETAINED one reproduction deliberately, labelling it "retained as reproduction" because it "stays true for any pool nobody has deregistered, which is the motivation for the remedy rather than a defect in it". A retained reproduction is therefore a legitimate outcome, not an oversight. THE CENSUS, taken by checking every `test_P1_` name the ledger cites against every one the tree DECLARES: SEVEN citations name no declared case, across FIVE rows. Two are this rung's and close with it, F-62 at `:109` (E.7a, one name) and F-64 at `:111` (E.7c, two). THE OTHER THREE ARE CLOSED ROWS AND ARE FILED HERE RATHER THAN FIXED BY THIS RUNG: F-29 at `:76`, stamped `Fixed at PP4.12`, cites both original B.2 names where `test/whitehat/P1_B2.t.sol` declares `test_P1_B2_trustedExitClearsTheCheckpointUnderAStaleEma`; and F-44 at `:91` and F-45 at `:92`, both stamped `Fixed at PP4.9`, cite the G.2 and G.3 reproductions, which survive only as the fixture comments named above. So the live-pointer rule was BROKEN THREE TIMES BEFORE THIS RUNG, silently, because nothing cross-checks a ledger citation against the tree — which is the hardening item, not the three repairs. THE INSTRUMENT MATTERS AND IS PP15 AGAIN: a bare-name `git grep` reports the G.2 and G.3 names as PRESENT, because it finds the fixture's comments; only a `function `-anchored sweep answers the question actually being asked, and the two greps disagree on exactly the rows at issue. A zero from the wrong instrument here would have closed the census three rows early. THE PP4.13 CLOSURE SET IS F-62, F-64 AND F-65, and it is NOT the set recorded earlier in this decision. F-62 (E.7a) closes because its fix landed in this rung and its done-criteria case `test_zeroNumeratorSkippedSymmetrically` exists; F-64 (E.7c) because the clamp, both inversions and `test_capEnforcedAtSettle` are green at commit `64bc42c`; F-65 (E.8) because `test_buyBoostRequiresLiveBinding` exists. F-63 DOES NOT CLOSE: E.7b's paging machinery is largely built, `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol` carrying five cases beyond its two reproductions, but its queued done-criteria case `test_advanceTournamentPaginates` does not exist and neither reproduction is inverted. F-66 does not close either: `test/whitehat/P1_E9.t.sol` still declares only its two original reproductions in reproduction wording. A later reader must not read the earlier F-63/F-64/F-65 wording as this rung's closure set. **(xxiv) AMENDED at PP4.13, and it does two things: it SUPERSEDES E.7b's done-criteria case name, which the user adjudicated, because a later clause of this decision deleted the function that name describes; and it CORRECTS clause (x)'s statement of what that case must prove, which states insertion sort's worst case backwards and its best case on the wrong axis, a correction measured against the code rather than adjudicated.** The queue's E.7b row at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_QUEUE.md:84` names its done-criteria case `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol::test_advanceTournamentPaginates`. That case was never written, and it can no longer honestly be: `advanceTournament` has ZERO occurrences in `src/`, removed at `a7a2579` under (x), which records that it is "REMOVED and not retained as a one-call convenience: a wrapper that accumulates everything and then finalizes IS the unbounded path this row is about, and a caller would reach for it." The paged entries that replaced it are `accumulateTournament` and `finalizeTournament` on `GaugeRegistry`. A regression bearing the bound name would pass while naming the defect the fix deleted, which is the reverse of what a done-criteria name is for. THE CASE IS RENAMED `test_accumulateAndFinalizeTournamentPaginates`, a name measured absent from the whole tree before this clause was written, and its PATH is unchanged, since the E.7b PoC already lives at `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol` and PP-D42 has the path follow D35 once the PoC lands. PP-D42 IS NOT WEAKENED. Its rule, that a done-criteria name binds at creation so that a later author cannot quietly retarget what a row must prove, stands for every other row. This is an exception recorded on the SAME decision lock that removed the named API, which is the only place such an exception can be granted without becoming the silent retargeting PP-D42 exists to prevent. The precedent for changing a done-criteria cell by decision is PP-D54, whose item (xi) lists among the bookkeeping it owes that "B.3's done-criteria cell is withdrawn", executed at `b9c6118`. WHAT THE CASE MUST PROVE IS (x)'s PER-PAGE BOUND UNDER THE WORST-CASE ARRIVAL ORDER, AND (x) STATES THAT WORST CASE BACKWARDS. (x) reads that the test must pin "a per-page bound under a DESCENDING-ratio arrival order, which is insertion sort's worst case, rather than only the ascending best case the fixture seeds today." ITS WORST-CASE HALF IS INVERTED. `_insertRanked` at `src/gauge/GaugeEligibility.sol:369` keeps the ranked list DESCENDING by ratio and walks each new entry leftward past every entry with a lower ratio, shifting each one right, so an entry that arrives with a ratio lower than everything already ranked shifts nothing and appends, while one that arrives higher than everything shifts every entry. DESCENDING-ratio arrival is therefore the BEST case and ASCENDING-ratio arrival the WORST. ITS BEST-CASE HALF IS NOT INVERTED BUT IS STATED ON THE WRONG AXIS: its phrase "the ascending best case the fixture seeds today" is true of the fixture's ADDRESS order, which `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol:200-202` describes as ascending and already sorted, and it holds only because in that fixture ascending addresses happen to produce descending ratios, as its NatSpec at `:90` says: the pools "arrive descending, which is insertion sort's best", the sentence completing on the next line. The sort is keyed on ratio, not on address, so address order is the wrong axis on which to state a bound. THE RENAMED CASE THEREFORE STATES ITS ARRIVAL ORDER IN BOTH TERMS — the ascending RATIO order under which it pins the per-page bound, and the address or seeding order that produces that ratio order — so the bound cannot quietly come to depend on a seeding that happens to sort. Pinned under descending-ratio arrival it would measure the cheapest possible page and certify that as the bound. The sort itself is correct; only (x)'s worst-case sentence is inverted, and a regression written to it verbatim would have passed while proving nothing about the bound. The address-framed comment at `:200-202`, true in this fixture only incidentally, is flagged for correction in a later sub-step and is not edited here. THE BOOKKEEPING THIS CLAUSE OWES, on PP-D54 (xi)'s shape: the queue's E.7b done-criteria cell at `docs/STAGE_P_PRIME_QUEUE.md:84` is edited to the new name as its own sub-step. That edit does NOT contradict (xxiii), which rules the queue's REPRODUCTION cells historical: the done-criteria cell is the column PP-D42 governs, a forward-looking binding rather than a record of what reproduced the defect, and PP-D54 edited exactly that column. **(xxv) AMENDED at PP4.13, user-adjudicated, the design in two steps and three of its terms at pre-flight: ACCUMULATION LINKS EACH RANKED POOL INTO A SORTED LIST BEHIND A PREDECESSOR THE CALLER NAMES AND THE CONTRACT VERIFIES, because insertion sort leaves the paged tournament a ROSTER CEILING that no page size removes, and the first alternative put to the user, a caller-sorted finalize, could be bricked in one transaction.** The PP4.13at1 probe paged the fourth tournament of a 300-pool roster whose efficiency ratios arrive ASCENDING, which (xxiv) establishes as insertion sort's worst case, stated in both terms: pools seeded in ascending address order with TVL falling as the index rises, so under constant fees each pool's ratio rises with its address. Each page was measured as its own transaction by cooling the tournament stack before it, and that method is measured rather than assumed: run again under `--isolate`, where every top-level call is a separate transaction, each of the 24 accumulate pages read exactly 28,704 gas higher, so every slope is unchanged. A one-pool accumulate page rose from 554,387 gas at 24 ranked entries to 5,140,235 at 299, about 16,676 per entry; a 24-pool page rose from 4,736,296 at 25 to 21,992,296 at 275, exactly 69,024 per entry; and the middle eight 25-pool finalize pages held flat at 800,119. THE COST IS THE SORT, NOT THE ORACLE READ. (iv) declined bounded-work selection on a figure taken under the BEST-case order inside one warm transaction, about 38,238 gas per pool that it attributed to the oracle-read loop, and (xi) concluded that "no single call need exceed a block". Under the worst case the insertion term dominates and grows with the ranked count rather than with the page, so extrapolating the one-pool line puts a single-pool page past a 30M block at about 1,790 ranked pools, and sooner under any per-transaction gas cap. Past that roster no `maxPools` completes an accumulation, the tournament stops and the caps freeze, which is E.7b's own harm, and (viii)'s gas-regression test could pin no bound that roster growth cannot re-cross, because under insertion there is none. THE FIRST ALTERNATIVE PUT TO THE USER IS WITHDRAWN BEFORE ANY CODE. Accumulation appending each pool in O(1) while finalize takes pools in an order the caller sorts off-chain, each checked to rank strictly after the one before, is unsound as a PAGED design: finalize writes caps, cohorts and crossing events page by page, so a caller who opens finalize with the second-ranked pool has it accepted at rank zero, the first-ranked pool can then never be placed because it ranks before a pool already finalized, `finalizeCursor` never reaches `nRanked`, and (xiv)'s complete-stale revert makes that permanent, while undoing the partial finalize would fire crossings a second time, which G-D5 forbids. RESOLUTION, HINTED INSERTION. The ranked scratch becomes a singly linked list in the unchanged G-D23 (iv) order, ratio descending and the lower address first on a tie. `rankedEntryOf` is a public mapping from pool to `RankedEntry`, whose members become the two SMAs packed as today, `efficiencyRatio`, and `next` sharing a slot with a `uint64` `rankedEpoch` stamp, three slots as today, `pool` being dropped because it is the key; a public `rankedHead` names the first entry; `_rankedScratch` and `_insertRanked` are deleted. `accumulateTournament(address[] calldata hints)` replaces `accumulateTournament(uint256 maxPools)`: the page is as many active gauges from the cursor as there are hints, clamped to those remaining as (xiv) already clamps, with any hint past the clamp unread, and the registry hands the page and the hints to `accumulateEpochSnapshot(address[] calldata page, address[] calldata hints, uint256 epoch)`. For each pool that passes (viii)'s gates, its hint names the entry it follows, `address(0)` meaning the head; the hint must be zero or an entry stamped with the page's `epoch` that ranks strictly before the pool, and the entry after the hint, or `rankedHead` for a zero hint, must be zero or rank strictly after it, else the page reverts `RankHintInvalid(address pool, address hint)`; the pool is then linked in, stamped with that `epoch` and counted in `nRanked`. A hint for a pool the gates skip is never read. Each insert touches a fixed number of slots whatever was ranked before it, so an accumulate page costs a fixed amount per pool at any roster size. A pool whose entry already carries the page's stamp is skipped rather than linked twice: no duplicate is reachable today, since a revocation's swap-and-pop moves the last member into the removed slot, where it is visited once or, below the cursor, skipped for that epoch as (xiv) records, never repeated, and `revokeGauge` is terminal per G-D17, but a pool linked twice would corrupt the list and leave `nRanked` unreachable. THE LIST IS RESET WHERE `nRanked` IS: `rankedHead` and `finalizeNext` are zeroed in the branch of `accumulateEpochSnapshot` that already zeroes `nRanked` and `finalizeCursor` whenever a page carries an epoch other than the seated one, which runs on every tournament's first page, a finished finalize having returned the seated epoch to zero, as well as on (xiv)'s reseat. Every insert therefore starts from a zero head, and by induction a current entry's `next` names only another current entry or zero; an abandoned or finished accumulation's entries need no clearing, since their stamps no longer match and a hint naming one reverts. NO CALL CAN LEAVE THE TOURNAMENT UNFINISHABLE: the list is sorted after every successful page, a wrong hint reverts its page and writes nothing, and no page can pass over a pool its gates rank, so a completed accumulation holds exactly the ranked pools in rank order. A rival page landing first leaves another caller's hints describing a page that is no longer next, which then either still verifies, and is correct progress, or reverts, a retry rather than a stall. FINALIZE KEEPS ITS SIGNATURE AND WALKS THE LIST: `finalizeTournament(uint256 maxPools)` and (xv)'s saturation stand, a new public `finalizeNext` holds the entry the next page starts from, taken from `rankedHead` on the first page, and each step does `_finalizeOne`'s work for that entry at rank `finalizeCursor` from the same settled `nRanked` before following `next`, so bands, cohorts, caps and G-D5's crossing events are exactly as today. THE HINTS COME FROM THE CONTRACTS' OWN VIEWS rather than from logic reimplemented off-chain, and the views MIRROR THE SEAT. `GaugeRegistry.nextAccumulationPage(uint256 maxPools)` returns `(address[] memory page, address[] memory hints)` for exactly the page the next `accumulateTournament` would build: it models `_seatAccumulationEpoch`, so where that call would seat or reseat, the page starts at cursor zero under the current epoch and the list counts as empty, and where it would revert, before month 13, with the epoch not yet elapsed, or on a complete stale accumulation, the view reverts with the same error. `GaugeEligibility.rankHints(address[] calldata page, uint256 epoch)` replays (viii)'s gates and the insertions in memory and returns an `address[] memory` holding each pool's hint, zero for a pool the gates skip, and treats the list as empty whenever `epoch` differs from its seated `accumulationEpoch`, exactly as the reset branch would on that page. Their walks are unbounded, which is acceptable only because no transaction calls them; they serve callers through `eth_call`, and every hint they return is still verified on-chain. The inputs a hint was computed from cannot move within the epoch, since `EfficiencyOracle.efficiencyInputs` sums the three finalized epochs before the current one and a record made in the current epoch lands outside that window. `10_constitution.md:25` requires "no off-chain dependencies for core operation", and the user confirmed, as a question of its own rather than with the design, that this design satisfies it: a hint comes from the contract's own view over public state, any caller can obtain it, and it decides no outcome, since a wrong one reverts and the only list that verifies is the one an on-chain sort would build, while the tournament already sits in PP-D31's keeper set, so an outside caller triggers every page regardless. (iv) IS NARROWED, NOT REVERSED: its ordering stands verbatim and no tier-boundary selection is introduced; what changes is that a sorted position is named by the caller and checked rather than found by shifting. (x)'s two cursors survive, its sorted scratch survives as a list, and (xv)'s saturation falls away on accumulate, where the hints array's length is the page. THE BOOKKEEPING THIS CLAUSE OWES, with the ripple measured at `3ca4a1a`: `accumulateTournament(` or `accumulateEpochSnapshot(` appears on four lines of the two source files, counting the registry's contract NatSpec, and on 56 lines of five test files, 36 in `test/unit/GaugeEligibility.t.sol`, 12 in `test/whitehat/P1_E7b.t.sol`, 6 in the fork fixture `test/fork/StageGIntegration.t.sol`, and one each in `test/whitehat/F16_emissionCapTournament.t.sol` and `test/whitehat/P1_E7a.t.sol`; none appears in `script/`, on any interface, or in the frontend repository. Every such test site takes its hints from the views, and a site behind an armed `vm.expectEmit`, which (xiii) found at four unit sites and one fork site, must take them before arming. Finalize's call sites keep their form. The done-criteria case keeps (xxiv)'s name and pins what this makes true: under the ascending-ratio order in both terms, with each page measured as its own transaction, an accumulate page's gas does not grow with the ranked count. The PP4.13at1 probe is replaced by that case rather than committed. **(xxvi) AMENDED at PP4.13at12, E.7b's two reproductions RETARGETED rather than retained, (xxiv)'s worst-case obligation RE-READ against the design (xxv) actually built, and the done-criteria measurement SPLIT so it bounds the transaction rather than a view.** Both reproductions still PASS against the built fix, for reasons that are no longer harmful. `test_P1_E7b_tournamentGasScalesWithTheUnboundedActiveSet` measures an UNBOUNDED page on each leg, the shape (xv) deliberately preserves, so its 890-pool extrapolation past a 30M block stays true of a shape a caller may still choose and false of the shape the protocol now requires; `test_P1_E7b_capsFreezeAtWhateverTheLastAffordableSnapshotWrote` asserts only that `finalizeEpochSnapshot` is the sole writer of `poolEmissionCapBps`, which held before the fix, holds after it, and was never itself the defect — AFFORDABLE is the whole of its defect framing, and no snapshot is unaffordable once it pages. A green file headed as a reproduction of a CLOSED root cause asserts the defect is live, which is PB22 in the ledger's own costume, so both cases take post-fix names and post-fix NatSpec: `test_unboundedPageIsACallerChoiceNotTheOnlyShape` keeps the thirty-against-ninety measurement and the 890-pool extrapolation while carrying the paged path beside it as the remedy, and `test_capsHoldOnlyUntilTheNextPagedSnapshot` keeps every sole-writer assertion unchanged. F-22's green PoC is NOT the precedent and must not be cited as one: PB-D63 (xi) scoped that residual OUT of its fix and PB-D64 (viii) changed its role to attestation-of-accepted-residual, whereas E.7b's fix is precisely that the unbounded call need never be made. **(xxiv) REASONED FROM A FUNCTION THIS DECISION HAD ALREADY DELETED, and a later reader must not carry its worst-case sentence forward.** (xxiv) grounds its correction on `_insertRanked` walking each new entry leftward and shifting, concluding that ASCENDING-ratio arrival is insertion sort's worst case. `_insertRanked` has ZERO occurrences in `src/` at HEAD; (xxv) replaced it. The on-chain linker is now `_linkRanked` at `src/gauge/GaugeEligibility.sol:393`, and it carries NO LOOP AT ALL — it reads `rankedEntryOf[hint]`, evaluates two `_ranksBefore` comparisons to verify the hint and its successor, writes the entry, relinks one pointer and increments `nRanked`. The transaction is therefore O(1) per entry AT EVERY ARRIVAL ORDER, which is a STRUCTURAL result and not a measured one, and it is the real ground on which the roster ceiling recorded above at about 1,790 ranked pools is REMOVED rather than raised. The unbounded walk survives in exactly one place, `_hintFor` at `:545`, an `internal view` reached only through `rankHints`, whose own NatSpec at `:563` records that the walk "is acceptable only because no transaction calls this: it serves callers through `eth_call`, and every hint it returns is still verified on-chain when the page lands." **THE at1 PROBE'S FIGURES ARE WITHDRAWN AS A BOUND, and the withdrawal is the reason this clause was rewritten before it was committed.** That probe bracketed `_accumulatePage`, which calls `nextAccumulationPage` ON-CHAIN, so its byte-identical 142,596 across eleven one-entry pages measured a transaction PLUS a view no production caller pays — and measured it under ascending-ratio arrival, which for `_hintFor` is the CHEAP side rather than the worst, since that loop breaks on the first entry whenever the arriving pool outranks the head. Recording those figures as E.7b's closing bound would have pinned the done-criteria case on the favourable order, which is the precise defect (xxiv) exists to prevent, arriving one clause later in a new costume. **THE MEASUREMENT IS SPLIT, user-adjudicated at PP4.13at12.** The done-criteria case bounds `accumulateTournament(hints)` ALONE, with the hints fetched OUTSIDE the gas window, so the figure is the transaction's; the one-entry EQUALITY binds there rather than across a combined window, and it is honest because the on-chain path carries no order-dependent term to hide. `rankHints`' walk is measured and recorded SEPARATELY as an accepted off-chain cost under DESCENDING-ratio arrival, its true worst case. (xxiv)'s worst-case obligation is DISCHARGED rather than withdrawn: with the only order-dependent term off-chain, no arrival order can inflate the transaction's bound, and the case still states its arrival in BOTH the ratio and the address terms (xxiv) requires, so a later reader can see which axis each figure belongs to. The figures land with the done-criteria case rather than here, because this clause must not again record a number before the window that produces it exists.

## PP-D57 — rung 14 (PP4.14): two rows, both divisor sites, C.7's two slots take different shapes

**PP-D57 (user-adjudicated 2026-09-18): rung 14's scope and the build shape of both rows in it, settled before any code.** (i) **SCOPE IS TWO ROWS ACROSS THREE CONTRACTS AND THE SPINE.** D.8 and C.7 ship here. D.8 edits `src/ccb/CCBMultiplier.sol` and `src/emission/EmissionDistributor.sol`; C.7 edits `CCBMultiplier`, `src/fee_router/AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol` and `script/`. The queue's `ccb` cell therefore UNDERSTATES both rows, which together touch `ccb`, `emission-stack` and `hook`; that is RECORDED here rather than relabelled, on PP4.8's and PP-D55 (i)'s precedent, and it costs nothing on chain because PP-D12 makes those units ONE TRANSITIVE UNIT in which every rung completes before any deployment is scheduled. (ii) **THE SECOND DIVISOR SITE IS FIXED, NOT STRUCK, AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS CLAUSE HAD IT BACKWARDS.** That draft argued the literal at `EmissionDistributor.sol:599` is inert because emission shares normalize by `totalScore`, so the divisor divides out. IT DIVIDES OUT ONLY AT THE ENDPOINTS. With `effective_i = (1-a)(f5Total/D) + a*score_i` for a Miliarium pool and `a*score_j` for the rest, the normalized share is `[(1-a)/D + a*s_i/S] / [M(1-a)/D + a]`, in which `D` appears in both terms and is not a common factor. It cancels at `a = 0`, giving `1/M`, and trivially at `a = 1e18`; between them it does not. `_alphaF3` at `:557-563` returns an interior value across the whole months-11-and-12 window, 437,999 blocks, so `D` sets the weight of the equal leg against the individual score for that entire window. With `D = 28` and 26 pools live the equal leg is under-weighted by 26/28, and the blend leans about 7% further toward CCB than the rule in (v) intends. A CLAUSE THAT CONTRADICTS ITSELF IS THE TELL: the same draft also said replacing the literal would make H-D33's conservation hold for every M, which is a behaviour change, while claiming the change had none. (iii) **D.8 SITE ONE, `CCBMultiplier.sol:260`, where nothing normalizes the divisor away at any alpha.** `:255` reads `poolCount = miliariumRegistry.miliariumPoolsCount()`, the loop at `:256-258` walks exactly that many entries, and `:260` divides by the constant `MILIARIUM_POOL_COUNT = 28`. The quotient is compared against `DEAD_ZONE` bounds, so a divisor larger than the count walked understates the constellation mean directly and permanently. The fix divides by `poolCount`, already in scope at `:255`. NO ZERO GUARD IS BUILT, and that is a measured result rather than an omission: `:232` reverts `NotMiliariumPool(pool)` unless `miliariumRegistry.isMiliarium(pool)`, and `MiliariumRegistry` writes `_isMiliarium` and pushes `_enumerated` together at `:159` and `:161` and clears both in lockstep at `:165-175`, with `miliariumPoolsCount()` returning `_enumerated.length` at `:97-99`, so membership implies a count of at least one and a zero divisor cannot reach `:260`. A guard there would be untestable by construction. `MILIARIUM_POOL_COUNT` IS DELETED, which the queue's fix intent asks for: it carries four references, its declaration at `:61-62`, a NatSpec at `:224`, the single live use at `:260`, and the D.8 PoC, which reads it through the public getter — so deletion is a public-surface change and the PoC inverts with it in the same rung. THE CONTRACT IS ALREADY INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT, which is a sharper argument than any canon reading: `:240` computes the GLOBAL aggregate over `gauges.gaugeCount()`, a live count read the same way, so `delta_global` already tracks the live roster while `delta_intra` alone does not. (iv) **D.8 SITE TWO, `EmissionDistributor.sol:599`, divides by the live GAUGED-MILIARIUM count.** The roster count will not serve: a Miliarium pool that is not gauged never reaches `recordScore`, whose `:590` step-1 gate reverts `NotApproved`, so it contributes no effective score, and dividing by a roster that counts it breaks conservation exactly as the literal does. The divisor is therefore a counter of gauged Miliarium pools, maintained O(1) as pools gauge and revoke rather than enumerated, because an enumeration here is the sweep H-D5 forbids. With `D = M` the conservation invariant H-D33 states holds for EVERY M rather than only for M equal to 28, which is how that clause reads today, so H-D33 is reopened by this rung and restated. (v) **EMISSIONS SPLIT EVENLY ACROSS THE LIVE MILIARIUM POOLS, WHICH MAKES THE CODE'S INTENT RIGHT AND TWO DOCUMENTS WRONG.** The user ruled it directly: one Miliarium pool takes all the emissions, 12 take a twelfth each, 26 take a twenty-sixth each, 28 take a twenty-eighth each. H-D6's unminted clause is therefore WITHDRAWN — it reads that for any live count below 28 each block leaves `(28 - N)/28` of the LP tranche "structurally unminted, never parked in a treasury slot, never deferred for catch-up minting", and under the ruling nothing is withheld. THAT WITHDRAWAL LANDS IN `docs/STAGE_H_NOTES.md` AS A DATED `H-D6 v2` ENTRY IN THIS RUNG, on the precedent of `H-D9 v2` at `STAGE_H_NOTES.md:61`, which withdrew a lemma from a later stage by writing into the Stage-H file rather than leaving a carry; H-D6 is load-bearing for H-D7, H-D18, H-D28, H-D33 and the phase table, all of which read it unamended until that entry exists. The canon pass is a CARRY and gates nothing here. Its surface is measured WITH ITS PATTERN, without which no count is reproducible: `grep -rn` over the clone with `--include` for md, txt, html and json, excluding `node_modules` and `.git`, for `1/28` or `1 / 28` or `twenty-eighth`, returns 43 lines across 21 files, being 22 lines in 11 source docs and 21 lines in 10 files of the generated `dist/aumm-skill/references/` mirror, the one-file difference being `aureum_schedule.md`, which is not mirrored; `miliarium_profiles/` measures 0 of 28. THE EXCLUSION STATEMENTS SURVIVE UNCHANGED and must not be swept into the amendment: `02_mental_model.md:97` and `03_theoretical_foundation.md:39` say other pools receive none of this tranche, which stays true, since the ruling changes the DENOMINATOR among Miliarium pools and not who is eligible. §xxix does NOT bar the amendment: the 1/28 split, F-1, F-3 and the regime split are all absent from it, and the nearest immutables are the 28-pool MEMBERSHIP, the CCB formula and F-3's endpoint block. (vi) **C.7's REGISTRY SLOT IS SEALED ON BOTH DEPLOYMENT PATHS, and PP-D27's prescription is amended.** PP-D27 says both slots become constructor immutables where the value is known at construction. For this slot it is not: `CCBMultiplier` is constructed at `DeployStageF.s.sol:148` and `MiliariumRegistry` only at `DeployStageJ.s.sol:60`, so an immutable needs a Stage-F prediction of a Stage-J address, adding an edge to the cycle PP4.0 calls irreducible. The shape is the seal the spine already uses for this contract's SIBLING one-shot, and that precedent is TWO PATHS rather than one call: `DeployStageP.s.sol:146` forwards through `DeployStageF.sealGaugeRegistry` at `:105-107` under the fork `deploy()`, while `:228` calls `ccbMultiplier.setGaugeRegistry` DIRECTLY under the governor broadcast, because `:194-196` records that the CCBMultiplier's CREATE sender is the governor on that path. `CCBMultiplier.sol:146` sets `registrySetter = msg.sender` at construction, so the identical split governs `setMiliariumRegistry` and BOTH paths must seat it. A forwarder alone would leave the production spine unseated with every gate green, which is PP15 exactly. `setMiliariumRegistry` self-seals at `:167`, so one call per path zeroes `registrySetter` with no new burn primitive, and the `DeployStageP` post-condition asserts the seal on whichever path ran. (vii) **C.7's HOOK SLOT IS BURNED RATHER THAN BOUND, AND GAINS THE GETTER THE POST-CONDITION NEEDS.** `setIncendiaryModule` has zero call sites in `script/` and `test/fork/`, so `_incendiaryAdmin` never seals and stays live on the deployer, which is the finding. Binding it would be wrong: the entry it gates, `routeIncendiaryDeposit` at `AureumFeeRoutingHook.sol:762`, has zero callers anywhere in `src/`, because `IncendiaryRegistry` reaches der Bodensee through its own immutable `BODENSEE_CHANNEL` at `:65`, donating at buy time per `:293-294`. The remedy is a new `renounceIncendiaryAdmin()` called once in the spine, AND the queue's fourth remedy, a getter for burn state, which is not optional here: `:137` declares `address private _incendiaryAdmin` with no getter, so without an `incendiaryAdmin()` view the post-condition has nothing to read and the burn is asserted only behaviourally. THE CONSEQUENCE IS RECORDED so a later reader does not read the inert route as a live one: with the admin burned, `incendiaryModule` can never be set and `routeIncendiaryDeposit` reverts `ModuleNotSet` forever. The surface is NOT deleted, on PP-D2's standing disposition that pre-audit dead code is hygiene rather than remediation; that deletion joins the existing hygiene carries. (viii) **C.7's DONE-CRITERIA NAME IS SUPERSEDED, on PP-D56 (xxiv)'s precedent one decision over.** The queue names `test/whitehat/P1_C7.t.sol::test_registrySetterIsImmutable`, and under (vi) the setter is not immutable — it is sealed by the spine, a different property and a different assertion, and a regression bearing the immutable name would pass while naming a shape this rung deliberately did not build. THE CASE IS RENAMED `test_registrySetterIsSealedByTheSpine`, measured absent from the whole tree before this clause was written, its PATH unchanged. PP-D42 is NOT weakened: its rule that a done-criteria name binds at creation stands for every other row, and this exception is granted on the SAME decision that chose the shape, which is the only place it can be granted without becoming the silent retargeting PP-D42 exists to prevent. **(ix) AMENDED at PP4.14g, user-adjudicated 2026-09-18: D.8's done-criteria case must tell the divisor (iii) requires apart from every other count in scope, not only from the deleted literal, and must check it at every Miliarium roster size from one to twenty-eight.** The user restated the rule it rests on directly: the divisor is however many Miliarium pools are deployed — ten if ten, seventeen if seventeen — and 28 is not assumed to be the launch roster; the user then ruled that the case checks every size from one to twenty-eight rather than a sample, which tests that rule at every count the constellation can hold. The first draft of `test_meanDivisorEqualsLengthWalked` set the Miliarium and gauge rosters from one array, and gatekeeper pre-flight caught that `gaugePoolCount`, read at `CCBMultiplier.sol:241` as PP4.14f leaves it, in the same function as the divisor at `:261`, then equalled the Miliarium count at every size, so a divisor reading that sibling count would have passed all three of that draft's test functions. The rosters are therefore DECOUPLED: the Miliarium roster varies across every size from one to twenty-eight while the gauge roster stays fixed at twenty-nine, the twenty-eight provisioned Miliarium pools plus ONE non-Miliarium gauge that exists only so the gauge count differs from every Miliarium count walked. That one extra gauge carries no protocol meaning, and any number of extra gauges above zero would serve. A literal divisor then fails at every size but its own, and a gauge-count divisor fails at every size. The global channel stays out of the measurement on the F-D18 cold-start sentinel, each size taking a fresh multiplier. This clause governs site one only: site two divides by (iv)'s gauged-Miliarium counter, which equals the deployed count exactly when every deployed Miliarium pool is gauged, and its regression is not settled here. **(x) AMENDED at PP4.14l, user-adjudicated 2026-09-18: (iii)'s divisor fix WITHDRAWS three records that call the 28 divisor intended, each in its own file and written BEFORE the fork tests that cite them change.** F-D26 in `docs/STAGE_F_NOTES.md` accepted the fork harness's permanent intra step-down under equal TVL as "mechanical-divisor arithmetic, not an implementation defect" and pinned that direction in its assertion scope; it was the defect D.8 files, seen at three pools rather than twenty-six. F-D27 in the same file made padding unit-test registries to twenty-eight entries a rule so tests could route around it, and named four sub-28 unit tests as passing by "a coincidence rather than a correctness property", the coincidence being the defect. OQ-23 (iv) in `docs/FINDINGS.md` defines the Miliarium average as `protocolTVLEMA(t) / 28`. F-D26 and F-D27 each gain a dated v2 entry and OQ-23 a dated status line, recording that the simple mean survives and its divisor is the count walked; F-D27's padded tests stay correct and are not unpadded. The ripple those records shaped is handled in three shapes. Eight unit assertions across seven tests drop the phantom intra step they carried. Two more unit tests and both P1_D4 regressions seed their updated pools above the Miliarium mean, so the step each asserts is genuine. And the three fork cases a sizing pass predicts will flip, each asserting the step under equal TVL, are to assert neutral instead, which the fork gate confirms or refutes: `test_Fork_CCBEngine_Composition_DeltaIntraNegStepUnderNearEqualTVL` is RENAMED `test_Fork_CCBEngine_Composition_DeltaIntraNeutralUnderEqualTVL`, and StageJ's replace-slot case will prove what the literal hid, that the dropped pool's TVL is absent from the intra mean, since neutral holds only if it is. A fourth fork case, StageP's leg-D month walk, is predicted to keep its step, because the readiness gate zeroes every pool but the one it matures; only its `/28` NatSpec goes stale and is corrected. **(xi) AMENDED at PP4.14r, user-adjudicated 2026-09-18: site two's counter is DISTRIBUTOR-LOCAL, reconciled where the distributor already observes gauge state, because no callback from `GaugeRegistry` or `MiliariumRegistry` reaches `EmissionDistributor` today.** The distributor gains `uint256 internal _scoredMiliariumCount` and `mapping(address => bool) internal _countedAsMiliarium`. `recordScore` reads `isMiliarium(pool)` once after its gauge gate; where the answer differs from the pool's flag, the flag takes the answer and the counter moves one step the same way; the Miliarium branch then divides `f5Total` by `_scoredMiliariumCount`, which that branch has just made at least one, so no zero guard is built, on (iii)'s reasoning. `deregisterScore`, after its `StillApproved` gate, clears a set flag and decrements. The count is therefore the number of Miliarium pools whose score sits in `totalScore`, which is exactly the M in (ii)'s share formula, with no new cross-contract call, no setter and no redeploy edge beyond the emission stack. THIS SUPERSEDES (iv)'s "maintained O(1) as pools gauge and revoke": the count moves when the distributor records or deregisters a pool, so a Miliarium pool that is gauged but never recorded is outside it, and (ix)'s closing equivalence holds only as "equals the deployed count exactly when every deployed Miliarium pool is gauged and recorded". A pool re-slotted out of Miliarium, or revoked, stays counted until the next `recordScore` or `deregisterScore` on it, the same staleness every stored score already carries under H-D31 Q2. Two alternatives were REJECTED: `GaugeRegistry` pushing gauge and revoke transitions needs a new distributor binding, widens the redeploy to the gauge unit and still misses re-slotting; and `MiliariumRegistry.miliariumPoolsCount()` reverses (iv). No public getter is added; tests read the counter through an `EmissionDistributorHarness` exposer, on PP-D55 (xv)'s precedent. The regression lands in `test/whitehat/P1_D8.t.sol` as a second contract, and what it must tell apart and the name of its case are locked by a later clause before it is drafted, on (ix)'s lesson. The four NatSpec sites in `EmissionDistributor.sol` stating the 1/28 literal, at `:84`, `:308`, `:350` and `:586`, are corrected with the code, and H-D33's restatement lands in `docs/STAGE_H_NOTES.md` as a dated v2 entry in its own docs commit immediately after the code commit and never before it, since its conservation statement stays true of the code until then. **(xii) AMENDED at PP4.14v, 2026-09-18, user-adjudicated as to D.8's done-criteria and drafted under (xi)'s ruling as to the rest, the later clause (xi) promised: site two's regression must tell the scored-Miliarium count apart from every other count the equal leg could divide by, at every count from one to twenty-eight, on (ix)'s lesson.** `test_equalLegDividesByTheScoredMiliariumCount` takes a fresh `EmissionDistributorHarness` at each M from one to twenty-eight and seats M gauged Miliarium pools, one Miliarium pool that is registered but never gauged and so can never be recorded, and one gauged non-Miliarium pool, every gauged pool carrying a non-zero TVL EMA and a non-zero multiplier at a block past EMA maturity and inside the bootstrap window, so `f5Total` is non-zero and no divisor passes vacuously. It records every gauged pool, re-records the first, and asserts, with alpha at zero so the equal leg is the whole score, that `extScoredMiliariumCount()` equals M and that the first pool's `poolScore` equals `f5Total() / M` exactly. The literal 28 then fails at every M but twenty-eight, and the registry count and the gauged count, both M plus one, fail at every M. A second case, `test_scoredMiliariumCountFollowsDeregistrationAndReslotting`, drives the two paths that lower the count: revoking a counted pool and calling `deregisterScore` takes it down by one, and re-slotting a counted pool out of Miliarium takes it down by one at that pool's next `recordScore`, which then takes the non-Miliarium branch; after each, re-recording a remaining Miliarium pool shows the divisor following the count. The fixture imports the unit suite's mocks from `test/unit/EmissionDistributor.t.sol` under aliases, since three of their names collide with the `test/unit/CCBMultiplier.t.sol` mocks the file already imports, and it reads no per-pool flag, so `extScoredMiliariumCount` stays the harness's only PP-D57 exposer. BY USER RULING, D.8's done-criteria cell gains `test_equalLegDividesByTheScoredMiliariumCount` beside `test_meanDivisorEqualsLengthWalked` at the rung's bookkeeping, so D.8 closes only when both divisor sites are proven; the grant is made on the decision that chose site two's shape, as (viii) made C.7's rename, and it adds a name rather than retargeting one, so PP-D42's bind on the existing name stands. **(xiii) AMENDED at PP4.14aa, 2026-09-18, user-adjudicated as to the registry slot's shape, the done-criteria path and the second done-criteria case, and drafted under (vi) and (vii) as to the rest: C.7's build, locked before any C.7 code.** (vi)'s REASON for sealing rather than making the registry slot a constructor immutable is WITHDRAWN and its CHOICE kept. It said an immutable needs a Stage-F prediction of a Stage-J address, but both deploy paths build the Stage-J `MiliariumRegistry` before Stage F constructs `CCBMultiplier`, at `DeployStageP.s.sol:124` before `:132` on the fork `deploy()` and at `:201-203` before `:214` on the production `run()`, so the constructor already receives the real registry. The user ruled to keep the seal, and its grounds from here are that it is script-only, adds no contract surface, and mirrors the sibling gauge-registry seal. Because the value is already right, the seal re-sets the same registry and its whole effect is burning `registrySetter`. The fork path seals through a new `DeployStageF.sealMiliariumRegistry(IMiliariumRegistry)` forwarder, gated on `sealAuthority` exactly as `sealGaugeRegistry` is and called by `DeployStageP` beside `f.sealGaugeRegistry`; the production path calls `ccbMultiplier.setMiliariumRegistry` directly inside the governor broadcast that already calls `setGaugeRegistry`. `DeployStageP` gains post-condition (5), reverting `CCBMiliariumRegistryNotSealed(address registrySetter)` unless `registrySetter()` reads zero, because post-condition (4)'s value check would pass unsealed on this slot. The hook gains `renounceIncendiaryAdmin()`, callable only by `_incendiaryAdmin` and reverting `NotIncendiaryAdmin()` otherwise, which zeroes the slot and emits a new `IncendiaryAdminRenounced(address indexed formerAdmin)`, and the getter `incendiaryAdmin()`; all three are declared on the concrete hook and not on `IAureumFeeRoutingHook`, which carries neither `setIncendiaryModule` nor the admin-slot events `GovernanceModuleSet`, `IncendiaryModuleSet` and `EmissionRecorderSet`. The spine calls the renounce once in `DeployStageI._wire`, right after `setEmissionRecorder`, which both deploy paths reach as the governor holding the slot, and `DeployStageP` gains post-condition (6), reverting `HookIncendiaryAdminNotBurned(address incendiaryAdmin)` unless `incendiaryAdmin()` reads zero. The C.7 PoC inverts in place, its two cases becoming unit regressions of the two primitives, the same-registry seal and the renounce. BY USER RULING, (viii)'s case `test_registrySetterIsSealedByTheSpine` moves from its unit path to `test/fork/StagePRunRehearsal.t.sol`, since only a fork fixture runs the spine and that file drives the production `run()` and its direct `setMiliariumRegistry` call, the leg (vi) flags as PP15's risk, while the fork `deploy()` forwarder stays enforced by post-condition (5); this corrects (viii)'s "its PATH unchanged", and its name binds as (viii) granted. BY USER RULING, C.7's done-criteria cell also gains `test_incendiaryAdminIsBurnedByTheSpine` in the same file at the rung's bookkeeping, so C.7 closes only when both of its findings are proven, as (xii) did for D.8's second site.
